[^43]: **inviting the King along with the Queen…** Inviting King *Tif’eret* and Queen *Shekhinah*, who is symbolized by the ark. See Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 232–33; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-36a|1:36a]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-242a|3:242a]].

    On David bringing the ark to the City of David, see [[II Samuel 06#^6-1|2 Samuel 6]]; [[I Chronicles 15#^15-1|1 Chronicles 15]].

[^44]: **whoever invites a king should alter his behavior** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-242a|3:242a]].

[^45]: **redheaded regal jesters** רופינוס‎ בדיחי (*Bediḥei rufinus*). Rufinus is a Roman name derived from the Latin *rufus*, “red-haired.” The name is shared by various early Christian saints, a fourth-century Roman minister, and Tineus Rufus, the second-century governor of Palestine ordered by Emperor Hadrian to crush the Jewish rebellion.

    On Tineus Rufus, see JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b; *Ta’anit* 4:5, 69b; BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 65#65b|65b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 11:5; *Tanḥuma, Ki Tissa* 33, *Terumah* 3.

    In the *Zohar, rufinus* refers to a royal official. See 2:36b, 40a, 68a, 85a, 175a. For the medieval Castilian usage, see Corominas, *Diccionario*, s.v. *rufián*. Cf. Aramaic, רופילא (*rufila*), “high official.”

[^46]: **commanders** פרדשכי (*Pardashkei*). See BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 94#94a|94a]]; *Megillah* [[Megillah 12#12b|12b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 94:9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-8b|2:8b]], [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36b|36b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-144b|3:144b]] (IR); *Tosefot he-Arukh ha-Shalem*, 336, s.v. *pardakhsh*.

[^47]: **if not, then he himself, for the king’s amusement** As David himself danced before God. See [[II Samuel 06#^6-14|2 Samuel 6:14–23]].

    In thirteenth-century Castile, King Alfonso X (1252–1284) sang to the Virgin Mary, imagining himself as her troubadour. In his *Cantigas de Santa María* (1:102, 19–26) he chants: “I wish today to be her Troubadour, and I beg that she will desire me for her Troubadour and will want to receive my song.”

[^48]: **The verse should read: *May Your Levites sing for joy*…** The Levites, not the priests, were the ones who sang in the Temple, delighting God. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-103b|1:103b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|142b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|230b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-48b|3:48b]], [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-121b|121b]] (*RM*).

[^49]: **and *faithful*** Aramaic, וחסידי (*va-ḥasidei*), corresponding to the biblical Hebrew וחסידיך (*va-ḥasidekha*), *and Your faithful*. In Kabbalah the priests symbolize the *sefirah* of *Ḥesed*, while the Levites symbolize *Gevurah*.

[^50]: **when invited out he should fulfill the will of his host…** See BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 86#86b|86b]], in the name of Rav Huna: “Whatever the host tells you, do—unless he says, ‘Get out!’”

[^51]: **I will not even use My own vessels, but yours instead** God accepts David’s arrangement of having the priests sing instead of His usual singers, the Levites. See *Tanḥuma, Beha’alotekha* 6.

[^52]: **If I walked this way just to hear this, I am satisfied!** Similar exclamations appear in rabbinic literature and often in the *Zohar*. See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 1:3; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 3:11; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 6:2; *Qohelet Zuta* 5:17; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 16#16a|16a]], 24b; *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 41#41a|41a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-2a|1:2a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164b|164b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-240a|240a]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-99a|2:99a]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-121b|121b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-122a|122a]], [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-193b|193b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-26a|3:26a]]; *KP*, 1:20d.

[^53]: ***Therefore a man leaves his father and mother and cleaves to his wife*** The verse continues: *and they become one flesh*. Correspondingly, Jacob left his parents and went to Haran in search of a wife.

[^54]: ***Jacob left Beer-sheba*… alluding to when Israel left the Temple…** Beer-sheba symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who dwelt in the Temple, while Haran stands for the Diaspora. See above, note 9.

[^55]: ***He encountered a certain place*…** The verse concludes: *He took of the stones of the place and put them at his head and lay down in that place*.

[^56]: **A king who comes to visit his consort must למפגע (*le-mifga*), entreat, her** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49a|1:49a]]–b; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-225b|3:225b]]. Cf. *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ* 2; *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:9; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 26#26b|26b]]; *Zohar* [[#^z1-165a|1:165a]].

[^57]: **piece of abandoned property** הפקירא (*Hephqeira*), “Ownerless property.” On the connection between the root פקר (*pqr*) and sexuality, see M *Yevamot* 13:1; BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 61#61b|61b]], 66a; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^25-5|Deuteronomy 25:5]].

[^58]: **elaborately embroidered bolsters in a spacious palace** באפלייטא‎ מרקמאן‎ כסתותי (*Kastutei meraqqeman be-applaita*), “Cushions embroidered in *applaita*.” The last word apparently derives from the Greek *plateia*, “an open place,” though the author may have in mind פלטין (*palatin*), “palace,” derived from the Greek *palation*. See Galante. The original reading, however, could have been אפליקטא (*appliqta*), “embroidery,” derived from the Greek *poikilton*, yielding a phrase: “bolsters embroidered with embroidery,” with no mention of the palace. My rendering here interweaves both readings.

    See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 11:8; ibid., ed. Buber, 84b, n. 88; *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:1; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 4:11, 4:13; *Arukh ha-Shalem*, s.v. פלקט (*plqt*); *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 173; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1359. Cf. the rare word at the beginning of this story (note 36): אפיקותא (*afiquta*).

[^59]: **castle** קסטירא (*Qastira*), derived from the Latin *castrum* (pl. *castra*), “fortress, castle.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|30a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85a|85a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128a|128a]].

[^60]: **he should leave his and spend the night there…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-242a|3:242a]].

[^61]: **as soon as he came to Her** As soon as Jacob, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, came to *Shekhinah, the place*.

[^62]: **With such pearls in your hands…** For similar expressions, see BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 3#3a|3a]]; *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 123#123a|123a]].

[^63]: **wedding celebration of this son of mine** A little earlier, this son had cited an appropriate verse from Genesis: *Therefore a man leaves his father and mother and cleaves to his wife*.

[^64]: **All pertains to the blessed Holy One** The biblical account of Jacob’s journey alludes to the inner dynamics of the *sefirot*.

[^65]: **forty years he fasted over Jerusalem…** See BT *Gittin* [[Gittin 56#56a|56a]]: “Rabbi Tsadok sat fasting for forty years so that Jerusalem would not be destroyed. \[He grew so thin that\] when he ate anything, it could be seen from outside \[as it passed through his throat\]. When he regained his strength, they used to bring him dried figs; he sucked the juice and threw them away.” See *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:31.

[^66]: **stayed with his wife** Fulfilling the verse he had cited: *Therefore a man leaves his father and mother and cleaves to his wife* \[*and they become one flesh*\].

[^67]: **it is our custom that everyone… offers them gifts…** See *Kallah Rabbati* 1:9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-96b|3:96b]].

[^68]: **one concerning prophecy and two concerning dreams** It is unclear precisely how the following mysteries divide up according to this scheme. Several witnesses read here (continuing into the following paragraph): “one concerning prophecy, one concerning dreams, and he said, ‘What is the difference between prophecy and dream?’”

[^69]: **He said** Rabbi Tsadok’s son begins a long homily.

[^70]: **Prophecy derives from World of the Male** From the constellation of *sefirot* ranging from *Binah* through *Yesod*, specifically from *Netsaḥ* and *Hod*.

    On the phrase “World of the Male,” see above, note 16.

[^71]: **from World of the Female** From *Shekhinah* and the powers beneath Her.

[^72]: **from one to the other, a descent of six rungs** The six rungs apparently are: *Netsaḥ, Hod, Yesod, Shekhinah*, and the first two archangels, Michael and Gabriel. As Rabbi Tsadok soon explains, Gabriel transmits dreams.

    The connection between the number six and dreams derives from BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 57#57b|57b]]: “A dream is one-sixtieth of prophecy.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-149a|1:149a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|183a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-191b|191b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-196a|196a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-238a|238a]].

[^73]: **Prophecy, on the right and the left** Deriving from *Netsaḥ* and *Hod*, right and left legs of the sefirotic body.

[^74]: **dream, on the left** Deriving from Gabriel, angel of the left side, associated with *Gevurah*.

[^75]: **countless rungs below** Lower angelic powers who convey dreams.

[^76]: **corresponding to one’s rung, he sees…** Each person sees dreams corresponding to his level of virtue. See JT *Ma’aser Sheni* 4:6, 55b: “Rabbi Yose said, ‘… A person dreams corresponding to who he is.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|1:183a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-194a|194a]].

[^77]: **Prophecy spreads only in its domain** Only in the land of Israel. This statement by the son of Rabbi Tsadok corresponds with the tradition attributed to Rabbi El’azar son of Tsadok in *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1: “*Shekhinah* is not revealed outside the land \[of Israel\], for it is said: *Jonah set out to flee to Tarshish from the presence of YHVH* ([[Jonah 1#^1-3|Jonah 1:3]]). Was he fleeing *from the presence of YHVH*? Has it not already been said: *Where can I go from Your spirit?* \[*Where can I flee from Your presence?*\] *If I ascend to heaven, You are there*. \[*If I make my bed in Sheol, here You are*\]…([[Psalms 139#^139-7|Psalms 139:7–8]]). Rather, Jonah said, ‘I will go outside the land, where *Shekhinah* is not revealed.’”

    See ibid.: “Until the land of Israel was chosen, all lands were suitable for divine speech; once the land of Israel was chosen, all other lands were eliminated.”

[^78]: ***He dreamed: Here, a ladder set up on earth, its head reaching to heaven*** The verse continues: —*and here, angels of God ascending and descending on it*.

[^79]: **היה‎ היה (*Hayoh hayah*), *Happening it happened*** This phrase combines the infinitive and finite forms of the verb “to be.”

[^80]: **prophecy happened at that particular moment…** The unusual phrase, *happening it happened*, implies a unique event. When *Shekhinah* appeared to Ezekiel in Babylon, it was the first time She had ever appeared outside the land of Israel, but this was an emergency the stunned people in exile had to be comforted and reassured.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85a|1:85a]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-2a|2:2a–b]], [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-5a|5a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-82a|82a]]; Moses de León, *Peirush ha-Merkavah*, 58–59. Cf. *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1; BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 25a.

[^81]: ***Shekhinah* descended among Israel in exile** See BT *Megillah* [[Megillah 29#29a|29a]]: “Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai says, ‘Come and see how beloved are Israel in the sight of the blessed Holy One! Wherever they went in exile, *Shekhinah* accompanied them. When they were exiled to Egypt, *Shekhinah* was with them… When they were exiled to Babylon, *Shekhinah* was with them…And even when they are destined to be redeemed, *Shekhinah* will be with them.’” See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 14.

[^82]: **even though that site was unsuitable** Being outside the land of Israel.

[^83]: **moving above, moving below** The ladder symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who moves up or down, depending on human action, as explained below.

    The word נטיל (*nateil*) can mean “moving” or “taking/receiving.” According to the latter sense (adopted by several commentators), *Shekhinah* receives the flow of emanation and prophecy from above, and the arousal of virtuous human conduct below.

[^84]: ***Nehar Kevar, the River Kevar… kevar*, already—previously** The prophet Ezekiel experienced his vision at the River Kevar, a stream near Nippur in Babylon. In Hebrew, though, the word כבר (*kevar*) means “already,” “long ago,” so the son of Rabbi Tsadok sees here an allusion to the River of Already, the primordial river issuing from Eden.

    See *Re’uyyot Yeḥezqel*, ed. Gruenwald, 111–14; Matt, *Essential Kabbalah*, 126, 205; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6b|1:6b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85a|85a]].

    On the relation between prophetic revelation and the purity of water, see *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1 (citing [[Daniel 08#^8-2|Daniel 8:2]]; [[Daniel 10#^10-4|10:4]]; [[Ezekiel 01#^1-3|Ezekiel 1:3]]); *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-222b|1:222b]]; Idel, in *Sinai* 86 (1979): 1–7.

[^85]: ***Shekhinah* dwelled on it…*A river issues from Eden*…** The river of emanation flows from the highest *sefirot* to water the garden of *Shekhinah*.

[^86]: **This is one of those four rivers** The River Kevar is identified with the Euphrates, one of the four riverheads issuing from the primordial river of Eden. See [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-14|Genesis 2:14]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 16:3, in the name of Rabbi Yudan.

[^87]: **Holy Jacob, Consummate Patriarch** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 76:1: “Rabbi Pinḥas said in the name of Rabbi Re’uven, ‘… The chosen of the patriarchs is Jacob, as is said: *For Yah has chosen Jacob for Himself* ([[Psalms 135#^135-4|Psalms 135:4]]).’” Cf. BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 56#56a|56a]].

    Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who harmonizes and completes the polar opposites, *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, symbolized by Abraham and Isaac. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|1:119b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144b|144b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-152a|152a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-163b|163b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-171b|171b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-172b|172b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-180a|180a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207a|207a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-23a|2:23a]].

[^88]: **In such a sacred site…** In Bethel, within the land of Israel. See above, page 323.

[^89]: **Jacob was not yet married** So he was still incomplete and unfit for prophecy. See BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 63#63a|63a]]: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Any man without a wife is not a man.’”

    Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 17:2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|1:55b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-165a|165a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-5a|3:5a]], [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-7b|7b]], [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-34a|34a]].

[^90]: **Isaac was still alive** So *Shekhinah* rested primarily upon him.

[^91]: **But there the place proved decisive** Since Jacob was then outside the land of Israel, he could not experience full prophetic vision.

[^92]: **consummating the essence of the house and the joyous mother…** By then Jacob had married both Rachel and Leah, who symbolize respectively *Shekhinah* (“essence of the house”) and *Binah* (Divine Mother).

    The phrases “essence of the house” and “joyous mother of children” derive from [[Psalms 113#^113-9|Psalms 113:9]]: *He settles* הבית‎ עקרת (*aqeret ha-bayit*), *the barren woman, in her home as a joyous mother of children*. עקרת (*Aqeret*), “Barren woman,” is reinterpreted as עיקר (*iqqar*), “essence.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 20:2; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 15; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 14:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|50a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154a|154a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157b|157b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]].

[^93]: **from another, higher rung** Directly from *Shekhinah*, who is known both as *Elohim* (*God*) and *night*.

[^94]: **Dream is transmitted by Gabriel, who stands below…** See above, note 72.

[^95]: **Vision, by the rung of that creature who rules the night** Prophetic vision derives from *Netsaḥ* and *Hod*, and is transmitted by *Shekhinah*, who rules the night.

[^96]: **Look at what is written: *Gabriel, help this man understand the vision*** Implying that Gabriel, not *Shekhinah*, conveys prophetic vision.

[^97]: ***I appeared*** The verse continues: *to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob through El Shaddai*.

[^98]: ***El Shaddai*** שדי‎ אל, “God of *Shaddai*.” *Shaddai* is an obscure divine name, which may mean “of the mountain.” The traditional rendering “Almighty” is unjustified. In Kabbalah this name denotes *Shekhinah*.

[^99]: ***mar’ah*, a mirror, displaying another image… all supernal images** *Shekhinah* reflects the images of the higher *sefirot* and specifically the image of Her partner, *Tif’eret*, known as יהוה (*YHVH*). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88b|1:88b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91a|91a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|183a]].

[^100]: **So at that time** When he was still unmarried.

[^101]: **rung on which other rungs depend** *Shekhinah*, from whom lower rungs and powers derive. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|1:74a]].

[^102]: ***head of the bed*** The verse, describing Jacob on his deathbed, concludes: *Israel bowed at the head of the bed*.

[^103]: **He is the *head* of this *bed*, which shines from Him** The *head of the bed* and “head of that ladder” is *Yesod*, linked with *Shekhinah* and illumining Her.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-225b|1:225b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-226b|226b]]. On *Shekhinah* as bed, see also above, page 324.

[^104]: ***Reaching to heaven*, for He is consummation of the body…** *Yesod*, the divine phallus, is the consummation of the male sefirotic body and links *Tif’eret* (*heaven*) with *Shekhinah*. Similarly, in the human male body, the covenant of circumcision constitutes the consummation, linking lower with upper.

[^105]: **deputies of all nations who ascend and descend on this ladder** According to rabbinic tradition, the seventy nations of the world are governed by angels or heavenly princes appointed by God.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; Jubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84b|84b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33a|2:33a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151b|151b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

    In midrashic literature these angels are described as ascending and descending Jacob’s ladder. See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 29:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 23:2; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 2.

[^106]: **When Israel sins, this ladder is lowered and they ascend** Israel’s sins weaken *Shekhinah*, making Her vulnerable to the foreign deputies, who mount Her and gain power. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84b|1:84b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-6a|2:6a–b]].

[^107]: **Here Jacob saw in his dream dominion of Esau…** See the sources cited above, end of note 105. Esau symbolizes the Roman Empire and medieval Christianity.

[^108]: **בו (*bo*), *because of him*** Usually translated: *on it*. The Hebrew preposition ב (*be*) means “on, in,” but also “with, through, on account of, because,” as in [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-28|Genesis 18:28]]; [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^24-16|Deuteronomy 24:16]]. The pronominal suffix ו (*vav*) can mean either “it” or “him.”

[^109]: **when its head withdraws, the ladder is subdued…** When *Yesod* withdraws from *Shekhinah*, interrupting their union, She is lowered and exploited by the foreign deputies.

[^110]: **All is one word** Both renderings of the verse—*ascending and descending on it* and *ascending and descending because of him*—yield a similar meaning.

[^111]: ***God appeared to Solomon… and said to him*…** The verse reads: *YHVH appeared to Solomon… and God said*…

[^112]: **What authority does a dream have to do this?** The angel Gabriel, in charge of dreams, is not authorized to offer divine gifts.

[^113]: **here rung merged with rung—upper rung with lower rung** The wording *God appeared to Solomon in a dream by night* implies that *Shekhinah* (called *Elohim* \[*God*\] and symbolized by *night*) merged with the prince of dreams, though She did not yet appear to Solomon in an unmediated fashion.

[^114]: ***increased*** תרב (*Terev*), *Surpassed*. The verse reads: *The wisdom of Solomon surpassed the wisdom of all the children of the East and all the wisdom of Egypt. Shekhinah*, who derives from *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”), is called in Kabbalah “Wisdom of Solomon.”

[^115]: **moon became full** According to the Midrash, just as the moon does not become full until the fifteenth day of its cycle, so it remained incomplete until the glorious reign of Solomon in the fifteenth generation from Abraham. See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 5:12; and *Shemot Rabbah* 15:26: “When Solomon appeared, the disk of the moon became full.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73b|1:73b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|74a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223a|223a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-225b|225b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-249b|249b]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-85a|2:85a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-61a|3:61a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-83b|83b]] (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 342. In Kabbalah the moon symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

[^116]: ***who had appeared to him twice*** The verse reads: *YHVH became angry with Solomon because his heart turned away from YHVH, God of Israel, who had appeared to him twice*. See [[I Kings 09#^9-2|1 Kings 9:2]]; and *Nefesh David* on this passage, cited in *NZ*.

[^117]: **aspect of dream excelled that of all other humans…** Solomon’s dreams excelled all others since *Shekhinah* conveyed Her wisdom through Gabriel.

[^118]: **Because the sacred covenant was violated … foreign women** See [[I Kings 11#^11-1|1 Kings 11:1–13]]. By cohabiting with foreign women, Solomon violated the covenant of circumcision, symbolizing *Yesod*, the divine phallus. He thereby ruined the union of the divine couple, impairing *Shekhinah* (symbolized by the moon).

    On the link between the covenant of circumcision and divine vision, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:2; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 12:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88b|1:88b]]–89a, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91a|91a–b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b–98b]] Wolfson, *Circle in the Square*, 29–48; idem, *Through a Speculum That Shines*, 342, 357–59, 396–97; idem, “Woman—The Feminine as Other in Theosophic Kabbalah,” 186–88.

[^119]: ***adei ad, until forever… the heavens over the earth*** The word *ad* (*forever*) alludes to *Yesod*, who links the divine couple, *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*, symbolized respectively by heaven and earth. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|1:50a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247b|247b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-22a|2:22a]].

[^120]: ***Here, YHVH… was standing upon it*** The verse continues: *and He said, “I am YHVH, God of Abraham your father and God of Isaac: the land on which you lie I will give to you and to your seed*.”

[^121]: **he saw the cluster of faith as one** Jacob saw the full spectrum of *sefirot* arrayed upon the ladder, *Shekhinah*.

[^122]: ***pillar of salt*** The verse describes what happened when Lot and his family escaped the destruction of Sodom: *His wife looked behind him, and she became a pillar of salt*.

[^123]: ***God of Abraham… God of Isaac*… on the right and the left** These two patriarchs symbolize *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, the right and left divine arms embracing *Shekhinah*.

[^124]: ***Here, YHVH was standing* עליו (*alav*), *upon him*—upon Jacob** The word עליו (*alav*) can be rendered *upon it* (the ladder) or *upon him* (Jacob), as noted in *Bereshit Rabbah* 69:3.

[^125]: **so that all would become a holy chariot: right, left, Jacob in their midst** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:6: “Resh Lakish said, ‘The patriarchs themselves constitute the \[divine\] Chariot.’”

    Here the three patriarchs (Abraham on the right, Isaac on the left, and Jacob between them) symbolize the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, which serves as a chariot for the higher *sefirot*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 69:3; Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 57; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|1:60b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|99a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|2:144a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-38a|3:38a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-99a|99a]].

[^126]: **Assembly of Israel linking with them…*the land*** The verse concludes: *the land on which you lie I will give to you and to your seed. The land* symbolizes *Shekhinah*, also known as Assembly of Israel (see above, note 15).

    *Shekhinah* completes the chariot, serving as the fourth leg of the divine throne. See the citations in the preceding note; Jacob ben Sheshet, *Ha-Emunah ve-ha-Bittaḥon*, 396; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20a|20a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-89b|89b]] (*ST*); Baḥya ben Asher on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^32-10|Genesis 32:10]].

[^127]: ***the land on which you lie*** The verse continues: *I will give to you and to your seed*.

[^128]: **he would become Consummation of the Patriarchs** Balancing *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, and entitled to *lie* on *the land*, to unite with *Shekhinah*. See above, note 87.

[^129]: **the extra ו (*vav*)…revealing that Jacob embraces both sides** The letter ו (*vav*), *and*—whose numerical value is six—alludes to Jacob’s *sefirah, Tif’eret*, core of the six *sefirot* from *Hesed* through *Yesod*. The fact that the *vav* (*and*) is attached to *God of Isaac* indicates that Jacob (symbolizing *Tif’eret*) is also linked to Isaac (symbolizing *Gevurah*).

[^130]: **nothing more is said explicitly** The formula *God of Jacob* does not appear, and the patriarch’s sefirotic role is not mentioned.

[^131]: ***There he set up an altar, and the God of Israel called him God*** *The God of Israel* means “the God of Jacob.” Now that the patriarch is married, this formula can appear and Jacob’s divine status be celebrated. He has attained the rung of *Tif’eret Yisra’el* (“Beauty of Israel”), partner of *Shekhinah*.

    The verse is usually translated: *There he* \[Jacob\] *set up an altar* ישראל‎ אלהי‎ אל‎ לו‎ ויקרא (*va-yiqra lo El Elohei Yisra’el*), *and called it God, God of Israel*. The radical interpretation here derives from BT *Megillah* [[Megillah 18#18a|18a]]: “Rabbi Aḥa said in the name of Rabbi El’azar, ‘How do we know that the blessed Holy One called Jacob “God”? Because it says: *The God of Israel called him God*. For if you imagine that Jacob called the altar “God,” then the verse should read: *Jacob called it* \[*God*\]. Rather, *He called him*, namely Jacob, *God*. And who called him *God*? *The God of Israel*.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 79:8, and Theodor, ad loc.; Rashi, Naḥmanides, Baḥya ben Asher, and Recanati on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^33-20|Genesis 33:20]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138a|1:138a]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-86a|3:86a]]. For various readings of the verse here, see *OY, KP, Sullam, MmD*, Mopsik.

[^132]: **whoever is incomplete below is incomplete above** Whoever remains unmarried cannot penetrate the divine realm.

[^133]: **Jacob is different, for he was complete, but not openly** Even before marrying, Jacob’s sefirotic role is intimated in the phrase *God of Abraham your father and God of Isaac*.

[^134]: ***Here, I am with you; I will protect you wherever you go*** Implying that Jacob was already intimate with God and worthy of His protection.

[^135]: **in the supernal world** To fully attain the rung of *Tif’eret*.

[^136]: **Is it any wonder that he didn’t know?** This being Jacob’s first encounter with God.

[^137]: ***va-anokhi, and I…sweetened the face of YHVH*** The idiom “sweeten the face” means “entreat the favor.” *The face of YHVH* denotes *Shekhinah*, the Divine Presence, who is also known as אנכי (*Anokhi*), *I*, because through Her the divine reveals itself, declaring “I am.”

[^138]: ***She said, “If so, why Anokhi?”*** The verse, describing Rebekah, reads: *The children jostled each other within her, and she said*, “*If so, why* אנכי (*anokhi*), *should I live?” So she went to inquire of YHVH*.

[^139]: **Rebekah saw the radiance of *Shekhinah* in her dwelling** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 60:16: “As long as Sarah existed… the lamp would burn in her tent from one Sabbath eve until the next. As soon as she died, it went out. As soon as Rebekah arrived, it returned.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|1:50a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]].

[^140]: ***She went to inquire of YHVH*, leaving this rung… *Why Anokhi?*** Suffering pangs, Rebekah left the rung of *Anokhi* (*Shekhinah*) and sought the rung of *YHVH* (*Tif’eret*).

[^141]: **alone** Single.

[^142]: ***He was awestruck, and said, “How awesome is this place!”*** The verse continues: *This is none other than the house of God; this is the gate of heaven!*

[^143]: **that *place* mentioned previously** *Shekhinah*. See above, page 320.

[^144]: **this sign of the sacred covenant, which must not stagnate** *Yesod*, the divine phallus, symbolized by the covenant of circumcision, must be continuously active, uniting with *Shekhinah*.

[^145]: **Although these are two aspects, it is one** *Shekhinah* and Yesod are intimately linked, so the expression *this place* alludes to both.

[^146]: ***this* must not stagnate; *this* must not exist alone** The Hebrew pronoun זה (*zeh*), *this*, denotes *Yesod*, which must continually unite with *Shekhinah*. Similarly, the human male must seek his sexual partner.

[^147]: ***house of God*** *Shekhinah*, who contains all the other *sefirot*.

[^148]: **creating fruit within it** The union of *Yesod* and *Shekhinah* engenders souls. See *Bahir* 14 (22); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19a|19a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|33a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b–60a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|85b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 51; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 56 (69). Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Psalms 001#^1-3|Psalms 1:3]].

[^149]: **smooth members…** Rendering two senses of one term, שייפי (*shaifei*). Deriving from a root meaning “to smooth, rub, slip,” the word signifies “limbs” in the *Zohar*, perhaps based on the Talmudic expression (BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 7#7b|7b]]) לשפא‎ איבריה‎ על (*al eivreih le-shafa*), “each limb entered its socket”—“slipping” into place—or “… the chest.”

    See *Arukh*, s.v. *shaf;* Rashi, ad loc., and on [[Job 33#^33-21|Job 33:21]], citing *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 42#42b|42b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-170a|3:170a]].

    Here the word refers to the sefirotic limbs, all of which emanate through *Yesod* into *Shekhinah*.

[^150]: **this is the gate of the whole body… *this is the gate of heaven!*…** *Yesod*, the divine phallus, is the gate of the *sefirot*, specifically of *Tif’eret*, who is the core of the *sefirot* and known as גופא (*gufa*), “body” (or “torso”) and *heaven*.

[^151]: **Above, as written: *this is the gate of heaven!*** Indicating that *Yesod* is fastened to *Tif’eret* (*heaven*) above.

[^152]: **Below, as written: *This is none other than the house of God!*** Indicating that He is fastened to *Shekhinah* (*the house of God*) below.

[^153]: **Yet human beings do not consider its precious glory…** They remain unaware of the wondrous quality of *Yesod* and the profound significance of sexuality, through which one becomes fulfilled, linked with the divine.

[^154]: **His father came and kissed him** Rabbi Tsadok kisses his younger son, who has just completed a long homily, which began above, page 329.

[^155]: **I accompanied them for three parasangs** According to Rav Sheshet (BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 46#46b|46b]]), one should escort his teacher a distance of a parasang. A distinguished teacher, however, is to be escorted for three parasangs. (The Greek parasang equals about 3.5 miles.)

    See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 18:5; *Bereshit Rabbah* 32:10; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51a|1:51a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96b|96b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-14a|2:14a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-187a|187a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8b|3:8b]].

[^156]: **May your words not be in vain!** Since you taught how vital it is to marry, you should immediately fulfill what you said!

[^157]: **these words all inhere in a mystery of wisdom…** The words of the Bible convey secrets of divine life. See above, page 328.

[^158]: ***Jacob made a vow*…** This and the following verse read: *Jacob made a vow, saying, “If Elohim will be with me and watch over me on this way that I go and give me bread to eat and clothing to wear, and if I return in peace to my father’s house, then YHVH will be to me Elohim*.

    *Elohim* is a divine name whose various sefirotic connotations are clarified below.

[^159]: **promised him all this** As indicated in [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-15|Genesis 28:15]]: *Here, I am with you, I will protect you wherever you go, and I will bring you back to this land. I will not leave you until I have done what I have promised you*.

[^160]: **Now, some dreams are true and some are false…** See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), intro, 125–26: “*If Elohim will be with me*… And if not, he would not have believed? Rather, Jacob thought, ‘I don’t know if it is merely a dream—and there is no dream without nonsense—or if it is a \[prophetic\] vision.’ So he said, ‘May He do so for me!’”

    A rabbinic tradition in BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 55#55b|55b]] states that angels convey prophetic dreams, while demons convey false ones. See ibid. 55a: “Rabbi Yoḥanan said in the name of Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai, ‘Just as there cannot be wheat without straw, so there cannot be a dream without nonsense.’ Rabbi Berekhiah said, ‘Though part of a dream is fulfilled, the whole of it is not fulfilled.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:12: “*He* \[Jacob\] *dreamed* ([[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-12|Genesis 28:12]]). Rabbi Abbahu said, ‘Dreams have no effect at all.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 76:2: “Rav Huna said in the name of Rabbi Aḥa, ‘*Here, I am with you* ([[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-15|Genesis 28:15]]); *If Elohim will be with me*. From here we learn that a righteous person has no assurance in this world.’”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|1:83a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130a|130a–b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|183a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-199b|199b–200a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|2:130a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264a|264a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25a|3:25a]], [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-156b|156b]].

[^161]: **I will draw blessings from the bubbling spring… to… *Elohim*** I will attain the rung of *Tif’eret*, known as יהוה (*YHVH*), and thereby convey the stream of emanation from the upper sefirotic source to *Shekhinah*, known as *Elohim*.

[^162]: **Israel, being in the middle, receives all…** Israel, or Jacob, symbolizes *Tif’eret*, situated in the middle of the *sefirot*. He receives the entire flow of emanation from the sefirotic source above.

[^163]: **this site** *Shekhinah*, known as *Elohim*.

[^164]: **connecting the nexus of all with Him** Connecting Yesod with *Shekhinah*. *Yesod* is the cosmic link, joining all the higher *sefirot* together and uniting them with *Shekhinah*.

[^165]: **ושבתי (*Ve-shavti*), *I will return, in peace… I will sit on peace*, precisely!** Rabbi Yehudah plays with the related roots שוב (*shuv*), “to return,” and ישב (*yshv*), “to sit.” See [[Psalms 023#^23-6|Psalms 23:6]].

    When Jacob marries and engenders children, he will attain the rung of *Tif’eret*, closely linked with *Yesod*, who is known as “peace.” Then he will be able to enhance and fulfill *Shekhinah, my father’s house*.

    *Yesod*, the divine phallus, brings peace by uniting *Tif’eret* with *Shekhinah*. See BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 152#152a|152a]], where Rabbi Shim’on son of Ḥalafta refers to the phallus as “peacemaker of the home.”

    *Shekhinah* is known as *house* and derives from *Gevurah*, symbolized by Isaac, Jacob’s father.

[^166]: **there is the Holy Land** Jacob envisions returning to Beer-sheba.

[^167]: **לי‎ יהוה‎ והיה (*ve-hayah YHVH li*), *and YHVH will be mine*…** By ending the citation of the verse here, Rabbi Yehudah has Jacob charting his spiritual journey from *Shekhinah* to *Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*. See Naḥmanides, ad loc.

[^168]: **where I will perform His rite** Uniting Him with *Shekhinah*.

[^169]: **its end is inconsistent with its beginning…** The verse begins in the first person (*overwhelm me*) and concludes in the plural (*our transgressions*).

[^170]: **site where the Book of Sinners lies open** In the realm of *Gevurah*, or *Din* (“Judgment”). See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 16#16b|16b]], in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan: “Three books are opened on Rosh Hashanah: one for the completely wicked, one for the completely righteous, and one for the intermediate.”

[^171]: **above David’s head** Above the realm of *Shekhinah*, or *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”), symbolized by King David.

[^172]: ***words of iniquity overwhelm me*** The sins recorded in the realm of *Gevurah* inflame the harsh quality of Judgment, which now dominates *Shekhinah*.

[^173]: **Rather, he didn’t believe in himself, lest he sin…** See *Mekhilta, Amaleq* (*Beshallaḥ*) 2: “He \[God\] gave Jacob a hint, but he did not perceive it, as is said: *Here, I am with you; I will protect you* ([[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-15|Genesis 28:15]]). In the end he was very afraid, as is said: *Jacob was very frightened and distressed* (ibid. 32:8). A person assured by the blessed Holy One was so afraid? It is simply because Jacob our father said, ‘Woe is me! Perhaps sin will be the cause \[of God’s withdrawing His promised protection\].’”

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 4#4a|4a]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-20|Genesis 28:20]].

[^174]: **I will face even Compassion as Judgment…** I will not rely on God’s Compassion, symbolized by the name *YHVH*, but will serve Him in total awe, constantly aware of His Judgment, symbolized by the name *Elohim*.

    On the contrasting qualities of these two divine names, see *Sifrei*, [[Torah 50 - Ki Tavo#^26-1|Deuteronomy 26]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15; 33:3.

[^175]: **Now I don’t need Judgment** On my journey, I need God’s quality of Compassion.

[^176]: **I will merge with Judgment, bonding with it** Returning to the house of Isaac, symbolizing *Din* (“Judgment”), I will merge my quality, *Tif’eret*, known as *Raḥamim* (“Compassion”), with *Din*.

[^177]: **I need Judgment to protect me** I need the protective power of *Din*, or *Gevurah* (“Power”).

[^178]: **this *stone* blessed from right and left…** The *stone* symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is blessed by both sides of the *sefirot* above Her, as well as by human righteous action below.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72a|1:72a]]–b, [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-146b|146b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231a|231a]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-229b|2:229b–230a]].

    The “tithe” refers to the conclusion of Jacob’s promise in this verse: *and everything that You give me I will surely tithe to You*. This tithe also symbolizes *Shekhinah*, tenth of the *sefirot*, whose blessing is ensured by Jacob’s offering.

[^179]: **supernal stone** *Shekhinah*.

[^180]: **many stones serving as the foundation upon which it rests** Angelic powers beneath *Shekhinah*. See above, page 323.

[^181]: **yet it is written: *This stone that I set up as a pillar*!** Implying that *this stone* stands below, not above.

[^182]: **Here he raised Her as a supernal pillar… all the glory of *this*…** Jacob raised *Shekhinah* toward Her partner, *Tif’eret*, linking Her with Him by means of *Yesod*, the divine phallus, who is known as *this* (see above, page 338). The world below is sustained only through the union of the masculine divinity with *Shekhinah, the house of Elohim*.

[^183]: ***Will be a house of Elohim*, forever** The Temple will be built and rebuilt here.

[^184]: **The verse should read: *a house of YHVH*…** The usual designation of the Temple, as indicated in the verse from Psalms. The preceding citation, *to establish the house of YHVH*, does not appear in the Bible. See [[Ezra 01#^1-5|Ezra 1:5]]; [[II Chronicles 31#^31-11|2 Chronicles 31:11]].

    Sefirotically, since *Shekhinah* welcomes, or houses, *Tif’eret*, She should be called *house of YHVH*, not *house of Elohim*.

[^185]: **site of the house of Judgment…** *Shekhinah* is characterized by *Din* (“Judgment”). First, She is the daughter of *Binah*, who is the source of Judgment and known as both Jubilee and *Elohim*, or “Living *Elohim*.” Second, She derives from *Gevurah*, or *Din*, symbolized by Isaac.

    On *Binah* as Jubilee, see above, note 19.

[^186]: **Even though judgments arouse from Jubilee…** Although *Binah* generates *Din*, and in fact, all of the seven lower *sefirot*, which express both compassion and judgment.

[^187]: **all freedom issues from Her** Symbolized by Jubilee, She is the source of redemption and liberation, specifically the Exodus from Egypt. See above, note 19.

[^188]: **Rather, *house of Elohim*—aspect of severe Judgment** The title *house of Elohim* describes *Shekhinah*, who houses *Din* (“Judgment”), known as *Elohim*.

[^189]: **If for good, from the side of the left love arouses…** The passion of the left side stimulates the union of the masculine divinity with *Shekhinah*.

[^190]: **If for evil, from the side of the left severe Judgment arouses…** Then the harsh aspect of *Din* (symbolized by north) dominates, generating evil. See *Bahir* 109 (162).

[^191]: **Certainly the supernal world is a *great king***… *Binah*, the supernal world, is a *great king*, while *Shekhinah* is the royal city and *house of Elohim*, house of *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-206b|1:206b]].

    The lower king is either *Tif’eret* or *Shekhinah*, known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”).

[^192]: **field of Ono** A fertile region near Lydda. See BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 111#111b|111b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-10b|2:10b]], [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-103b|103b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-144b|3:144b]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-217b|217b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-81b|81b]] (*MhN, Rut*), [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-84b|84b]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^193]: **saw Elijah** According to the Bible ([[II Kings 11#^11-12|2 Kings 11:12]]), the prophet Elijah did not die a normal death but was carried off to heaven in a chariot of fire. He became associated with the Messianic age ([[Malachi 3#^3-23|Malachi 3:23–24]]) and in rabbinic tradition is described as “still existing” (BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 121#121b|121b]]) and revealing divine secrets to righteous humans (BT *Bava Metsi’a* 59b).

    In Kabbalah mystical experiences are known as revelations of Elijah. See Scholem, *On the Kabbalah*, 19–21; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-1b|1:1b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-221a|3:221a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231a|231a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-241b|241b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.11 - Zohar al ha-Parashot#^zh-59d|59d]], [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-62c|62c]] (*ShS*), [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-63d|63d]] (*ShS*), [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-70d|70d]] (*ShS*), [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-73c|73c]] (*ShS*).

[^194]: **He said to him** Rabbi Ḥiyya said to Elijah.

[^195]: **luster** קסטוטירא (*Qastutira*), apparently derived from the Aramaic קסיטרא (*qasitra*) and the Greek *kassiteros*, “tin.” See *Targum Yerushalmi* and *Targum Yerushalmi* (frag.), [[Torah 42 - Matot#^31-22|Numbers 31:22]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125a|1:125a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168a|168a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-24b|2:24b]]; *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 188.

[^196]: **He said** Elijah said.

[^197]: **Jerusalem is about to be destroyed** The Romans destroyed Jerusalem and burned the Temple in 70 C.E. Various commentators try to explain how Elijah could predict the destruction to these two companions of Rabbi Shim’on in the second century C.E.—after the fact! The explanations are rather contrived. Apparently the thirteenth-century author(s) either didn’t care about the chronology or intentionally inserted an anachronism to confuse or shock the reader. See above, note 65.

[^198]: **Jerusalem is judgment…** Jerusalem symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who derives from *Din* (“Judgment, Justice”). See [[Lamentations 1#^1-21|Lamentations 1:21]]: צדק (*Tsedeq*), *Justice, lodges there*. The continued existence of Jerusalem and the fulfillment of *Shekhinah* depend upon justice; when this is lacking, *Shekhinah* weakens and Jerusalem becomes vulnerable to harsh judgment.

    See BT *Bava Metsi’a* 30b: “Rabbi Yoḥanan said, ‘Jerusalem was destroyed only because they rendered judgment there strictly in accordance with the law of Torah \[rather than going beyond the requirements of the law\].’”

[^199]: **Samael has already been empowered over her…** Over Jerusalem and the mighty scholars of Torah. The phrasing here apparently derives from a passage in Heikhalot literature describing the martyrdom of ten famous rabbis during the reign of the Roman Emperor Hadrian in approximately 135 C.E. See Schäfer, *Synopse zur Hekhalot-Literatur*, §108.

[^200]: **Torah is the Tree of Life…** Torah is identified with the Tree of Life, based on the description of wisdom in [[Proverbs 03#^3-18|Proverbs 3:18]]: *She is a tree of life to those who grasp her*. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 32#32b|32b]], 61b.

    Here Torah symbolizes *Tif’eret*, known as the Tree of Life, who stands above Jerusalem, symbolizing *Shekhinah*.

[^201]: **As long as Torah is aroused below** By human study.

[^202]: **Tree of Life withdraws from her** From Jerusalem and from *Shekhinah*, interrupting the divine union.

[^203]: ***The voice is the voice of Jacob*…** The verse continues: *and the hands are the hands of Esau*. According to a midrashic reading, as long as *the voice of Jacob* is engaged in prayer and study, *the hands of Esau* are powerless against him, but when *the voice of Jacob* falls silent or complains against God, *the hands of Esau* dominate him. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 65:20; *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 15:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-171a|1:171a]].

    Here *the voice* symbolizes *Tif’eret*, known as both *voice of Jacob* and Torah. “Speech” symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who conveys the divine word. She is empowered by human study of Torah, which stimulates *Tif’eret* to unite with Her. Esau symbolizes Rome, led by Samael.

[^204]: ***Unless YHVH watches over the city*—those engaged in Torah** Those who study Torah, which symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who is also known as *YHVH*.

[^205]: **the Holy City** Jerusalem.

[^206]: **not by the mightiest men in the world** See *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 2: “Rabbi \[Yehudah Nesi’ah II\] sent Rabbi Assi and Rabbi Ammi on a mission to improve the cities of the land of Israel. They entered a city and said to the residents, ‘Bring us the guardians of the city.’ They brought them the commander of the watch and the guardsman. They \[the rabbis\] exclaimed, ‘Are these the guardians of the city? These are the destroyers of the city!’ They asked, ‘Who, then, are the guardians of the city?’ They replied, ‘The teachers of Bible and Mishnah, who meditate upon, teach, and preserve Torah day and night, in accord with what is said: *Meditate on it day and night* ([[Joshua 01#^1-8|Joshua 1:8]]), and similarly: *Unless YHVH builds the house*, \[*its builders labor in vain. Unless YHVH watches over the city, the watchman guards in vain*\] ([[Psalms 127#^127-1|Psalms 127:1]]).’”

[^207]: ***He looked, and here: a well in the field*…** The entire verse reads: *He looked, and here: a well in the field, and there were three flocks of sheep lying beside it, for from that well the flocks were watered. The stone on the mouth of the well was large*.

[^208]: **he should have wailed a lamentation…** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 7#7b|7b]]: “*A psalm of David, when he fled from his son Absalom. A psalm of David?* The verse should read: *A lamentation of David*! Rabbi Shim’on son of Avishalom said, ‘To what can this be compared? To a person in debt. Until he pays it off, he is distressed; once he pays it, he rejoices. So it was with David. When the blessed Holy One said to him, *I am about to raise up evil against you from your own house* ([[II Samuel 12#^12-11|2 Samuel 12:11]]), he was distressed, thinking: “Perhaps it will be a slave or a bastard who has no pity on me.” Once he saw that it was Absalom, he rejoiced, and therefore he said, *A psalm*.’”

[^209]: **he thought that the blessed Holy One had deferred his sin…** David thought that God had deferred punishment for his cardinal sin (with Bathsheba) until the world to come. Seeing Bathsheba bathing, David was attracted to her and slept with her. He then arranged for her husband, Uriah the Hittite, to die in battle, after which he married her. See [[II Samuel 11#^11-1|2 Samuel 11]]–12.

[^210]: **as soon as he saw that He wanted to collect His due from him here…** See *Midrash Tehillim* 3:1: “The righteous rejoice when He executes judgment upon them, for He cleanses them of their sins. Know that this is so, for when the Attribute of Judgment struck David, he began to rejoice and sing: *A psalm of David*.”

[^211]: **Jacob fled… Moses fled…** See *Midrash Tehillim* 3:3.

[^212]: **when David fled…** On David’s flight from Absalom, see [[II Samuel 15#^15-1|2 Samuel 15]]–17; *Midrash Tehillim* 3:3.

[^213]: **They all encountered this well** Both Jacob and Moses encountered a well, symbolizing *Shekhinah*. See the conclusion of the verse cited ([[Torah 13 - Shemot#^2-15|Exodus 2:15]]): *He settled in the land of Midian, and he sat upon the well*. Similarly, Abraham and Isaac are linked with Beer-sheba (“Well of Seven”).

[^214]: **Because at that time it was hostile toward David…** David’s sin with Bathsheba, who symbolizes *Shekhinah*, had alienated him from the divine feminine, so She along with Her symbol (the well) were hostile toward him. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93b|1:93b]]–94a.

[^215]: **water rose… like a woman delighting with her husband** *Shekhinah* was eager to unite with Jacob and Moses, both of whom symbolize *Tif’eret* and are described in the *Zohar* as Her “husband” or “master of the house.” See above, note 42; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|1:138b]].

    On the female waters, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 13:13: “Rabbi Shim’on son of El’azar said, ‘Every single handbreadth \[of water\] descending from above is met by two handbreadths emitted by the earth. What is the reason? *Deep calls to deep*… ([[Psalms 042#^42-8|Psalms 42:8]]).’ Rabbi Levi said, ‘The upper waters are male; the lower, female. The former cry to the latter, “Receive us! You are creatures of the blessed Holy One and we are His messengers.” They immediately receive them, as is written: *Let the earth open* ([[Isaiah 45#^45-8|Isaiah 45:8]])—like a female opening to a male.’”

    See *Tosefta, Ta’anit* 1:4; 1 Enoch 54:8; *Seder Rabbah di-Vreshit*, 10 (*Battei Midrashot*, 1:25); *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 23; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-17b|1:17b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|29b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|35a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46a|46a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|60b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|85b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a–b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245b|245b]]; [[Zohar 3.223b|3:223b]]. According to the second-century Greek physician Galen, sperm is generated by both male and female. See [[Torah 27 - Tazria#^12-2|Leviticus 12:2]]; Ibn Ezra, Naḥmanides, Baḥya ben Asher, and Sforno, ad loc.; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 60#60a|60a]]; *Niddah* [[Niddah 31#31a|31a]]. The ascent of “female waters” is emphasized by Isaac Luria.

[^216]: **Elijah fled and didn’t encounter it** On Elijah’s flight, see [[I Kings 19#^19-1|1 Kings 19]]. *Pesiqta Rabbati* 4 (citing [[I Kings 19#^19-3|1 Kings 19:3]]) notes that Elijah also encountered a well, namely, Beer-sheba (“Well of Seven”).

[^217]: **Elijah was below the well… he is an angel…** Elijah is an angel dwelling beneath *Shekhinah*, unlike Moses and Jacob, who attained the rung of *Tif’eret*, above Her.

    See above, note 193. Various sources allude to Elijah’s heavenly or angelic status. See *Midrash Tehillim* 8:7: “*The bird of heaven* ([[Psalms 008#^8-9|Psalms 8:9]])—this is Elijah, who flies through the world as a bird.” In *Targum Qohelet* 10:20 and *Ma’yan Ḥokhmah* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 1:60), Elijah is identified as “Master of Wings.” See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 4#4b|4b]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 29, and David Luria, ad loc., nn. 66–67; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-209a|209a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245b|245b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-197a|2:197a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-88b|3:88b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-84c|84c]]–d (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 60–63, 68–71; Moses Cordovero, *Pardes Rimmonim* 24:14.

[^218]: **he saw this well above, one corresponding to the other** As Jacob encountered the well, he saw the divine well, *Shekhinah*.

[^219]: ***All the flocks were gathered there*** The full verse reads: *When all the flocks were gathered there, they would roll the stone off the mouth of the well and water the sheep, and then they would put the stone back in its place on the mouth of the well*. The wording *all the flocks* implies more than just these three.

[^220]: **these three are south, east, and north…** Symbolizing the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*. The polar opposites *Ḥesed* (the warm south) and *Gevurah* (the cold north) are balanced by *Tif’eret* (east). See *Zohar* [[#^z1-151b|1:151b]] (*ST*).

[^221]: **These stand beside this well, embracing it, filling it** The three principal flocks unite with *Shekhinah*, conveying to Her the flow of emanation.

[^222]: ***the flocks were watered…watering all beasts*…** The numerous flocks symbolize the camps of angels, *beasts of the field*, nourished by *Shekhinah*, who is pictured as a field. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-151b|1:151b]]–152a (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-161b|161b]].

[^223]: ***All the flocks…All the streams flow into the sea*** According to this interpretation, the flocks symbolize all the *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*, which pour into the sea of *Shekhinah*.

[^224]: ***They would roll the stone off the mouth of the well*** The verse continues: *and water the sheep*.

[^225]: **removing from Her the rigor of severe Judgment…** The severe aspect of Judgment freezes the water in the well of *Shekhinah*, which can only be thawed by an abundant flow of emanation. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-152a|1:152a]] (*ST*).

[^226]: **south is invigorated, and north cannot congeal the water** The warm waters of *Ḥesed* gush into *Shekhinah*, and *Din* (“Judgment”) can no longer freeze them.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-161b|161b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-30a|2:30a]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 341.

[^227]: **the world needs Her judgment…** The power of *Din* has a positive aspect, since by executing judgment *Shekhinah* punishes wrongdoing.

[^228]: **When he sat by the well** When Jacob sat by the well.

[^229]: ***While he was still speaking with them, Rachel came*…** The two verses read: *While he was still speaking with them, Rachel came with her father’s sheep, for she was a shepherdess. When Jacob saw Rachel, daughter of his uncle Laban, and the sheep of his uncle Laban, he went over and rolled the stone off the mouth of the well and watered the sheep of his uncle Laban*.

[^230]: ***Moses rose up and saved them*** The verse continues: *and watered their sheep*.

[^231]: **there Zipporah appeared** See [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^2-16|Exodus 2:16–21]].

[^232]: **That well brought this about for them** They discovered their wives at the well through the agency of the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*.

[^233]: **alluding to Seven…שבע‎ באר (*Be’er Sheva*), Well of Seven** *Shekhinah* is called Seven and Well of Seven because She receives the flow of emanation from seven *sefirot* (*Binah* through *Yesod*, or *Ḥesed* through *Shekhinah* Herself). See above, page 321.

[^234]: **inscribed only once** The well is mentioned only once.

[^235]: **Moses separated himself completely from the house below** According to rabbinic tradition, after encountering God on Mount Sinai, Moses abstained from sexual contact with his wife and maintained union with *Shekhinah*.

    See *Sifrei*, Numbers 99; BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 87#87a|87a]]; *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 13; Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Yesodei ha-Torah* 7:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-22a|1:22a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-234b|234b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-222a|2:222a]]; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|3:148a]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-180a|180a]].

[^236]: **Jacob did not separate at all** Jacob maintained sexual relations with his two wives, Leah and Rachel, and with their maidservants, Bilhah and Zilpah.

[^237]: ***One is my dove, my perfect one, only one of her mother*** Alluding to *Shekhinah*, only daughter of *Binah*.

[^238]: **Moses was master of the house, ascending on high** Moses served as husband of *Shekhinah*. See above, note 42.

[^239]: **Of Moses is written… Of Jacob is written…** Moses was more deeply intimate with *Shekhinah* than was Jacob.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]]–22a, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-236b|236b]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|3:187b]]; Liebes, “Myth vs. Symbol,” 213–19.

[^240]: **whoever engages in Torah gains total freedom—freedom from death…** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 18:3: “חרות (*Ḥarut*), *Engraved, upon the tablets* ([[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^32-16|Exodus 32:16]]). Do not read *ḥarut*, engraved, but rather חירות (*ḥeirut*), freedom. Rabbi Yehudah, Rabbi Neḥemiah, and the Rabbis: Rabbi Yehudah said, ‘Freedom from the Angel of Death.’ Rabbi Neḥemiah said, ‘Freedom from kingdoms.’ The Rabbis said, ‘Freedom from suffering.’”

    See BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 54#54a|54a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-131b|1:131b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-45b|2:45b]]. On the notion that the Angel of Death is powerless against one engaged in Torah, see BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 30#30b|30b]]; *Bava Metsi’a* 86a; *Makkot* [[Makkot 10#10a|10a]].

[^241]: **Tree of Life** Torah is identified with the Tree of Life, based on the description of wisdom in [[Proverbs 03#^3-18|Proverbs 3:18]]: *She is a tree of life to those who grasp her*. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 32#32b|32b]], 61b.

    In Kabbalah both Torah and Tree of Life symbolize *Tif’eret*.

[^242]: **Tree of Death** According to *Seder Eliyyahu Rabbah* 5, the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil is called the Tree of Death because when Adam and Eve ate its fruit, death ensued (see [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-17|Genesis 2:17]]).

    Here the term designates the demonic, though it often appears in the *Zohar* as a name of *Shekhinah* because of Her association with judgment and because if She is separated from the other *sefirot*, the vivifying flow of emanation is cut off and replaced by a deadly potion. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51b|51b–52a]]. On the relation between *Shekhinah* and the demonic, see Scholem, *On the Mystical Shape of the Godhead*, 189–92; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:376–79.

[^243]: ***If you slacken*—losing your grip on Torah…** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 63#63a|63a]]: “Rabbi Tavi said in the name of Rabbi Yoshiyah, ‘Whoever relinquishes words of Torah has no strength to stand in a day of adversity, as is said: *If you slacken, in the day of adversity how narrow is your strength!’”*

[^244]: ***strength of koh is narrow*** כה (*Koh*), “Here, thus,” designates *Shekhinah*, the Divine Presence, which is always right here.

[^245]: **then *the strength of koh is narrow* … painfully constricted** By abandoning Torah, one endangers both himself and *Shekhinah. צר (*Tsar*) means both “narrow” and “distress.”*

    See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 16#16a|16a]]: “Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘Whoever sins secretly, it is as if he squeezes \[or: thrusts away\] the feet of *Shekhinah*.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-174b|1:174b]].

[^246]: **loved above, loved below** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 17#17a|17a]]; *Qiddushin* [[Kiddushin 71#71a|71a]]; *Bahir* 80 (111); *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-45b|1:45b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-159a|159a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|242b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-46a|2:46a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-228a|228a]].

[^247]: ***YHVH loved him*** Referring to Solomon.

[^248]: ***strength of koh is tsar*, his ‘adversary,’ hostile toward him** *Shekhinah* turns into his adversary. See above, note 214. On the link between the demonic and *Shekhinah*, see above, note 242.

[^249]: **that one called Evil dominates him** The demonic dominates him through the evil impulse. See BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 16#16a|16a]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘Satan, the evil impulse, and the Angel of Death are one and the same.’”

[^250]: **prevails over the body** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-213b|2:213b]], [[Zohar 2.269a|269a]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-172b|3:172b]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:764–65.

[^251]: **does not prevail before obtaining authorization** See the description of Satan’s itinerary in BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 16#16a|16a]]: “He descends and seduces, ascends and arouses wrath, obtains authorization, and seizes the soul.” According to Rabbi Abba, however, the demonic has no power over the soul, which departs on its own. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|1:148a]]–b (*ST*).

[^252]: **We have learned that they control all events of the world…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-262b|2:262b–269a]] (*Heikh*).

[^253]: **End of the Left…an end on the right and an end on the left** The demonic derives from *Gevurah* on the left side, while *Shekhinah* is the culmination of the flow of emanation, characterized by *Ḥesed* on the right.

    See *Eikhah Rabbah* 2:6; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 17:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|1:54a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|62b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|63a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-75a|75a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-210b|210b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-34a|2:34a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-134a|134a–b]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-181b|181b]].

[^254]: **End of all flesh—called End of all flesh, not End of all spirit** The demonic manifests as the Angel of Death, destroying all flesh, but he cannot harm the spirit, which is subject to *Shekhinah*. The phrase “End of all flesh” derives from the announcement of the Flood in [[Torah 02 - Noach#^6-13|Genesis 6:13]]: *God said to Noah, “End of all flesh has come before Me*.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|1:54a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58a|58a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|62b]].

[^255]: **mystery of world of male and world of female** The divine couple. See above, page 322.

[^256]: **bountiful dew** Based on [[Psalms 068#^68-10|Psalms 68:10]]: *bountiful rain*.

[^257]: **this side, mystery of the south** The right side of the lower *sefirot*, extending from *Ḥesed*, identified with the south, to *Shekhinah*, “End of the Right.”

[^258]: **side of the north** Symbolizing *Gevurah*, divine source of the demonic. See [[Jeremiah 01#^1-14|Jeremiah 1:14]]; *Bahir* 109 (162).

[^259]: **scoria** קסטופא (*Qastupha*), “Dross, slag, scoria,” a neologism apparently based on the Aramaic כוספא (*kuspa*), “pomace, husk, residue.” Gold symbolizes *Gevurah*, while the dross symbolizes the forces of evil left over after the refining process of emanation.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-50b|3:50b]]; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 348. Cf. the synonymous neologism סוספיתא (*suspita*) in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|1:30a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-71b|71b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118b|118b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-228a|228a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-24b|2:24b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-203a|203a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-224b|224b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-236b|236b]]; Moses de León, *Sod Eser Sefirot*, 384; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 389, n. 54. On gold and the north, see [[Job 37#^37-22|Job 37:22]].

[^260]: **linked to this above, linked below** Impurity is linked to its source in *Gevurah* above, and to the demonic depths below. For other interpretations, see Galante, Mopsik, *MmD*.

[^261]: **rider, serpent, mystery of male and female** The demonic couple of Samael and Lilith, pictured as a rider on his serpent.

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 13: “Samael… took his band and descended and saw all the creatures created by the blessed Holy One. He determined that the most cunningly evil was the serpent, as is said: *Now the serpent was slier than any creature of the field that YHVH Elohim had made*. He \[the serpent\] looked like a camel, and he \[Samael\] mounted and rode him.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64a|64a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-137b|137b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-146a|146a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|148a]] (*ST*).

[^262]: **This mystery is Azazel** This wilderness demon appears in [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^16-1|Leviticus 16]], where he is offered a scapegoat bearing the sins of Israel on Yom Kippur. In *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 46, he is identified with Satan.

    See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^16-8|Leviticus 16:8]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-11a|1:11a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64a|64a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65a|65a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-113b|113b–114b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-139b|139b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-142b|142b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-145b|145b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-174b|174b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-190a|190a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-210b|210b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-154b|2:154b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-237b|237b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266b|266b]]; [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-63a|3:63a]] (*Piq*), [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-102a|102a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-202b|202b–203a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-258b|258b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-87b|87b]]–c (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 49; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:890–95.

[^263]: **Esau… red as a rose, hairy as a goat** In [[Torah 06 - Toldot#^25-25|Genesis 25:25]], Esau is described as *ruddy, like a hairy mantle all over*, and in 27:11 as שעיר‎ איש (*ish sa’ir*), *a hairy man*. The word שעיר (*sa’ir*) also means “goat, demon, satyr.” Further, Esau and his descendants inhabited שעיר (*se’ir*), *Seir* (see [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^32-4|Genesis 32:4]]; [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^36-8|36:8–9]]).

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 65:15; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65a|1:65a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-145b|145b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-64a|3:64a]].

[^264]: **root of empowered, shield-bearing chieftains ruling the world…** Esau is prince of demons. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-177a|1:177a]].

[^265]: **the blessed Holy One is *justice* *Tif’eret*,** the blessed Holy One, is symbolized by *justice*.

[^266]: **to another way** Following the demonic.

[^267]: **all His ways *justice*** See [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-4|Deuteronomy 32:4]].

[^268]: **Now, can a person *perform tsedaqah, charity, all the time*?** The Hebrew word צדקה (*tsedaqah*) means both “righteousness” and “charity.” This question derives from BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 50#50a|50a]].

[^269]: **site of Jacob, supernal chariot** Jacob (symbolizing *Tif’eret*) together with Abraham and Isaac (who symbolize *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*) constitute a throne or chariot for the higher *sefirot*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:6: “Resh Lakish said, ‘The patriarchs themselves constitute the \[divine\] Chariot.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 69:3; Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 57; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|1:60b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|99a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|2:144a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-38a|3:38a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-99a|99a]].

[^270]: **spring of all springs** The highest and deepest sefirotic realm.

[^271]: **all of them are called עת (*et*), *time*** All of these forces beneath *Shekhinah* share Her name: עת (*et*), *time*. *Shekhinah* is given this name because She is constantly changing, influenced by various forces at various times. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-116b|1:116b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-194a|194a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-198a|198a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-155b|2:155b]].

[^272]: **When Israel left the Holy Land, they came under alien authority** See *Sifra, Behar* 5:4, 109c: “Every Israelite who dwells in the land of Israel accepts upon himself the yoke of the Kingdom of Heaven, and everyone who leaves the land is like an idol worshiper.”

    See *Tosefta, Avodah Zarah* 4:5; BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 110#110b|110b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95b|1:95b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-177a|177a]]; [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-266b|3:266b]].

    See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel is watered by the blessed Holy One Himself, while the rest of the world is watered by a messenger.”

[^273]: **blessings were withheld from the world** See M *Sotah* 9:12: “Rabban Shi’mon son of Gamliel says in the name of Rabbi Yehoshu’a, ‘Ever since the day the Temple was destroyed, there is no day without a curse, dew has not descended for blessing, and flavor has departed from fruit.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|1:55b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-70b|70b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-134a|134a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-74b|3:74b]].

[^274]: **as soon as he left the land…** See above, page 321.

[^275]: **As soon as he sat by the well, water rose toward him…** See above, pages 346–48.

[^276]: ***Jacob…went to Haran…and went to the land of the Easterners*** According to the plain meaning of the text, *Haran* and *the land of the Easterners* are one and the same, and [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-10|Genesis 28:10]] should be rendered: *Jacob…set out for Haran*. See Rashi, Rashbam, and Bekhor Shor, ad loc. Here, though, Rabbi Abba reads the text hyperliterally: *Jacob … went to Haran…and* \[subsequently\] *went to the land of the Easterners*.

[^277]: ***He said to them, ‘Do you know Laban son of Nahor?’*…** The verse continues: *They said, ‘We know him.’*

[^278]: **I desire to enter *Shekhinah*, for I desire to couple** By finding a wife, one attains intimacy with the divine feminine. See above, page 331.

[^279]: **When Father married he sent a servant…** See [[Torah 05 - Chayei Sara#^24-1|Genesis 24]], where Abraham sends a servant to find a wife for Isaac.

[^280]: **Haran was right there, and this well was in the field** Haran and *the land of the Easterners* are synonymous; the well was in a field outside the city.

[^281]: ***She ran and told her father*** Rachel ran and told her father, Laban, that she had met Jacob at the well.

[^282]: **since she engendered all those tribes** Leah, Laban’s older daughter and Jacob’s first wife, gave birth to six sons: Reuben, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun.

[^283]: **the blessed Holy One did not want to couple Leah… openly…** Jacob discovered the identity of his first wife only the morning after the consummation of their wedding. The reason God brought this about was that Leah symbolizes *Binah*, who is concealed. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-152a|1:152a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]].

[^284]: **in order to draw Jacob’s heart to the beauty of Rachel…** Jacob’s unfulfilled passion for Rachel compelled him to stay with Laban for many years, during which time Leah gave birth to many children.

[^285]: **Those shepherds told him: *Here comes Rachel*…** The shepherds themselves spoke these words, not the biblical narrator. See Rashi and Ralbag, ad loc.

[^286]: ***Jacob said to Laban, ‘I will serve you seven years…’*** The verse reads: *He said, “I will serve you seven years….”*

[^287]: **moon is seven years old** The moon symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is filled with the emanation of seven *sefirot* (*Binah* through *Yesod*, or *Ḥesed* through *Shekhinah* Herself).

[^288]: **so that he would approach her fittingly** So Jacob would convey the entire stream of emanation to Rachel, who symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

[^289]: **so that he would be Heaven, and she, Earth** So he would play the role of *Tif’eret*, known as Heaven, and she would play the role of *Shekhinah*, known as Earth.

[^290]: ***They seemed to him like only…a few days*** The full verse reads: *Jacob served seven years for Rachel, and they seemed to him like only a few days because of his love for her*.

[^291]: **those supernal ones—*aḥadim*, united…** The seven *sefirot* above, known as cosmic *days*, united with one another. For *aḥadim* as “united,” see [[Ezekiel 37#^37-17|Ezekiel 37:17]].

[^292]: **corresponding to the supernal pattern** As above, *Tif’eret* unites with *Shekhinah*, conveying to Her the sevenfold emanation.

[^293]: ***Tov, Better, that I give her to you*** The verse continues: *rather than give her to another man*. The word *tov* designates *Yesod* (known as Righteous One), who channels the entire flow to *Shekhinah*, based on [[Isaiah 03#^3-10|Isaiah 3:10]]: *Say of the righteous one that he is good*. Alternatively, *tov* designates *Ḥesed*, first to issue from *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127a|2:127a]].

[^294]: **Sabbatical** According to the Bible, every seventh year is a Sabbatical (Hebrew, שמטה \[*shemittah*\], “release”), during which the land must lie fallow and at the end of which all debts are remitted ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-1|Leviticus 25:1–24]]; [[Torah 47 - Re'eh#^15-1|Deuteronomy 15:1–3]]). In Kabbalah the Sabbatical symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is seventh of the lower *sefirot* and is filled with the emanation of seven *sefirot*. See above, pages 322–23.

[^295]: **Jubilee is universally concealed… Sabbatical is revealed** According to the biblical cycle, after seven Sabbaticals comes the Jubilee, proclaimed every fifty years, when slaves are released and land reverts to its original owner ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-8|Leviticus 25:8–55]]). In Kabbalah the Jubilee symbolizes *Binah*, who generally is characterized by the number fifty, based on BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 21#21b|21b]], where Rav and Shemu’el teach: “Fifty gates of בינה (*binah*), understanding, were created in the world, all of which were given to Moses except for one, as is said: *You made him little less than God* ([[Psalms 008#^8-6|Psalms 8:6]]).” *Binah* is the source of redemption and liberation, specifically the Exodus from Egypt. See above, page 322.

    Here Rabbi El’azar notes that Jubilee and Sabbatical respectively reflect the concealed nature of *Binah* and the revealed nature of *Shekhinah*.

[^296]: **When Jacob had served the first seven years** Jacob served Laban seven years in order to marry Rachel, but Laban tricked him, substituting Leah for Rachel. Then Jacob married Rachel and served Laban for seven more years. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-15|Genesis 29:15–30]].

[^297]: **The *world* above: Jubilee—there, beginning…** The divine voice reassures Jacob that he was meant to first marry Leah, symbolizing *Binah*, the hidden upper world of Jubilee, the Divine Mother whose womb holds the seven lower *sefirot*.
