Just as *Binah* and Her gestating progeny are concealed, the true significance of Jacob’s first seven years of service was concealed from him: he thought he was serving Laban for Rachel, who symbolizes Sabbatical, whereas he was really serving for Leah, so that he could begin from Jubilee.

[^298]: **he served revealed years of Sabbatical…** Jacob then served an additional seven years for Rachel, symbolizing the lower world, *Shekhinah*, known as Sabbatical, who contains seven *sefirot* in a more revealed mode. Jacob, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, spans both sefirotic worlds.

    On the phrase *from world to world*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34a|1:34a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-22a|2:22a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-53b|53b]]; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-145b|3:145b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285b|285b]], [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-297b|297b]].

    The act of marrying two sisters is explicitly forbidden in [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^18-18|Leviticus 18:18]]. See BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 119#119b|119b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|1:76a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168a|168a]]. For its mystical justification, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-126b|2:126b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 351–55; idem, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 40–41.

[^299]: **Leah gave birth to six sons and one daughter, fittingly so…** Leah’s six sons (Reuben, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, Zebulun) symbolize the six sons of *Binah* (from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*), who stand above *Shekhinah*, symbolized by Leah’s daughter, Dinah.

    The term “six directions” derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13, where it refers to: above, below, north, south, east, and west. Here it designates *Binah*’s six sons.

[^300]: **Rachel gave birth to two righteous ones, fittingly so…** Rachel’s two sons (Joseph and Benjamin) symbolize *Yesod* (known as Righteous One) and the righteous human being. Both of these righteous ones are intimate partners of *Shekhinah*, who is known as Sabbatical and *land*. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-155a|1:155a]]–b, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245b|245b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-211b|2:211b]].

    In rabbinic literature Joseph is granted the title Righteous in recognition of resisting the sexual advances of Potiphar’s wife. See [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^39-1|Genesis 39]]; BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 35#35b|35b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 93:7; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana, nispaḥim*, 460. Cf. *Tanḥuma, Bereshit* 5, and *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 38, which cite [[Amos 2#^2-6|Amos 2:6]].

    According to Kabbalah, because of his sexual purity Joseph attained the level of *Yesod*, the divine phallus and site of the covenant. *Yesod* is known as Righteous One, based on [[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]: עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*). The verse literally means *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, but is understood as *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*. See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 12#12b|12b]]; *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

    Benjamin is the only other one of Jacob’s sons who is accorded the title Righteous. See BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 12#12a|12a]]; *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 11#11a|11a]]; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 3.

[^301]: **From Righteous One above, She obtains supernal water** From *Yesod, Shekhinah* receives the flow of emanation.

[^302]: **from righteous one below, female emits water toward male…** Righteous human action stimulates *Shekhinah*’s passion for Her partner, *Tif’eret*. On female waters, see above, note 215.

[^303]: **Just as male above dwells between two females…** Just as *Tif’eret* dwells between *Binah* and *Shekhinah*, so *Shekhinah* dwells between *Yesod* and the righteous human being. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]]–50a, [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-66b|66b]].

[^304]: **because he guarded the sign of the covenant** By withstanding the sexual advances of Potiphar’s wife, he preserved the sign of circumcision and attained the rung of *Yesod*, the divine phallus. See above, note 300.

[^305]: **he did not sin with this sign of the covenant** He committed no sexual sin.

[^306]: **If so, why he is called Righteous?** If he did not undergo the trial of temptation, how did he deserve this title?

[^307]: **the whole time Jacob was mourning over Joseph, he did not perform…** After Joseph disappeared and Jacob went into mourning, Benjamin abstained from sexual relations.

[^308]: **just a child, unmarried; yet you say…** At that time, Benjamin was a young boy, so his lack of sexual activity is not remarkable.

[^309]: **We have learned as follows: When Joseph asked Benjamin…** The midrash recorded in *Bereshit Rabbah* 94:8 reads differently: “*The sons of Benjamin: Bela, Becher* \[*and Ashbel, Gera and Na’aman, Ehi and Rosh, Muppim, Huppim, and Ard*\]. When Joseph stood with Benjamin he asked him, ‘Do you have sons?’ He replied, ‘Yes.’ He asked him, ‘How many?’ He replied, ‘Ten.’…” The names of each of Benjamin’s ten sons are then linked with Joseph’s life and character.

    See ibid. 93:7, in the name of Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥman: “He asked him, ‘Do you have a wife?’ He replied, ‘I have a wife and ten sons.’…” Cf. BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 36#36b|36b]].

    Here Rabbi El’azar offers a different version in support of his view that Benjamin had not consummated his marriage. The text is in disarray. I follow the reading of MSS R1 and M7; see *KP*. For various readings and interpretations, see the standard editions, variants, and *OY*, Galante, *MM, MmD, Zohorei Ya’bets*.

[^310]: **Now, if you say, *‘The sons of Benjamin: Bela, Becher*…’** The verse continues: *and Ashbel, Gera and Na’aman, Ehi and Rosh, Muppim, Huppim, and Ard*. If Benjamin spoke this verse as part of his reply to Joseph’s question, his sons must have already been born.

[^311]: **when they entered Egypt it was certainly so** Benjamin is described as having ten sons only later, when Jacob’s entire clan came to Egypt. See [[Torah 11 - Vayigash#^46-6|Genesis 46:6–27]].

[^312]: **later he did, engendering children** Several commentators wrestle with the problem of how Benjamin could have sired ten sons in the short interval between his reunion with Joseph and the clan’s arrival in Egypt.

[^313]: **Joseph constituted the covenantal sign of my father…** Joseph symbolizes *Yesod*, the divine phallus, which is the site of the covenant of circumcision and consummation of the male sefirotic body. His father Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, the torso of the body. See above, page 333.

[^314]: **Since he has vanished, I will guard my brother’s site** Since Joseph has disappeared, I will guard the covenant, symbolizing *Yesod*, by refraining from sexual activity. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-155b|1:155b]].

[^315]: **when he vanished he was not righteous…** When Joseph was kidnapped by his brothers and sold into slavery, he was not yet righteous since he had not yet withstood the attempted seduction by Potiphar’s wife.

[^316]: **they all knew from Jacob, since Jacob knew…** All the tribes had learned from Jacob that Joseph had inherited the rung of *Yesod*.

[^317]: ***When Rachel had given birth to Joseph, Jacob said*…** The birth of Joseph, symbolizing *Yesod*, indicated the consummation of the divine body, so now Jacob, symbolizing the torso, could return to the Holy Land. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-150b|1:150b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158a|158a]].

[^318]: **Once Joseph was discovered** Once his identity was revealed to his brothers. See [[Torah 11 - Vayigash#^45-1|Genesis 45]].

[^319]: **Jacob didn’t perceive them since they belonged to Jubilee** Jacob was not aware that for those seven years he was serving Laban for Leah, since Her realm, *Binah* (Jubilee), is concealed.

[^320]: **while those belonging to Sabbatical were revealed** During the second set of seven years, Jacob was fully aware that he was serving Laban for Rachel, who symbolizes *Shekhinah* (Sabbatical).

[^321]: **For Sabbatical, revealed, he served Jubilee, concealed** Though Jacob thought he was serving Laban for Rachel, symbolizing Sabbatical, he was really serving for Leah, symbolizing Jubilee.

[^322]: ***seven years*, unidentified: *for Rachel* he served…** Although the verse obviously refers to the second set of seven years rather than the first, this is not specified. Rabbi El’azar takes advantage of this lack of specificity and concludes that the text here refers to the first seven years, symbolizing the *sefirot* hidden within *Binah*, so Jacob’s service spanned both realms: Rachel (his intended focus) and Leah (the actuality), Sabbatical and Jubilee, *Shekhinah* and *Binah*.

    For other interpretations, see *OY, MmD*.

[^323]: ***Seven years* appear, but where are *seven times*?** If the first set of years derives from Jubilee, why are those years not described as seven times seven?

[^324]: **those seven days on which he celebrated his wedding with Leah…** See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-22|Genesis 29:22–28]]. The seven days turned the seven years into seven times seven.

[^325]: **But with Rachel…he didn’t celebrate seven days…** Concerning her, no seven-day wedding feast is mentioned, just the seven additional years of service, since she symbolizes Sabbatical, not Jubilee. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-30|Genesis 29:30]].

[^326]: **he should have served the years of Sabbatical first…** As he did with Leah.

[^327]: **Of that site is written…** Of *Shekhinah*, known as *righteousness*.

[^328]: **But as to the four sons of the maids…** Bilhah and Zilpah, the maids of Rachel and Leah, gave birth to Dan, Naphtali, Gad, and Asher. What role do these four sons play in the sefirotic realm? See *Zohar* [[#^z1-155a|1:155a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158a|158a]].

[^329]: ***their hinder parts all inward*** From the description of the large bronze reservoir built by Solomon in the Temple, which rested on twelve bronze oxen, three facing outward in each direction, with their hindquarters toward the center.

[^330]: **there are three joints in the right arm, but the one in the middle…** The shoulder, the wrist, and—sticking out in the middle—the elbow. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-241a|1:241a]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244a|2:244a–b]].

[^331]: **one in the right leg, one in the left leg** The knee, between the hip and ankle. The arms and legs symbolize *Ḥesed, Gevurah, Netsaḥ*, and *Hod*; the elbows and knees protrude.

[^332]: **When all is aligned** When a person stands erect, with arms hanging straight down.

[^333]: **Each differs from the other, so all is arrayed as one** The unique function of every limb and joint contributes to the unified organism, both human and divine.

[^334]: ***aqeret ha-bayit*… (*iqqara de-veita*), essence of the house** The word עקרת (*aqeret*), *barren woman*, is reinterpreted as עיקר (*iqqar*), “essence.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 20:2; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 15; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 14:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|50a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149b|149b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157b|157b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]].

[^335]: **Sabbatical, essence of this world, by whom it is conducted** Rachel symbolizes *Shekhinah*, also known as Sabbatical. *Shekhinah* conducts the lower world. See above, pages 355–56.

[^336]: ***As a joyous mother of children*—Jubilee…** Leah symbolizes *Binah*, the Divine Mother, also known as Jubilee. See above, pages 355–56.

[^337]: **This verse is totality of all… *Hallelujah!*** The verse spans the higher and lower *sefirot*, from *Binah* to *Shekhinah*. Its concluding exclamation, הללו־יה (*Halelu-Yah*), *Hallelujah*, “Praise *Yah*,” alludes both to *Shekhinah*, known as Praise, and to *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*, symbolized by the divine name יה (*Yah*). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|1:178b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-232b|232b]].

[^338]: **children of a hated woman are unvirtuous** According to Rabbi Levi (in BT *Nedarim* [[Nedarim 20#20b|20b]]), children of a hated wife turn out to be rebellious sinners.

[^339]: **But truly…all its actions were concealed from Jacob** *Binah*, symbolized by Leah, is a concealed realm. Leah was not actually *hated* by Jacob, but rather offensively strange to him, as suggested by Jacob’s startled reaction the morning after he slept with her ([[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-25|Genesis 29:25]]): *In the morning, here, she was Leah!* See above, page 354.

[^340]: **Lower world is revealed, beginning of all, to climb its rungs** *Shekhinah* is the gateway to the divine world, the first rung of the sefirotic ladder. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7b|1:7b]].

[^341]: **As supernal Wisdom is beginning of all…** *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”) is the primordial point of emanation, while *Shekhinah* is lower Wisdom, beginning of the spiritual ascent.

[^342]: **So we call it אתה (*Attah*), You…** The second-person pronoun indicates *Shekhinah*, who, being revealed, can be addressed directly. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-37a|37a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-138b|2:138b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-261a|261a]] (*Heikh*).

[^343]: **higher world, Jubilee, we call הוא (*Hu*), He…** The third-person pronoun indicates *Binah*, who, being concealed, can be referred to only indirectly. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-158b|1:158b]].

[^344]: ***He lay with her in the night*, הוא (*Hu*), *He*** This description of Jacob’s union with Leah is usually understood: *He lay with her that night*. However, the wording הוא‎ בלילה (*ba-lailah hu*), *in that night*, is strange; one would expect ההוא‎ בלילה (*ba-lailah ha-hu*). Rabbi El’azar reads the phrase hyperliterally: *in the night, Hu, He*. This pronoun refers to *Binah*, who is symbolized by Leah and facilitated the union.

    See BT *Niddah* [[Niddah 31#31a|31a]]; Rashi and *Sekhel Tov* on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-16|Genesis 30:16]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-157b|1:157b]] (*ST*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 98; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-16|Genesis 30:16]], n. 60. Cf. Philo, *On the Cherubim*, 42–46.

[^345]: ***The Levite will serve Hu, He*, drawing from Him blessings for all** *The Levite*, symbolizing *Gevurah*, draws blessing from *Hu, He*, symbolizing *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-221b|2:221b]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-171a|3:171a]], [[Zohar 9.06 - Parashat Qorah#^z3-178a|178a–b]].

[^346]: ***He cleaves to his wife*** Jacob, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, cleaved to Rachel (symbolizing the Divine Wife, *Shekhinah*) rather than to Leah (symbolizing the Divine Mother, *Binah*). The full verse reads: *Therefore a man leaves his father and mother and cleaves to his wife, and they become one flesh*.

[^347]: ***That Leah was hated*…a man hates incest with his mother…** Since Leah symbolized the Divine Mother, Jacob, who symbolized the Divine Son (*Tif’eret*), was loath to sleep with her after he discovered who she was.

    According to M *Qiddushin* 4:12, a man is permitted to remain alone with his mother since there is no danger of sexual relations between them.

[^348]: **The world exists only for the sake of Jacob** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 36:4: “Rabbi Berekhiah said, ‘Heaven and earth were created only for the sake of Jacob.’” See above, page 324.

[^349]: **For the sake of Abraham…** According to Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Korḥah (*Bereshit Rabbah* 12:9), the word בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*, is an anagram of באברהם (*be-Avraham*), “through Abraham,” indicating that *heaven and earth* were created for his sake.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105b|105b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128b|128b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|230b]]; [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-117a|3:117a]].

[^350]: ***Thus says YHVH to the house of Jacob, who redeemed Abraham*** The verse is usually understood as: *Thus says YHVH, who redeemed Abraham, to the house of Jacob*. However Rabbi El’azar reads it hyperliterally, based on the midrashic tradition that Jacob’s merit *redeemed Abraham* from Nimrod’s fiery furnace.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 63:2; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 36:4; BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 19#19b|19b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-57a|3:57a]].

[^351]: **the blessed Holy One built worlds and destroyed them** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 9:2: “Rabbi Abbahu said, ‘… The blessed Holy One kept creating worlds and destroying them until He created these \[i.e., heaven and earth\]. Then He declared, “These please Me, those do not.”’”

    The theme of previous worlds that did not endure appears in the *Idrot* (*Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-128a|3:128a]] \[*IR*\], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]] \[*IZ*\]) and inspired Isaac Luria’s theory of “the breaking of the vessels.”

[^352]: **upon him worlds were founded… *your Creator, Jacob*…** Normally, the verse is read: *Thus says YHVH, your Creator, O Jacob; your Former, O Israel!* Here, following midrashic tradition, Rabbi El’azar understands it to mean that Jacob himself functions as the Creator. See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 36:4.

    Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who balances the polar extremes: *Ḥesed* on the right and *Gevurah* on the left. This mediating quality ensures the stability of the world. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15.

[^353]: **Israel is called ‘son of the blessed Holy One’…** The people of Israel, descended from Jacob, who is called Israel, share in the designation “son” and cleave to *Tif’eret*, who is the blessed Holy One and the Divine Son. See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 1:4.

[^354]: **ראובן (*Re’uven*)… בן‎ ראו (*Re’u ven*), *See, a son!*—nameless…** Drawing on the simplest etymology, Rabbi El’azar suggests that Leah did not actually name her son, but simply said: בן‎ ראו (*Re’u ven*), *See, a son!* His individual identity merged with that of his soon-to-be-born brothers, Simeon and Levi, the three of them together symbolizing the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:3, and Theodor, ad loc.; *Midrash ha-Gadol*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-32|Genesis 29:32]]; *Zohar* [[#^z1-155a|1:155a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-176b|176b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-222b|222b]].

[^355]: **Why לוי (*Levi*), Levi?…ולויות (*ve-loyot*), *with his companion*…** The verse from Kings describes the engravings of cherubs, lions, and palm trees on the laver stands built by Solomon for the Temple. These figures were engraved *according to the space between each one*, ולויות (*ve-loyot*), *with wreaths*. Here, drawing on Talmudic tradition, Rabbi El’azar derives *loyot* from the root לוה (*lvh*), “to be joined, accompany.” See BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 54#54a|54a]]–b; and [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-34|Genesis 29:34]], cited below.

    Usually in the *Zohar*, Levi symbolizes *Gevurah* (see above, note 345). Here, however, he symbolizes *Tif’eret*, “juncture” of right and left—*Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*—symbolized respectively by Reuben and Simeon.

[^356]: **From here…*surpassing*…according to its translation…** Rabbi Yehudah agrees that Reuben merged with Simeon and Levi, but he derives this from the blessing bestowed by Jacob on his firstborn: *Reuben, you are my firstborn, my strength and first fruit of my vigor! Surpassing in dignity, surpassing in power!* The Aramaic rendering of this verse in *Targum Onqelos* concludes: “You are worthy of obtaining three portions: birthright, priesthood, and kingdom,” which according to Rabbi Yehudah alludes to *Tif’eret* (the son, or firstborn), *Ḥesed* (symbolized by the priest), and *Gevurah* (source of *Malkhut*, “Kingdom”).

[^357]: **Leah thought so…** Leah thought that her first son was part of the triad, as indicated by her reference to “joining” when she named her third son, Levi.

[^358]: **supernal chariot comprises the patriarchs…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:6: “Resh Lakish said, ‘The patriarchs themselves constitute the \[divine\] Chariot.’”

    In Kabbalah the three patriarchs (Abraham on the right, Isaac on the left, and Jacob between them) symbolize the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, which serves as a chariot for the three highest *sefirot*. See Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 57; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|1:60b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|99a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|2:144a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-38a|3:38a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-99a|99a]].

    Here King David, symbolizing *Shekhinah*, completes the Chariot, serving as the fourth leg of the divine throne. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20a|20a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82a|82a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-89b|89b]] (*ST*).

    On the relation between David and the patriarchs, see [[Acts 02#^2-29|Acts 2:29]]; *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 16#16b|16b]]; *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 117#117b|117b]]; *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 107#107a|107a]]; *Midrash Tehillim* 18:8, 25; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 45 (54); Jacob ben Sheshet, *Ha-Emunah ve-ha-Bittaḥon*, 396; Baḥya ben Asher on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^32-10|Genesis 32:10]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 6:265, n. 94. Cf. above, p. 23, n. 164.

[^359]: **supernal chariot, mystery of the holy name** יהוה (*YHVH*). *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret* are symbolized respectively by the first three letters, while the final ה (*he*) designates *Shekhinah*.

[^360]: **subsequently Judah, who inherited kingdom** While Leah’s first three sons symbolize the triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, her fourth son, Judah—progenitor of the Davidic dynasty—symbolizes *Shekhinah*, or *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”).

[^361]: ***This time I will praise YHVH*…** The verse records Leah’s reaction at the birth of her fourth son: ‘*This time* אודה (*odeh*), *I will praise, YHVH.’ So she named him* יהודה (*Yehudah*), *Judah. Then she stopped giving birth*.

[^362]: **as long as Assembly of Israel is in exile…** The name “Assembly of Israel” refers to both the people of Israel and their divine counterpart, *Shekhinah* (see above, note 15). *Shekhinah* follows Israel into exile, and without Her ה (*he*), the divine name יהוה (*YHVH*) is incomplete. See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 3:16.

[^363]: **Why *va-ta’amod, she stood*?** The literal meaning of the word. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-186a|1:186a]].

[^364]: **until here She stands in unity…** The *sefirot* are a realm of unity; below Shekhinah begins the realm of multiplicity and division. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-241a|1:241a]].

[^365]: **Similarly with those two sons she bore afterward** It would seem that Issachar and Zebulun, born later, inhabited the World of Division.

[^366]: **six directions of the world are as one** The term “six directions” derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13, where it refers to: above, below, north, south, east, and west. Here it designates *Binah*’s six sons, the six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*, symbolized by Leah’s six sons, including Issachar and Zebulun, who correspond specifically to *Netsaḥ* and *Hod*. See above, page 356.

[^367]: **black light** *Shekhinah*, who has no light of Her own. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50b|1:50b]]–51b, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83b|83b]].

[^368]: **restoring the essence of all to its site** Restoring *Shekhinah*, the essence of the world (see above, page 361), to Her site of union with *Tif’eret*.

[^369]: **All those worlds assume the same pattern…** The twelve tribes below adorn *Shekhinah*, as their corresponding aspects above enthrone *Binah*.

[^370]: **Sons of the maids are four, conjoined with these…** The sons of the maids are Dan, Naftali, Gad, and Asher. On their sefirotic role, see above, page 360.

[^371]: **everything engendered by the lower world is division…** Once the stream of emanation flows from the highest *sefirot* through *Shekhinah* (“the lower world” or “garden”), it divides into the world of multiplicity, as indicated by the verse in Genesis: *A river issues from Eden to water the garden, and from there it divides and becomes four riverheads*.

[^372]: **So what do you say about Joseph and Benjamin?…** These two were brought forth by Rachel, who symbolizes *Shekhinah*, source of the World of Division. How can they share in the unity of Jacob’s other ten sons?

[^373]: **Rabbi Abba came and kissed him** For posing such a challenging question. See above, page 360.

[^374]: **higher world is arrayed in twelve of its own** *Binah* is arrayed in twelve aspects corresponding to the twelve sons, all of whom pertain to Her.

[^375]: **Righteous One perpetually emerges and enters…** There are two aspects of *Yesod*, known as Righteous One. From above, *Yesod*, the divine phallus, unites with *Shekhinah*, “the lower world,” entering Her; yet this union of the divine couple is stimulated by the righteous human being on earth who emerges from *Shekhinah*. Both aspects of Righteous One focus continually on the site of *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-153b|1:153b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245b|245b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-211b|2:211b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-259a|259a]] (*Heikh*).

[^376]: ***As her soul departed—for she was dying*…** The verse describes Rachel giving birth to Benjamin: *As her soul departed—for she was dying—she named him Ben Oni; but his father called him Benjamin*.

[^377]: **entering in the mystery of Joseph…** The two aspects of *Yesod* correspond to Joseph and Benjamin. See above, note 300.

[^378]: **thinking she had given birth below in the World of Division…** Rachel thought that Benjamin belonged below and would play no role in the unified realm of Jacob’s other sons.

[^379]: **ימין‎ בן (*Ben Yamin*), Son of the Right, for he ascended…** The right symbolizes the higher realm of unity. On Benjamin filling Joseph’s place, see above, pages 357–59.

[^380]: **The verse should read: *with all my* לב (*lev*), *heart*** Why the unusual form לבב (*levav*), with a double ב (*bet*)*?*

[^381]: ***with all my levav*… two sides…** The double ב (*bet*) symbolizes the two impulses, associated with the right and left sides of the *sefirot*, which are characterized by *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*. The totality of the *sefirot* expresses the divine name.

    See M *Berakhot* 9:5: “*With all* לבבך (*levavekha*), *your heart* ([[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-5|Deuteronomy 6:5]])—with both your impulses: your good impulse and your evil impulse.”

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 61#61a|61a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144b|144b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-165b|165b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-174b|174b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]]; [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-263b|3:263b]].

[^382]: **other directions of this world** Symbolizing the other four *sefirot*, which complete the “six directions” of *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*. See above, note 366.

[^383]: **south and north** Symbolizing *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^384]: **ועדה (*ve-edah*), *and the assembly*—site of… Judah…** Judah, progenitor of the Davidic dynasty, symbolizes *Shekhinah*, or *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”), who is also known as *Keneset Yisra’el*, “Assembly of Israel.” Here Rabbi Shim’on plays with the alliteration: ועדה (*ve-edah*), יהודה (*Yehudah*), ועדותי (*ve-edoti*), עוד (*od*). The verse from [[Psalms 132#^132-1|Psalm 132]] refers to the Davidic line.

[^385]: **Yet…*with all* לבי (*libbi*), *my heart; before Elohim*… a single site…** The spelling לבי (*libbi*), with a single ב (*bet*), indicates David’s focus on the right side in chanting this verse. Since *Shekhinah*, who is known as *Elohim*, derives from the left, he sought to draw Her to the right.

[^386]: **embracing south…east** Judah, symbolizing *Shekhinah* (in the west), joins with *Ḥesed* and *Tif’eret* (south and east).

[^387]: **from the left side, originating from the north** From the side of *Gevurah*, symbolized by north.

[^388]: **body** *Tif’eret*, torso of the divine body.

[^389]: **So, *This time I will praise YHVH*** This is why Leah praised *YHVH* when Judah was born. See above, pages 364–65.

[^390]: **ותעמוד (**Va-ta’amod**), *She stopped… She stood*…** Now the divine throne was stable and complete. See above, page 365.

[^391]: **went out to the villages** For this phrase, see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 18#18b|18b]], *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 3#3b|3b]], *Beitsah* 9b, *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 105#105a|105a]], *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 98#98b|98b]].

[^392]: **Innovations of Torah are required here!** Their presence together demands that they engage in Torah. See M *Avot* 3:6: “Rabbi Ḥalafta of the village of Ḥananya says, ‘If ten are sitting engaged in Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells among them….even five…even three…even two…even one.’” Here Rabbi Shim’on calls for creativity.

[^393]: **They sat for three days** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-240b|1:240b]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-205a|3:205a]].

[^394]: **How far can a human see? Three parasangs, or four or five** The Greek parasang equals about 3.5 miles. See BT *Bekhorot* [[Bekhorot 54#54b|54b]]: “The eye of a shepherd can exercise control for a distance of sixteen mils \[about nine miles\].” See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-18|Genesis 15:18]].

[^395]: **the blessed Holy One lifted the land of Israel…** Therefore God told Abraham: “*Raise your eyes now and look*….” According to rabbinic tradition, the land of Israel stands at the center of the world. See *Tanḥuma, Qedoshim* 10: “The land of Israel sits in the center of the world, Jerusalem in the center of the land of Israel, the Temple in the center of Jerusalem, the nave in the center of the Temple, the ark in the center of the nave, and in front of the ark the Rock of Foundation, from which the world was founded.”

[^396]: **whoever sees Rabbi Shim’on sees the whole world…** On the mythic stature of Rabbi Shim’on, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 35:2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-79a|3:79a]]; Liebes, *Studies in the Zohar*, 28–30.

[^397]: ***The land on which you lie I will give to you and to your seed*** God’s promise to Jacob while he lay asleep dreaming.

[^398]: **it was no more than four cubits!** The length of ground occupied by Jacob as he slept was no more than four cubits (approximately six feet).

[^399]: **within those four cubits the blessed Holy One rolled up…** See BT *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 91#91b|91b]]: “*The land upon which you lie*. What is so great about that? \[The actual area of land occupied by Jacob’s body was miniscule.\] Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘This teaches that the blessed Holy One rolled up the whole land of Israel and placed it beneath our father Jacob, so that it would be easily conquered by his descendants.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 69:4; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72a|1:72a]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-84a|3:84a]].

[^400]: **Rabbi Shim’on, Lamp of the whole world!** Enlightening the world. On the title “Lamp,” see [[II Samuel 21#^21-17|2 Samuel 21:17]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:4; BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 17#17a|17a]], where Rabbi Abbahu is called: “Lamp of Light”; and *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 28#28b|28b]], where Rabban Yoḥanan son of Zakkai is called “Lamp of Israel.” On Rabbi Shim’on as “Lamp,” see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]]–4a, [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-171a|3:171a]]; *ZḤ* 85d (*MhN, Rut*).

[^401]: ***This time I will praise YHVH*** Leah’s exclamation upon giving birth to Judah. See above, pages 364–65, 367–68.

[^402]: **Judah is the fourth son of the Throne, consummating the Throne** Leah’s fourth son, Judah, progenitor of the Davidic dynasty, symbolizes *Shekhinah*, or *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). Together with Reuben, Simeon, and Levi (who symbolize *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*), Judah embodies the divine throne.

[^403]: **This verse has been established in numerous places** The verse is cited often in rabbinic and kabbalistic literature. See BT *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 127#127a|127a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-134b|1:134b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-42b|3:42b–43a]].

[^404]: **camps** Of angels.

[^405]: **all simultaneously** See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 7.

[^406]: **like a hammer scattering sparks within sparks…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|3:292b]] (*IZ*).

[^407]: ***By the word of YHVH*—Speech; *by the breath of His mouth*—Spirit** Speech symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who conveys the divine word. Spirit symbolizes *Tif’eret*, a more ethereal realm.

[^408]: **One does not proceed without the other** As speech is impossible without breath, so *Shekhinah* manifests only through the power of *Tif’eret*.

[^409]: **single concealed light, from which radiate all those revealed lights** *Binah*, from whom emanate all the lower *sefirot*.

[^410]: **higher world** Extending from *Binah* through *Yesod*.

[^411]: **fashioning an artisan, a light that does not shine…** *Shekhinah*, who has no light of Her own. She is the artisan who fashions the lower world. Alternatively, *Shekhinah* Herself is the lower world, emanating from *Binah*. The phrase here can also be rendered: “The artisan fashioned a light that does not shine,” i.e., *Binah* fashioned *Shekhinah*.

    On the connotations of “artisan,” see *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:1; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-22a|1:22a]] (*TZ*), [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47b|47b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-167b|2:167b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-219b|3:219b]].

[^412]: **by joining below, it joins illuminatingly the juncture above** By uniting below, *Shekhinah* is stimulated to unite above with Her partner, *Tif’eret*, who illumines Her.

[^413]: **as is written:…*In wisdom*…** By means of *Shekhinah*, the artisan, known as Lower Wisdom.

[^414]: **Everything on earth corresponds above** A general principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-38a|1:38a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57b|57b–58a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-129a|129a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-145b|145b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-205b|205b]]; [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-15b|2:15b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-20a|20a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-48b|48b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|144a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-251a|251a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-45b|3:45b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-65b|65b]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:273.

[^415]: **Even the tiniest thing in this world depends upon another…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 10:6: “Rabbi Simon said, ‘You cannot find a single blade of grass \[below (per Oxford MS 147)\] that does not have a constellation in the sky, striking it and telling it: “Grow!”’”

[^416]: **when this one below is aroused…** A related principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-235a|235a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-40b|3:40b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]].

[^417]: ***Give me some of your son’s mandrakes!*** Rachel asked Leah for these because of their supposed aphrodisiac power.

[^418]: **Issachar… embracing Torah…** See [[I Chronicles 12#^12-33|1 Chronicles 12:33]]; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 354; *Bereshit Rabbah* 72:5.

[^419]: ***Is your taking my husband a small thing?*…** The dialogue between Leah and Rachel runs as follows: *She* \[Leah\] *said to her, “Is your taking my husband a small thing, that you would take my son’s mandrakes too?” Rachel replied, “Then he may lie with you tonight in return for your son’s mandrakes*.”

[^420]: **הוא (*Hu*), *He—Hu*, precisely…the supernal world…** This description of Jacob’s union with Leah is usually understood: *He lay with her that night*. However, the wording הוא‎ בלילה (*ba-lailah hu*), *in that night*, is strange; one would expect ההוא‎ בלילה (*ba-lailah ha-hu*). Rabbi Yitsḥak reads the phrase hyperliterally: *in the night, Hu, He*. This pronoun refers to *Binah*, who is symbolized by Leah and facilitated the union that engendered Issachar. See above, note 344.

[^421]: **Torah emerged from the supernal world** Torah, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, issued from *Binah*.

[^422]: **Issachar was linked to it** To Torah. As Torah issued from *Binah*, so Issachar issued from Leah.

[^423]: **Tree of Life** Torah is identified with the Tree of Life, based on the description of wisdom in [[Proverbs 03#^3-18|Proverbs 3:18]]: *She is a tree of life to those who grasp her*. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 32#32b|32b]], 61b. Here the name applies as well to *Tif’eret*, trunk of the sefirotic tree.

[^424]: **אתה (*Attah*), You** The second-person pronoun indicates *Shekhinah*, who, being more revealed, can be addressed directly. See above, pages 361–62.

[^425]: **children depend on the flux of destiny** See BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 28a: “Rava said, ‘Life, children, and sustenance do not depend on merit but on *mazzala*, destiny.’”

    מזלא (*Mazzala*) means “constellation, planet, planetary influence, destiny, guardian angel.” In the *Zohar* it is associated with the root נזל (*nzl*), “to flow,” and often refers to the flow of emanation from the World of the Male, which comprises *Binah* through *Yesod*, the divine phallus. *Yesod* conveys this flow to *Shekhinah*, thereby engendering souls of the newborn.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-43b|1:43b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-137a|137a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-159b|159b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-181a|181a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207b|207b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-6a|2:6a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|252b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25b|3:25b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-289a|289a]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-295b|295b]] (*IZ*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 193; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 65 (82); Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Orah*, 12a, 37a–b, 74a, 95a.

[^426]: **in this matter** Conception.

[^427]: ***for I have surely hired you*** The verse continues: *with my son’s mandrakes*.

[^428]: **This statement is impudent, so it seems, but not really so** Leah’s boldness is praised in BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 100#100b|100b]]. See *Nedarim* [[Nedarim 20#20b|20b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 72:5; Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 40. Cf. JT *Sanhedrin* 2:5, 20d; *Bereshit Rabbah* 80:1.

[^429]: **Holy Spirit roused within her** On the prophetic power of the matriarchs, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 67:9; 72:6; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-9|Genesis 30:9]]. The wording here recalls the description of Tamar in *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:9: “Holy Spirit נצנצה (*nitsnetsah*), sparkled, within her.”

[^430]: **assigning them names in the mystery of wisdom** Through inspiration, Leah named her children according to their corresponding *sefirot*.

[^431]: **let us engage in Torah, so the blessed Holy One will couple with us** On the importance of engaging in Torah while on a journey, see [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-7|Deuteronomy 6:7]]; BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 54#54a|54a]]; *Ta’anit* 10b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|69b–70a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|115b]]. Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]]–50a, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a–b]].

    See M *Avot* 3:2: “Rabbi Ḥanina son of Teradyon says, ‘… If two are sitting engaged in Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells between them.’”

[^432]: ***you shall eat unleavened bread*** The verse continues: *until the twenty-first day of the month in the evening*.

[^433]: ***Seven days you shall eat it*…** The Passover sacrifice.

[^434]: **The Companions have already been aroused by this word** The spelling is discussed in BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 36#36a|36a]]–b, 115b–116a. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|1:33a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-40a|2:40a–b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 112,133.

[^435]: **under alien authority** Under demonic power.

[^436]: **Who is *impoverished*? King David…** Symbolizing *Shekhinah*, also known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). She is *impoverished* because She has no light of Her own, just what is provided Her by the higher *sefirot*. See *Bahir* 19 (27).

[^437]: **female without male is poor** *Shekhinah* is impoverished without the rich flow of emanation from Her partner, *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-40b|2:40b]].

[^438]: **First they approached *matstsah*** *Shekhinah,* opening of the divine realm.

[^439]: **other rungs** Higher *sefirot*.

[^440]: **מצה (*matstsah*)…מצוה (*mitsvah*)… supplemented with ו (*vav*)** The letter ו (*vav*), whose numerical value is six, symbolizes *Tif’eret*, surrounded by the five other *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*. When this masculine ו (*vav*) was added to מצה (*matstsah*), symbolizing *Shekhinah*, She was transformed into מצוה (*mitsvah*). See Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 133; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-251b|3:251b]] (*RM*).

[^441]: ***For* הזאת‎ המצוה (*ha-mitsvah ha-zot*), *this commandment*** *Shekhinah,* the Divine Presence, is known as זאת (*zot*), *this*. Here this name is linked to the name מצוה (*mitsvah*), indicating Her state of fullfilment.

[^442]: **Armed band of cutthroats** דקטנון‎ טופסרא (*Tufsera de-qatnun*). The first of a pair of enigmatic phrases. In the *Zohar, tufsera* can mean “form, mold, configuration,” based on טופסא (*tufsa*), derived from the Greek *tupos*, but here it seems to derive more naturally from the biblical term טפסר (*tifsar*), “marshal, commander, scribe.” See [[Jeremiah 51#^51-27|Jeremiah 51:27]], where *tifsar* is rendered by *Targum Yonatan* as “warriors” and understood by Rashi as “army.”

    See [[Nahum 3#^3-17|Nahum 3:17]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20b|1:20b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30b|30b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96b|96b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-241b|241b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|242b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-243b|243b]]; [[Zohar 11.08 - Tosefta#^z2-235b|2:235b]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-120a|3:120a]], [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-270b|270b]]; *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 178–80.

    *Qatnun* may derive from the term קטנטין (*qetantin*) in *Midrash Tehillim* 54:2. There, according to Saul Lieberman (in *JQR* 35 \[1944–45\]: 35), it probably refers to a Roman officer, a *centenarius* (“commander of a hundred”), but the medieval dictionary *Arukh* defines the word as “murderer,” based on the Greek *ktantes*.

[^443]: **unroofed fortress** דקיטרא‎ קוסטרא (*Qustera de-qitra*). *Qustera* derives from the Latin *castrum* (pl. *castra*), “fortified place, fortress, castle.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|30a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85a|85a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128a|128a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-148b|148b]].

    The root *qtr* usually means “smoke” or “knot” in the *Zohar*, but here *qitra* may derive from the rare word קטרות (*qeturot*) in [[Ezekiel 46#^46-22|Ezekiel 46:22]], as understood in M *Middot* 2:5: “unroofed.”

    On one level, the voice is cryptically warning the two rabbis of an impending ambush. Yet “veer above” implies as well the imperative of spiritual ascent. The apparent security of this world is illusory: an unroofed fortress. For various interpretations of the message, see *OY*, Galante, *KP, Sullam*.

[^444]: ***Arqa*** One of the seven levels of earth, corresponding to the seven heavens. See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 29:11; *Sefer Yetsirah* 4:12; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-9b|1:9b]], [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-39b|39b–40a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54b|54b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-9b|3:9b–10a]]; *ZḤ* 8c–9b [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-87b|87b]] (*MhN, Rut*).

    Cain was banished to *Arqa from the face of the earth* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^4-14|Genesis 4:14]]) and generated demonic offspring. See El’azar of Worms, *Ḥokhmat ha-Nefesh*, 26c; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-36b|1:36b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|54a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178a|178a–b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-76b|3:76b]], [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-122a|122a]].

[^445]: **Some of them look different, stranger than me** According to one tradition, descendants of Cain have two heads. See *Beit ha-Midrash*, 4:151–52; Judah ben Barzillai, *Peirush Sefer Yetsirah*, 173; *Tosafot, Menaḥot* 37a, s.v. *o qum gelei; Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-9b|1:9b]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-80a|2:80a]]; *ZḤ* 9b; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:143, n. 34; Ta-Shma, *Ha-Nigleh she-ba-Nistar*, 125, n. 84.

[^446]: **ארץ (*Erets*), Earth** Highest of the seven levels.

[^447]: **החיים‎ ארץ (*Erets ha-Ḥayyim*), Land of Life** *Shekhinah*, who is identified with *earth*. On Her name “Land of Life” (or “Land of the Living”), see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]]–66a, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95b|95b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-124b|124b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-143b|143b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 62 (77).

[^448]: **seven species** According to [[Torah 46 - Eikev#^8-8|Deuteronomy 8:8]], the land of Israel is blessed with seven species, but only two of these are grains. Rabbinic literature refers to five species of grain from which bread is normally produced and from which *matstsah* must be made. See M *Pesaḥim* 2:5; *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 8; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 35#35b|35b]]; and Jehuda Feliks, in *Encyclopaedia Judaica*, 6:1332–33. In fact, the version of this *Zohar* passage translated in Al-Nakawa’s *Menorat ha-Ma’or*, 2:233–34, mentions five and not seven species.

[^449]: **The blessed Holy One gave Israel this bread from the Land of Life** God gave f them *matstsah*, symbolizing *Shekhinah*. See above, page 373.

[^450]: **bread from heaven** As Rabbi Ḥiyya goes on to explain.

[^451]: **he knows nothing until he tastes bread** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 40#40a|40a]]: “Rabbi Yehudah said, ‘… A baby does not know how to call out “Daddy!” or “Mommy!” until he has tasted grain.’” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 15:7.

[^452]: **this *earth*** *Shekhinah*.

[^453]: **realm befitting this *earth*** The realm of *Tif’eret*, fitting partner of *Shekhinah.*

[^454]: **Heaven…*I am going to rain bread from heaven for you*** The manna from heaven provided for Israel in the desert symbolizes the flow of emanation from *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246a|1:246a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-40a|2:40a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-61b|61b]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-183a|183a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-95b|3:95b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]] (*IZ*).

[^455]: **It was precisely for this that the blessed Holy One aroused us!** Meeting the man from *Arqa* stimulated them to explore the meaning of the verse from Job: *Earth, from which bread emerges*.

[^456]: **two Damascene plums, one male and one female** Damascene plums are a variety native to Syria, mentioned in rabbinic literature. Although plum trees are not dioecious (distinguished as male or female), certain varieties require pollen from a different variety.

    See *Tosefta, Bava Metsi’a* 7:4; JT *Shabbat* 1:7, 4a; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 39#39b|39b]]; *Bava Qamma* [[Bava Kamma 116#116a|116a]]–b. On the significance of the dioecian palm tree, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 40:1; *Midrash Tehillim* 92:11; BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 56#56a|56a]]; *Bahir* 117 (172), 139 (198), and Scholem’s note, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82a|1:82a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-126a|126a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-16a|3:16a]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-135b|135b]] (IR); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 128.

[^457]: **Every single thing in the world corresponds to the pattern above…** See above, note 414. Here Rabbi Yose emphasizes that everything in the world imitates the male and female principle above. See BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 74#74b|74b]]: “Rav Yehudah said in the name of Rav, ‘Everything that the blessed Holy One created in His world, He created male and female.’”

[^458]: **everything on earth has its counterpart in the sea** See BT *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 127#127a|127a]]: “Everything existing on land exists in the sea, except for the weasel.”

[^459]: **How did she know?** That Jacob was coming.

[^460]: **donkey brayed…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 99:10: “*Issachar* גָּרַם‎ חמור (*ḥamor garem*), *a strong-boned donkey*—גָּרֶם‎ חמור (*ḥamor garam*), a donkey caused, his birth. For how did Leah know that Jacob was coming? Only because the donkey brayed, and she heard its voice and went out to meet him.”

    See BT *Niddah* [[Niddah 31#31a|31a]]; *Midrash Aggadah*, [[Torah 12 - Vayechi#^49-14|Genesis 49:14]].

[^461]: **Why *my son’s mandrakes*?** Why did Leah specify this?

[^462]: **So Jacob would be pleased, for these facilitate birth** And Rachel, who was barren, might conceive.

[^463]: **Jacob knew that this matter depended not on mandrakes…** See above, pages 371–72.

[^464]: **Holy Spirit said this** It was not Jacob who spoke this verse, but rather Holy Spirit.

[^465]: ***aqeret ha-bayit*—lower world. *A joyous mother*…higher world…** The word עקרת (*aqeret*), *barren woman*, is reinterpreted as עיקר (*iqqar*), “essence.” Rachel symbolizes *Shekhinah*, known as “lower world” and “essence” of the world. Leah symbolizes *Binah*, “higher world.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 20:2; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 15; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 14:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|50a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149b|149b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154a|154a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]].

    The conclusion of the verse, הללו־יה (*Halelu-Yah*), *Hallelujah*, “Praise *Yah*,” alludes both to *Shekhinah*, known as Praise, and to *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*, symbolized by the divine name יה (*Yah*). See *Zohar* [[#^z1-154a|1:154a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-232b|232b]].

[^466]: **All these tribes are adornments below, all corresponding above** The twelve tribes below adorn *Shekhinah*, as their corresponding aspects above enthrone *Binah*. See above, page 365.

[^467]: **to obtain a body from him. Who is that? Torah** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who is the torso of the sefirotic body and is symbolized by Written Torah. By uniting with Jacob, Leah engendered Issachar, famous for his Torah learning. See above, note 418.

[^468]: **world that is coming and the inheritance of Jacob** “The world that is coming” alludes to *Binah*, who is constantly coming and flowing. “The inheritance of Jacob” alludes to *Tif’eret*.

[^469]: **שבר‎ יש‎ יששבר, (*Yissachar, yesh sakhar*), *Issachar*—there is reward** Dividing the name into two words. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-18|Genesis 30:18]]. The word יש (*yesh*) indicates the rich flow of emanation from *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6a|1:6a]].

    Several manuscripts cite instead [[Jeremiah 31#^31-16|Jeremiah 31:16]]: *For* שכר‎ יש (*yesh sakhar*), *there is a reward, for your labor*. See *Tanḥuma, Shemot 3; Sekhel Tov*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-18|Genesis 30:18]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|1:242b]].

[^470]: ***So I may endow those who love Me with* יש (*yesh*), *substance*…** According to rabbinic tradition, this verse describes the abundant reward of the righteous in the afterlife.

    See M *Avot* 5:19; *Uqtsin* 3:12; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana, nispaḥim, Vezot Haberakhah*, 451; BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 100#100a|100a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-4b|1:4b]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-206a|206a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|242b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-166b|2:166b]].

[^471]: ***For I have borne him six sons!*** When Leah gave birth to Zebulun, she exclaimed: *God has presented me with a precious gift. This time my husband will prize me, for I have borne him six sons!*

[^472]: **above, below, and four directions of the world** Leah’s six sons symbolize the six directions (up, down, and the four cardinal points), alluding to the six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, born to *Binah*. See above, note 366.

[^473]: **Whoever prolongs אחד (*eḥad*), *one*…** The first line of the *Shema* contains six words in Hebrew: אלהינו‎ יהוה‎ ישראל‎ שמע‎ אחד‎ יהוה (*Shema Yisra’el YHVH Eloheinu YHVH eḥad*), *Hear O Israel! YHVH is our God, YHVH is one* ([[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-4|Deuteronomy 6:4]]). Traditionally, one who prays prolongs the pronuncation of the final word, אחד (*eḥad*), *one*.

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 13#13b|13b]]: “Rabbi Yirmeyah was sitting before Rabbi Ḥiyya son of Abba, who saw that he was prolonging very much. He said to him, ‘Once you have enthroned Him above and below and in the four directions of heaven, you need do nothing more.’”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-12a|1:12a]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-216b|2:216b]]; *Shulḥan Arukh, Oraḥ Ḥayyim* 61:6; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:971–74.

[^474]: ***upon mountains of separation*** בתר‎ הרי‎ על (*Al harei vater*). The word vater is interpreted variously and is probably derived from the root meaning “to cut, divide,” thus denoting “cleft, jagged, craggy” mountains.

[^475]: **Six sons of Leah, embracing six others…** Leah symbolizes the Divine Mother, *Binah*. Her six sons (Reuben, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun) symbolize the six offspring of *Binah*, the *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*. This first sextet implies another: Jacob’s other six sons, born to Rachel, Bilhah, and Zilpah (Joseph, Benjamin, Dan, Naftali, Gad, and Asher). See above, pages 365, 377; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-241a|1:241a]].

[^476]: ***Do not take the mother along with the young*** When taking fledglings or eggs from a nest.

[^477]: **She is the concealed world—unrevealed; so *Let the mother go*…** The mother bird symbolizes the Divine Mother, *Binah*, who lies beyond comprehension and cannot be grasped. So let Her go, put Her out of your mind, do not even try to capture this subtle dimension of divinity. Rather, focus on *the young*, the lower *sefirot* engendered by *Binah*.

    See *Bahir* 74 (104–105); Naḥmanides and Recanati on [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^22-6|Deuteronomy 22:6]]; Todros Abulafia, *Otsar ha-Kavod, Berakhot* 33b; *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 142#142a|142a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-8a|2:8a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-93a|93a]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-254a|3:254a]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 338–39.

[^478]: ***primal days…from one end of heaven to the other*** The cosmic days of Creation from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, above and below *Tif’eret*, known as *heaven*. See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 11#11b|11b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-141b|1:141b]].

[^479]: **All these are called *mountains of spices*** Rabbi Ḥizkiyah is apparently playing on בשמים (*besamim*), *spices*, and בשמים (*ba-shamayim*), “in heaven.” See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 8:14.

[^480]: ***From there it divides*** The full verse reads: *A river issues from Eden to water the garden, and from there it divides and becomes four riverheads*. In Kabbalah this refers to the stream of emanation flowing from the highest sefirotic sources to the *garden* of *Shekhinah* and then to the worlds below.

[^481]: **mountains of division** The forces outside *Shekhinah*, not included in the unified realm of the *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-234a|2:234a–b]].

[^482]: **Sons of the maids joined joints—joints essential for arrayal** Bilhah and Zilpah, the maids of Rachel and Leah, gave birth to Dan, Naphtali, Gad, and Asher. On the sefirotic role played by these four sons, see above, page 360.

[^483]: **those joints protrude… From here on, all are one in a straight line** See above, page 360.

[^484]: **He saw…that Esau’s adversary had been born** Joseph represents the adversary of Esau, so once he was born, Jacob no longer feared returning to Canaan, Esau’s abode. See [[Obadiah 1#^1-18|Obadiah 1:18]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 73:7; BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 123#123b|123b]].

[^485]: **Joseph completed his realm with his site… Righteous One** Joseph completed Jacob’s realm, *Tif’eret*, by attaining his own site, the rung of *Yesod*, which is linked to *Tif’eret*.

    *Yesod* is known as Righteous One, based on [[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]: עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*). The verse literally means *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, but is understood as *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*. See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 12#12b|12b]]; *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

    In rabbinic literature Joseph is granted the title Righteous in recognition of resisting the sexual advances of Potiphar’s wife. See [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^39-1|Genesis 39]]; BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 35#35b|35b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 93:7; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana, nispaḥim*, 460; above, pages 357–59. Cf. *Tanḥuma, Bereshit* 5, and *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 38, which cite [[Amos 2#^2-6|Amos 2:6]].

[^486]: **consummation of the body** *Tif’eret* is the torso of the body, which is consummated by *Yesod*, the divine phallus. See above, pages 333, 358.

[^487]: **He is consummation of the body; yet even so, Benjamin…** Though Joseph symbolizes consummation of the body, Benjamin actually completed the total of twelve tribes.

[^488]: **If all those tribes are completed here…** Then *Shekhinah* will rest upon them here in Paddan Aram (Mesopotamia), whereas Her proper site is the land of Israel. See the statement attributed to Rabbi El’azar son of Tsadok in *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1: “*Shekhinah* is not revealed outside the land \[of Israel\].”

[^489]: **all those twelve tribes comprise the adornment of the lower world** Of *Shekhinah*. See above, pages 365, 377.

[^490]: ***Rachel, alas, died in the land of Canaan*** As she was giving birth to Benjamin. See [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^35-16|Genesis 35:16–19]].

[^491]: **settling in a completed house** Among the full complement of twelve tribes. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-158b|1:158b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-174a|174a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-175b|175b]].

    On *Shekhinah* as Jacob’s wife, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-236b|236b]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|3:187b]].

[^492]: **Why didn’t Leah die at the same time?** After all, she had mothered half of the twelve tribes.

[^493]: **house belonged to the lower world…** The twelve tribes belonged to the lower world, *Shekhinah* (symbolized by Rachel), and were intended to adorn Her, not the higher world, *Binah* (symbolized by Leah). So Leah’s death was unnecessary.

[^494]: **Leah’s death is not mentioned…** It is not recorded in the Torah. See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^35-8|Genesis 35:8]].

[^495]: **Leah was concealed in the Cave… while Rachel in revealment…** On Leah’s burial in the cave, see [[Torah 12 - Vayechi#^49-31|Genesis 49:31]]. On Rachel’s burial on the road to Ephrat, see ibid. 35:19; 48:7.

[^496]: **So all blessings partake of two worlds, revealed and concealed** The Hebrew formula of blessing begins by addressing God directly in the second person (revealed): “Blessed are You, *YHVH*,” but then it switches to the third person (concealed): “… who has hallowed us by His commandments …” For Rabbi El’azar these two grammatical persons represent two aspects of divine personality: *Shekhinah* (revealed) and *Binah* (concealed).

    See *Bahir* 125 (184); Scholem, *Das Buch Bahir*, 134–35; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^15-26|Exodus 15:26]]; Todros Abulafia, *Otsar ha-Kavod, Berakhot* 12a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|1:18a]]; [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-271b|3:271b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-289a|289a]] (*IZ*) (and *NZ*, ad loc., n. 6); *TZ* 39, 79b; *Kolbo* 1.

[^497]: ***For daughters* אשרוני (*ishsheruni*), *will deem me happy*** The verse continues: *So she named him* אשר (*Asher*), *Asher*. When Zilpah, Leah’s maid, gave birth to a son, Leah named him Asher.

[^498]: **Because of Her, she named him אשר (*Asher*), *Asher*** Rabbi El’azar interprets *Asher* as alluding to *Binah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15a|1:15a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246a|246a]]. In Hebrew this name is identical with the opening word of the third-person phrase cited in the note before last: “אשר (*asher*), who, has hallowed us by His commandments,” which also alludes to *Binah*.

[^499]: ***Blessed be YHVH, God of Israel, from world to world*** *YHVH* refers to *Tif’eret*, the blessed Holy One. On the phrase *from world to world*, see M *Berakhot* 9:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34a|1:34a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-153b|153b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-22a|2:22a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-53b|53b]]; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-145b|3:145b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285b|285b]], [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-297b|297b]].

[^500]: **So we call higher world הוא (*Hu*), He, and lower world אתה (*Attah*), You** The third-person pronoun indicates *Binah*, who, being concealed, can be referred to only indirectly. The second-person pronoun indicates *Shekhinah*, who, being revealed, can be addressed directly. See above, pages 361–62.

[^501]: **Since He is blessed from the higher world… from the lower world…** Since *YHVH*, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, is blessed from *Binah* (higher world), He is also blessed from *Shekhinah* (lower world). The blessing from *Shekhinah* derives from righteous action on earth and is conveyed to *Tif’eret* via *Yesod* (Righteous One), who is known as *Zion* and dwells in *Jerusalem* (symbolizing *Shekhinah*).

[^502]: **יהוה‎ יהוה (*YHVH, YHVH*)—two times, two worlds…** Alluding to *Shekhinah* and *Binah*.

[^503]: **separated by a punctuation mark…** In the Masoretic text the two occurrences of the name are separated by a vertical line. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-120a|1:120a]]–b; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-138a|3:138a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|187b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-289b|289b]] (*IR*).

[^504]: **Why not the other time?** When later Jacob fled secretly from Laban. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-17|Genesis 31:17–21]].

[^505]: **twelve tribes would be completed in an alien land** See above, page 380.

[^506]: **once Benjamin was born, *Shekhinah* linked Herself with all those tribes…** See above, page 380.

[^507]: **Lower world was destined for Jacob as She was for Moses** *Shekhinah* was destined to unite with Jacob as well as Moses, since both of these male biblical heroes symbolize *Tif’eret*. According to rabbinic tradition, after encountering God on Mount Sinai, Moses abstained from sexual contact with his wife and maintained union with *Shekhinah*.

    See *Sifrei*, Numbers 99; BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 87#87a|87a]]; *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 13; Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Yesodei ha-Torah* 7:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-22a|1:22a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-152b|152b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-234b|234b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-222a|2:222a]]; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|3:148a]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-180a|180a]].

[^508]: **She possessed the house with all those tribes** *Shekhinah* obtained the house.

[^509]: **דביתא‎ עקרא (*iqqara de-veita*)… הבית‎ עקרת (*aqeret ha-bayit*)…** The verse in Psalms reads: *He settles* הבית‎ עקרת (*aqeret ha-bayit*), *the barren woman in her home, as a joyous mother of children*. Following midrashic tradition, Rabbi Yehudah interprets the word עקרת (*aqeret*), *barren woman*, as עיקר (*iqqar*), “essence.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 20:2; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 15; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 14:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|50a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149b|149b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154a|154a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157b|157b]].

[^510]: **world above** *Shekhinah*.

[^511]: **this poor woman** Rachel.

[^512]: **If she dies here, I will never escape. Furthermore…** If Rachel gives birth to Benjamin and dies here in Paddan Aram, then *Shekhinah* will find a home here among the twelve tribes and I will have to stay along with Her, whereas the land of Israel is the intended site for both Her and them. See above, page 380.

[^513]: ***When Rachel had given birth to Joseph*** The verse continues: *Jacob said to Laban, “Send me on my way, so I may go to my place and my land.”*

[^514]: **Why didn’t he set out on his way immediately?** Why didn’t Jacob leave Laban right after Joseph was born, rather than staying another six years? See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-41|Genesis 31:41]].

[^515]: ***Moses returned to Jethro his father-in-law*…** The full verse reads: *Moses returned to Jethro his father-in-law and said to him, “Let me go return to my brothers in Egypt and see if they are still alive.” Jethro said to Moses, “Go in peace.”*

[^516]: **perfect** שלים (*Shelim*), “Complete, perfect,” borrowed from *Targum Onqelos* on [[Torah 06 - Toldot#^25-27|Genesis 25:27]], where Jacob—in contrast to Esau the hunter—is described as תם (*tam*), “simple, innocent, plain, mild, quiet, sound, wholesome.”

[^517]: **when he told him at first, he immediately spun schemes…** That is, Jacob told Laban, who then spun schemes. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-25|Genesis 30:25–36]]; and above, pages 382–83.

[^518]: **Laban was a sorcerer…** On Laban’s powers of witchcraft, see [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-27|Genesis 30:27]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, Rashi, Ibn Ezra, and *Sekhel Tov*, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|1:133b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-139b|139b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-161a|161a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164b|164b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166b|166b–167b]].

[^519]: ***Return to the land of your fathers*…** The verse continues: *and your birthplace, and I will be with you*. See *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 23.

[^520]: ***God remembered Rachel*…** The verse continues: *God listened to her and opened her womb*.

[^521]: ***Of the sons of Korah, upon* עלמות (*alamot*)…** Eleven psalms are associated with the sons of Korah, who were a guild of Temple singers or musicians. See [[Torah 41 - Pinchas#^26-11|Numbers 26:11]]; [[II Chronicles 20#^20-19|2 Chronicles 20:19]].

    The technical term *alamot* may refer to an instrument such as a flute or express a vocal quality, perhaps “youthful” (soprano), based on עלמה (*almah*), “maiden, damsel.” See [[Psalms 009#^9-1|Psalms 9:1]]; [[Psalms 048#^48-15|48:15]]; [[I Chronicles 15#^15-20|1 Chronicles 15:20]].

[^522]: **all his associates** All those who are mentioned in the headings of various Psalms.

[^523]: **lower world corresponding to the higher world** A general principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-38a|1:38a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57b|57b–58a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-129a|129a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-145b|145b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-156b|156b]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-205b|205b]]; [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-15b|2:15b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-20a|20a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-48b|48b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|144a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-251a|251a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-45b|3:45b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-65b|65b]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:273.

[^524]: **All those arrayals arrayed by David and Solomon…** The rotating divisions of priests and Levites in the Temple. See [[I Chronicles 23#^23-1|1 Chronicles 23]]–26; [[Nehemiah 12#^12-45|Nehemiah 12:45–46]]; M *Ta’anit* 4:2; *Tosefta, Ta’anit* 3:2.

[^525]: **entirely according to the supernal pattern** Corresponding to the angelic choir. On the heavenly and earthly temples, see *Mekhilta, Shirta* 10; IT *Berakhot* 4:5, 8c; *Tanḥuma, Vayaqhel* 7; *Pequdei* 1–3; *Shemot Rabbah* 33:4; *Midrash Tehillim* 30:1.

[^526]: **Just as there are watches on earth…** The term משמרות (*mishmarot*) refers both to the rotating divisions in the Temple and to the three “watches” of the night. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 3#3a|3a]]: “There are *mishmarot*, watches, in heaven, and there are watches on earth.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-189a|1:189a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231a|231a–b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-173a|2:173a–b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-195b|195b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-64b|3:64b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-6a|6a]] (*MhN*).

[^527]: **chanting song constantly** See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 14#14a|14a]]; *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 91#91b|91b]].

[^528]: **What is *Alamot*… ועלמות (*va-alamot*), *and damsels*…** As mentioned above (note 521), the technical term *alamot* may in fact derive from עלמה (*almah*), “damsel, maiden.”

[^529]: **As is said: *Is there any number to his troops?*** Just as in this verse from Job the innumerable troops are angels, similarly in Song of Songs the innumerable damsels are angels.

[^530]: **Three arrays… three other arrays** Each of the three divisions of angels comprises three subdivisions. The same passage appears in *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-228a|2:228a]]–b.

[^531]: **to the east** Corresponding to *Tif’eret*.

[^532]: **These nine arrays are conducted by engraved letters…** The angels are conducted by letters, as is about to be explained.

[^533]: **they stir** The angels stir, stimulated by the motion of the letters.

[^534]: **becoming three letters, all corresponding to…יה״ו (*yod, he, vav*)** The three letters form into a permutation of יה״ו (*yod, he, vav*), the three components of the name יהוה (*YHVH*).

    See *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-43b|1:43b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51a|51a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-17a|3:17a]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-74b|74b–75a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-66d|66d]] (*ShS*), [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-68b|68b–c]] (*ShS*); *TZ* 69, 111b.

[^535]: **resplendent speculum** דנהרא‎ אספקלריא (*Ispaqlarya de-nahara*), “A speculum \[or: glass, mirror, lens\] that shines.” See BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 49#49b|49b]]: “All the prophets gazed through a dim glass \[literally: an *ispaqlarya* that does not shine\], whereas Moses our teacher gazed through a clear glass \[literally: an *ispaqlarya* that shines\].” Cf. [[1 Corinthians 13#^13-12|1 Corinthians 13:12]]: “For now we see through a glass darkly, but then face-to-face.”

    In the *Zohar, Shekhinah* is the *ispaqlarya* that does not shine on its own but rather reflects the other *sefirot*, while *Tif’eret* is the *ispaqlarya* that shines. Here Rabbi Abba indicates that the three letters derive from *Tif’eret*, associated with the name יהוה (*YHVH*).

[^536]: **Those two supernal letters… compassion with judgment** The letter י (*yod*) symbolizes *Ḥokhmah* and the right side of the *sefirot*, characterized by compassion. The letter ו (*vav*) symbolizes *Tif’eret* and the middle sefirotic column, which includes judgment on the left. See Vital, cited in *MM*. For a different interpretation, see *OY*.

[^537]: **two, from the supernal world in mystery of male** These two letters symbolize two components of the World of the Male, which extends from *Ḥokhmah* (united with *Binah*) through *Yesod*.

[^538]: **one ascending, joining them, is female** The letter ה (*he*) symbolizes the feminine.

[^539]: **Just as female is embraced by two sides, right and left** Just as *Shekhinah* is embraced by the two sefirotic arms, *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^540]: **supernal world** The sefirotic realm, specifically *Binah*, storehouse of letters.

[^541]: **engendering all works below…** Creation unfolds through the letters of the alphabet. According to *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:1–2, God created the world by means of “thirty-two wondrous paths of wisdom,” namely, the twenty-two letters of the Hebrew alphabet and the ten *sefirot*.

[^542]: **whoever knows them and is mindful of them is beloved…** Similar wording appears concerning the forty-two-letter divine name (in BT *Qiddushin* [[Kiddushin 71#71a|71a]]) and the twelve-letter divine name (*Bahir* 80 \[111\]). See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 17#17a|17a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-45b|1:45b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-152b|152b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|242b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-46a|2:46a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-228a|228a]].

[^543]: **All those letters are male and female** Rabbi Abba had said that י (*yod*) and ו (*vav*) are masculine, while ה (*he*) is feminine. Rabbi Shim’on teaches that all the letters are either male or female, as clarified below.

[^544]: **upper waters and lower waters** Symbolizing the male and female sefirotic potencies. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 13:13: “Rabbi Shim’on son of El’azar said, ‘Every single handbreadth \[of water\] descending from above is met by two handbreadths emitted by the earth. What is the reason? *Deep calls to deep*… ([[Psalms 042#^42-8|Psalms 42:8]]).’ Rabbi Levi said, ‘The upper waters are male; the lower, female. The former cry to the latter, “Receive us! You are creatures of the blessed Holy One and we are His messengers.” They immediately receive them, as is written: *Let the earth open* ([[Isaiah 45#^45-8|Isaiah 45:8]])—like a female opening to a male.’”

    See *Tosefta, Ta’anit* 1:4; 1 Enoch 54:8; *Seder Rabbah di-Vreshit*, 10 (*Battei Midrashot*, 1:25); *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 23; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-17b|1:17b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|29b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46a|46a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|60b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a–b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245b|245b]]; [[Zohar 3.223b|3:223b]].

    According to the second-century Greek physician Galen, sperm is generated by both male and female. See [[Torah 27 - Tazria#^12-2|Leviticus 12:2]]; Ibn Ezra, Naḥmanides, Baḥya ben Asher, and Sforno, ad loc.; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 60#60a|60a]]; *Niddah* [[Niddah 31#31a|31a]].

[^545]: **Three, three, on this side and on that side** Three arrays of angels on the right and on the left.

[^546]: **corresponding above; for that array is three, three—in single mystery** The triadic array of angels correponds to the sefirotic realm, which is also arranged in triads.

[^547]: **to the south** Corresponding to *Ḥesed*.

[^548]: **becoming one in the mystery of the complete holy name** The various male and female letters unite according to the pattern of the name יהוה (*YHVH*), in which the first and third letters are masculine; the second and fourth, feminine.

[^549]: **appointed arrays** Of angels.

[^550]: **array of supernal Patriarchs, according to… יה״ו (*yod, he, vav*)** The triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, symbolized by the three patriarchs, corresponds to the triad of letters יה״ו (*yod, he, vav*). Here *yod* symbolizes the right; *he*, the left; and *vav*, the center.

[^551]: **Countless forces and princes, all below** Lower angelic powers.

[^552]: **to the north** Corresponding to *Gevurah*.

[^553]: **Although they are twenty-two… twenty-seven** The full Hebrew alphabet consists of twenty-two letters plus five final letters.

[^554]: **nine letters in mystery of female… eighteen others…** Apparently the numbers nine and eighteen denote nine times the single female letter, ה (*he*), and nine times the two male letters, י (*yod*) and ו (*vav*). See also the list of letters in the note appended to *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-74d|74d]]–75a (*ShS*).
