[^555]: **supernal letters… other letters… High letters… low letters…** The large letters derive from *Binah*, the higher sefirotic realm, while the small letters derive from *Shekhinah*, the lower realm.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-132a|2:132a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-174a|174a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-180b|180b]]; [[Zohar 3.002a|3:2a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-220a|220a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-66c|66c]] (*ShS*), [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-74c|74c]] (*ShS*).

[^556]: **אלהים‎ ויזכור (*Va-yizkor Elohim*), *God remembered, Rachel*** The verse continues: *God listened to her and opened her womb*.

[^557]: **flux of destiny** מזלא (*Mazzala*), on which childbirth depends, according to BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 28a: “Rava said, ‘Life, children, and sustenance do not depend on merit but on *mazzala*, destiny.’”

    The word *mazzala* means “constellation, planet, planetary influence, destiny, guardian angel.” In the *Zohar* it is associated with the root נזל (*nzl*), “to flow,” and often refers to the flow of emanation from the World of the Male, which comprises *Binah* through *Yesod*, the divine phallus. *Yesod* conveys this flow to *Shekhinah*, thereby engendering souls of the newborn.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-43b|1:43b]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-137a|137a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-156b|156b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-181a|181a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207b|207b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-6a|2:6a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|252b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25b|3:25b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-134b|134b]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-289a|289a]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]] (*IZ*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-295b|295b]] (*IZ*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 193; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 65 (82); Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Orah*, 12a, 37a–b, 74a, 95a.

[^558]: **with her זכירה (*zekhirah*), remembering, is mentioned** Concerning Rachel’s conceiving of Joseph, the verb זכר (*zkhr*) appears, since this root means both “to remember” and “male,” alluding to the masculine potency of the divine phallus, *Yesod*, symbolized by Joseph.

    The name *Elohim* may refer here to *Binah*, from whom *Yesod* receives the flow, or to *Shekhinah*, recipient of the flow.

[^559]: ***And YHVH attended to Sarah*—not from the flux of destiny** Concerning Sarah’s conceiving of Isaac, the verb זכר (*zkhr*) does not appear; rather, the verb is פקד (*pqd*), “to attend,” since the focus is not on *Yesod* but on *Shekhinah*, who is associated with the latter verb. See above, page 174.

[^560]: **not below!** Not on *Shekhinah*, beneath *Yesod*.

[^561]: **However, it is written: ויהוה (*Va-YHVH*), *And YHVH*, all as one** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 51:2: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Wherever it is said: *And YHVH*, this implies: He and His Court.’” In Kabbalah this court symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment”) and pronounces the divine decree, so the phrase *And YHVH* encompasses “He \[*Tif’eret*, known as *YHVH*\] and His Court \[*Shekhinah*\].”

    Here the point is that the male divine potency (*Tif’eret* and *Yesod*) was involved along with *Shekhinah* in Sarah’s conceiving of Isaac.

    See JT *Berakhot* 9:5, 14b; Rashi on [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-29|Exodus 12:29]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64b|64b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105a|105a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-44b|44b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-149a|3:149a]]. The hermeneutical significance of *and* was championed by Rabbi Akiva. See BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 68#68b|68b]]; *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 51#51b|51b]].

[^562]: **If so, why is ‘attending’ mentioned?** If both the male and female potencies were involved, why specify “attending,” which indicates only *Shekhinah*?

[^563]: **‘remembering’ happened previously … *My covenant*…** The participation of *Yesod* (“remembering”) is already implied in the prediction of Isaac’s birth by the word *covenant*, referring to the covenant of circumcision associated with the divine phallus. Subsequently, the key of childbirth was transmitted to *Shekhinah*, who *attended to Sarah*.

    On the key of childbirth, see BT *Ta’anit* 2a–b. The verse from Genesis concludes: *at this appointed time next year*.

[^564]: **Similarly later…** The wording of the later verse, *And YHVH attended to Sarah*, implies that *Shekhinah* figured prominently along with the male potency.

[^565]: ***God remembered Rachel*** The verse continues: *God listened to her and opened her womb*.

[^566]: **Here זכירה (*zekhirah*), remembering, for this is above…** The word זכירה (*zekhirah*), “remembering,” alludes to זכר (*zakhar*), “male” (Aramaic, דכורא \[*dekhora*\]). The divine male potency united with *Shekhinah*, who is associated with the root פקד (*pqd*), “attend.” On the “flux of destiny,” see above, note 557.

    *Shekhinah* accompanied the Children of Israel in their Egyptian exile. See BT *Megillah* [[Megillah 29#29a|29a]]: “Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai says, ‘Come and see how beloved are Israel in the sight of the blessed Holy One! Wherever they went in exile, *Shekhinah* accompanied them. When they were exiled to Egypt, *Shekhinah* was with them…. When they were exiled to Babylon, *Shekhinah* was with them…. And even when they are destined to be redeemed, *Shekhinah* will be with them.’” See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 14.

    Here Rabbi Ḥiyya indicates that the male divinity responded to the exile by uniting with His female partner, who was suffering along with Her people. This reunion initiates the redemption of both *Shekhinah* and Israel.

[^567]: **אלהים‎ ויזכור (*Va-yizkor Elohim*), *God remembered, Rachel*…** As indicated above in note 558, Rachel’s conceiving of Joseph is marked by the verb זכר (*zkhr*), meaning both “to remember” and “male”—an allusion to the divine phallus, *Yesod*, symbolized by Joseph.

    The name *Elohim* may refer here to *Binah*, from whom *Yesod* receives the flow, or to *Shekhinah*, recipient of the flow. The reference to *covenant* reinforces the link with *Yesod*, who is associated with the covenant of circumcision.

    Further, Rachel may here symbolize *Shekhinah*, who shares the designation *covenant* with *Yesod*. According to this reading, both statements (*God remembered Rachel* and *I have remembered My covenant*) refer to the union of the divine male (*zkhr*) and female (*Rachel, covenant*).

[^568]: ***I have surely attended to you*** Meaning, *I have surely given heed to you*. The verse concludes: *and to what is being done to you in Egypt*. God declared this to Moses at the Burning Bush.

[^569]: **how could She say, *I have surely attended*?** Since *Shekhinah* Herself suffered exile and bondage along with Israel, how could She attend to Israel and promise to redeem them? She couldn’t redeem even Herself, since after all, “a prisoner cannot free himself from jail.” See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 5#5b|5b]]; *TZ* 6, 21b–22a.

[^570]: **If She was in exile, how could She manifest to Moses here?** If *Shekhinah* was confined to bondage in Egypt, how could She manifest to Moses in Midian at the Burning Bush? Normally, *Shekhinah* reveals Herself only in the land of Israel. See the statement attributed to Rabbi El’azar son of Tsadok in *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1: “*Shekhinah* is not revealed outside the land \[of Israel\].”

[^571]: ***full of His presence*** Full of *Shekhinah*, the Divine Presence.

[^572]: **As long as the Temple stood, *the whole earth*…the Holy Land** As long as the Temple in Jerusalem was still standing, *Shekhinah* dwelled there, Her presence permeating the land of Israel and from there extending abroad. The word ארץ (*arets*) means both “earth” and “land.”

[^573]: **Now that Israel is in exile, She is above, while Her power envelops Israel…** From one perspective *Shekhinah* is exiled along with Israel, while from another perspective She has withdrawn from earth and dwells in heaven, protecting Israel powerfully from above, wherever they are. See *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 24.

[^574]: ***Shekhinah* above, *Shekhinah* below** See *Midrash Konen* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 2:33); *Bahir* 116 (171).

[^575]: ***Shekhinah* above in twelve dominions, holy chariots… creatures** Surrounding and escorting *Shekhinah*. The chariots transport Her through heaven. The angelic creatures derive from Ezekiel’s theophany (1:5–19).

    On the twelve dominions, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-76b|1:76b]]–77a (*ST*), [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-156a|156a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-199a|199a]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-8a|2:8a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-62b|62b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-64b|64b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-66b|66b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-229b|229b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-78a|3:78a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-96b|96b]], [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-118b|118b]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-138a|138a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|148a–b]].

[^576]: ***Shekhinah* below among twelve holy tribes** See above, pages 365, 377.

[^577]: **although when Israel is in exile and She is disarrayed…** Lacking Israel’s accompaniment below, She is also isolated above.

[^578]: **This is exile along with Israel, for She shares with them in exile** While the people of Israel suffer below, She suffers above. See the view of Rabbi Shim’on cited above, note 566.

[^579]: **How is She arrayed?** Now, during this period of exile.

[^580]: **overturned his bed** Dismantling one’s bed and sleeping on a mattress on the floor are an expression of mourning. See BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 15a–b, 27a; *Semaḥot* 6:1.

[^581]: **took thorns and thistles…*from within a bush*…** *Shekhinah, the angel of YHVH,* appeared to Moses out of a thornbush to symbolize the pain She was experiencing on account of Israel’s suffering in Egypt.

    See *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 14; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 40; *Midrash Tehillim 27:2; Shemot Rabbah* 2:5. On *the angel of YHVH* as a name of *Shekhinah*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|1:61a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-113a|113a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-120b|120b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166a|166a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187a|3:187a]]. Cf. *Mekhilta, Shirta* 3.

[^582]: **outside his domain and no longer his own master** ברשותיה‎ קיימא‎ דלא (*De-la qayema birshuteih*), which bears both meanings.

[^583]: **how can he attend? What can he do?** How could *Shekhinah*, weakened in exile, respond to Israel’s suffering or redeem them?

[^584]: **But *paqod* from above, *paqadti* below** The duplicated verb implies “attending” both above and below.

[^585]: **Because *zekhirah*, remembering, had rested upon Her previously…** Once *Shekhinah* was attended to and empowered by masculine remembering from above, She Herself could attend to Israel.

    Actually, the verse *I have remembered My covenant* ([[Torah 14 - Vaera#^6-5|Exodus 6:5]]) comes after the verse *I have surely attended to you* (ibid. 3:16), but a nearly identical formulation appears in [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^2-24|Exodus 2:24]]: *God remembered His covenant*. On the sexual connotation of פקד (*pqd*), “attend,” see [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-1|Genesis 21:1]], cited below; *Bereshit Rabbah* 53:6; *Sekhel Tov*, [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-1|Genesis 21:1]].

[^586]: **She had already grasped a sign** Of deliverance. According to midrashic tradition, the duplicated verbal form of פקד (*pqd*) signifies redemption.

    See [[Torah 12 - Vayechi#^50-24|Genesis 50:24–25]]; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 24; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 48; *Shemot Rabbah* 3:8; 5:13; *Bereshit Rabbah* 97(98):6; Rashi and Naḥmanides on [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^3-18|Exodus 3:18]].

[^587]: **Similarly with Sarah: פקד‎ ויהוה (*Va-YHVH paqad*)…** The verb פקד (*pqd*) appears explicitly, but the male divine potency was also involved. See above, note 561.

[^588]: **But here—with Rachel, who had not been ‘remembered’ previously…** Since no indication of masculine remembering had appeared previously, now it does: אלהים‎ ויזכור (*Va-yizkor Elohim*), *God remembered, Rachel; God listened to her and opened her womb*.

[^589]: **flux of destiny** See above, note 557.

[^590]: **from Cappadocia to Lydda** This improbable journey from eastern Asia Minor to a city in Palestine recurs frequently in the *Zohar* and usually (though not here) includes an encounter with some surprising character. See 1:69b, 132a, 138a (*MhN*), 197b, 223a; 2:31a, 38b, 80b, 86a; 3:35a, 75b, 221b; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-22a|22a]] (*MhN*).

    The itinerary seems to be intentionally fantastic, though perhaps the author(s) imagined that Cappadocia was a Galilean village near Sepphoris, based on the phrase “Cappadocians of Sepphoris” in JT *Shevi’it* 9:5, 39a. According to a dream interpretation in *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:12, Cappadocia is not far at all from Palestine. Cappadocia figures prominently in M *Ketubbot* 13:11 and BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 110#110b|110b]], while Cappadocia and Lydda are linked in *Tosefta, Yevamot* 4:5 and BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 25#25b|25b]].

    See Scholem, in *Zion* (*Me’assef*) 1 (1926): 40–46 (and the appended note by S. Klein, 56); idem, *Major Trends*, 169; idem, *Kabbalah*, 222; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:63–64.

[^591]: **Rabbi Yehudah dismounted and said, “… let us engage in Torah”** Out of respect for the sanctity of Torah, he first dismounts from his donkey. See *Tosefta, Ḥagigah* 2:1, where Rabban Yoḥanan son of Zakkai dismounts from his donkey to hear an exposition of *ma’aseh merkavah*, “the account of the chariot,” from Rabbi El’azar son of Arakh, who had been driving the donkey from behind.

    Cf. BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 14#14b|14b]]; JT *Ḥagigah* 2:1, 77a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6a|1:6a]].

[^592]: **הבו (*Havu*), *Render, greatness to our God!*** The full verse reads: *When I proclaim the name of YHVH, render greatness to our God!* See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 21#21a|21a]]: “Rabbi Yehudah said, ‘Where do we find that a blessing before \[reading\] the Torah is ordained in the Torah? Because it says: *When I proclaim the name of YHVH, render greatness to our God!’”*

    In Kabbalah, Torah is pictured as one continuous divine name. See Scholem, *On the Kabbalah*, 37–44. By engaging in Torah, one celebrates and glorifies God.

[^593]: **If we were three, this would be fitting…** The verb הבו (*havu*), *render*, is in the plural, so if one person would *proclaim the name of YHVH* by expounding Torah, then two could *render greatness* by responding to his words.

[^594]: **This applies only to blessings…** Specifically to the grace after meals. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 45#45a|45a]]: “Three who have eaten together must invite \[one another to say grace\]… From where is this derived?… Rabbi Abbahu said, ‘From here: *When I proclaim the name of YHVH, render greatness to our God!’”* See *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 306.

[^595]: **But with Torah, even two…** See M *Avot* 3:2: “Rabbi Ḥanina son of Teradyon says, ‘… If two are sitting engaged in Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells between them.’”

    On the importance of engaging in Torah while on a journey, see [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-7|Deuteronomy 6:7]]; BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 54#54a|54a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|69b–70a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|115b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157a|157a]]; and the extreme formulation in BT *Ta’anit* 10b: “Rabbi Il’ai son of Berekhiah said, ‘Two disciples of the wise who are walking on the way and are not engaged in words of Torah deserve to be burned.’”

[^596]: **as is written: *Render greatness!*** With the verb in the plural. See above, notes 593 and 594.

[^597]: **two to respond** With “Amen.” Actually, only concerning the grace after meals are two required to respond with “Amen.” For all other blessings, even one can respond. On the significance of “Amen,” see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 53#53b|53b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-271a|3:271a]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285a|285a]]–b.

[^598]: **fulfillment of blessings in… mystery of three…** Corresponding to the triadic structure of the *sefirot*.

[^599]: **there is remembering for good and remembering for evil…** See M *Rosh ha-Shanah* 4:6; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 32#32a|32a]]–b.

[^600]: **פקידה (*Peqidah*), Attending, for good… *Peqidah* for evil…** See *Pesiqta Rabbati* 42; *Tanḥuma, Bemidbar* 21.

[^601]: **rememberings and attendings for good are well-known rungs…** Corresponding to the male and female couple in the sefirotic realm, mystery of faith: *Tif’eret* (or *Yesod*) and *Shekhinah*. See above, pages 388–92.

[^602]: **Other Side** The demonic realm.

[^603]: **Remembering and attending for evil… other gods…** Corresponding to the demonic male and female couple: Samael and Lilith. On the phrase “other gods,” see [[Torah 17 - Yitro#^20-3|Exodus 20:3]]: *You shall have no other gods besides Me*.

[^604]: **From here issue all mysteries of faith…** From remembering and attending for good.

[^605]: **From here issue all injuries, all evil…** From remembering and attending for evil.

[^606]: **wage war against that side!** Against evil and against the evil impulse.

[^607]: **He opened, saying** Rabbi Yehudah opened in reply.

[^608]: **on account of his side** The Other Side, led by Samael, who empowered Esau.

[^609]: **dealing with him as tortuously as necessary** See above, pages 269–72.

[^610]: **single pattern…בכרתי (*bekhorati*)…ברכתי (*birkhati*)…** First, Jacob cunningly obtained Esau’s birthright ([[Torah 06 - Toldot#^25-29|Genesis 25:29–34]]), and later he deceived Isaac into giving him Esau’s blessing. Immediately afterward the wronged son complained bitterly to his father: *He’s deceived me these two times: he took* בכרתי (*bekhorati*), *my birthright, and look, now he’s taken* ברכתי (*birkhati*), *my blessing!* ([[Torah 06 - Toldot#^27-36|Genesis 27:36]]).

    Rabbi Yehudah highlights the fact that the two words are anagrams. See above, page 308; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-111a|2:111a]].

[^611]: **Prevail against him precisely…** First obtaining the *birthright*, then the firstborn’s *blessing*.

[^612]: ***They will follow YHVH, roaring like a lion*** In the biblical context, the second phrase refers to God: *He will roar like a lion*. However, Rabbi Yehudah applies it to the human strivers. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 6#6b|6b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223a|1:223a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-79b|3:79b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-21d|21d]] (*MhN*).

[^613]: **as with Ezra who said…** The words are actually spoken by Nehemiah. The books of Ezra and Nehemiah were originally referred to by the name Ezra alone. See BT *Sukkah* [[Sukkah 37#37a|37a]], *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 14#14b|14b]]; *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 93#93b|93b]]; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 16:7; *Zohorei Ya’bets*.

[^614]: **Wherever a person prays, he should merge in multitude…** He should include himself with the entire community and formulate his prayer in the plural (“Remember us!”), not the singular (“Remember me!”), so as not to call attention to himself and make himself vulnerable to demonic forces who can expose his sins.

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 30#30a|30a]], regarding formulations of prayer: “Abbaye said, ‘A person should always associate himself with the Community.’” See ibid. 49b: “Samuel said, ‘A person should never exclude himself from the entire community.’”

    See ibid. 7b–8a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-167b|1:167b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-234a|234a]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245b|2:245b]] (*Heikh*).

[^615]: **Shunammite** The wealthy woman from the town of Shunem who fed and housed the prophet Elisha. She was rewarded with a son, who later died but was resuscitated by Elisha. See [[II Kings 04#^4-8|2 Kings 4:8–36]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|1:69b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33b|2:33b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-44b|44b]].

[^616]: **That day was Rosh Hashanah** The day referred to in [[II Kings 04#^4-8|2 Kings 4:8]]: *One day Elisha was passing through Shunem, where a wealthy woman lived, and she urged him to have a meal*. Similarly, according to a midrashic tradition, the phrase *one day* in [[Job 01#^1-6|Job 1:6]] refers to Rosh Hashanah, the Day of Judgment.

    See *Targum Yerushalmi*, Rashi, and Ibn Ezra on [[Job 01#^1-6|Job 1:6]]; *Midrash Iyyov*, ad. loc (*Battei Midrashot*, 2:158); *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231a|3:231a]].

[^617]: **the time the blessed Holy One is called King** Between Rosh Hashanah and Yom Kippur, God is addressed in the liturgy as “Holy King” and “King of Judgment.” See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 12#12b|12b]].

[^618]: **so they won’t notice him…** So demonic “remembering and attending” won’t notice him.

[^619]: ***He will slay me***, איחל‎ לא **(*lo ayaḥel*)…** According to the Masoretic text, the word *lo* is spelled לא (*lo*) with an א (*alef*), meaning *no*, but should be read לו (*lo*) with a ו (*vav*), meaning *in Him*. The two alternative spellings yield radically different readings: *See, He will slay me; I have no hope*, or: *Though He slay me, in Him will I hope*. According to Rabbi Yehudah, the ambiguous spelling is intentional, encompassing both contradictory declarations and reflecting Job’s torment.

    See M *Sotah* 5:5; BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 31#31a|31a]]; *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 47 - Re'eh#^14-2|Deuteronomy 14:2]].

[^620]: **on that side** The Other Side, the demonic.

[^621]: ***death*, as has been said, Angel of Death…** Lilith, the demonic feminine. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|1:148a]]–b (*ST*).

[^622]: **צלמות (*tsalmavet*), *darkness*—מות‎ צל (*tsel mavet*), *shadow of death*** This etymology appears frequently in *Targum Yonatan*, e.g., [[Isaiah 09#^9-1|Isaiah 9:1]]; [[Psalms 023#^23-4|Psalms 23:4]]; [[Job 10#^10-22|Job 10:22]]. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-242b|2:242b]].

[^623]: **riding upon it, its shadow and potency, coupling as one…** Shadowy Samael rides the serpent Lilith, empowering her. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 13: “Samael… took his band and descended and saw all the creatures created by the blessed Holy One. He determined that the most cunningly evil was the serpent, as is said: *Now the serpent was slier than any creature of the field that YHVH Elohim had made*. He \[the serpent\] looked like a camel, and he \[Samael\] mounted and rode him.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-64a|64a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-137b|137b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-146a|146a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|148a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-153a|153a]].

[^624]: ***Lift up your heads, O gates!*…** The verse continues in parallelism: *Be lifted up, openings of eternity! So the King of Glory may come*.

[^625]: **those gates being rivers and streams, six directions of the world** The gates referred to in Psalms are holy gates, the six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, who convey the flow of emanation. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-103b|1:103b]]; 3:253, 253b (*RM*).

    The term “six directions” (above, below, north, south, east, and west) derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13.

[^626]: ***whoever goes down to Sheol rises no more*** According to the Talmudic sage Rava, with these words Job denied the resurrection of the dead. See BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 16#16a|16a]].

[^627]: **One from World of the Male** The first *Elohim* designates *Binah*. Although She is often depicted as Divine Mother, *Binah* is also called “World of the Male,” encompassing the entire configuration of *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, who constitute a masculine entity ready to join *Shekhinah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|1:96a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-147a|147a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149a|149a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246a|246a–247a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127b|2:127b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|72b]] (*ShS*). Cf. 1:17b, 46b, 163a; 2:4a.

[^628]: **one from World of the Female** The second *Elohim* designates the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*.

[^629]: **depends on the flux of destiny** Childbirth depends on the flow of emanation from *Binah* through *Yesod* to *Shekhinah*. See above, note 557.

[^630]: **When Rachel was aroused by this name…** According to the verse, when Rachel gave birth to her first son, *she named him* יוסף (*Yosef*), *Joseph, saying, “May YHVH* יוסף (*yosef*), *add, another son to me!”* Rachel was aroused, or inspired, by the duplication of *Elohim* to invent the name *Yosef* (*May He add*), which indicated to Jacob that soon another son (Benjamin) would be born, completing the twelve tribes. Once the tribes were complete, Rachel would have to die, as explained below. See above, pages 380–83.

[^631]: **So he wanted to leave… he fled…** Jacob wanted to be sure that “the house,” the full array of twelve tribes, would be completed in the Holy Land, not in an alien land, so that *Shekhinah* (“the holy world”) would join with them in an appropriate setting. He wanted to leave Laban as soon as Joseph was born, but his departure was delayed some six years (see [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-41|Genesis 31:41]]). Once Rachel conceived Benjamin, he could wait no longer, so he fled. See above, pages 379–83.

[^632]: ***Elohim said to Jacob…*** The Masoretic text reads: יהוה (*YHVH*) *said to Jacob*…, as do the printed editions of the *Zohar* and certain manuscripts. However, according to the earlier recension of the *Zohar* preserved by several manuscripts, Rabbi Yehudah cites the reading *Elohim*, enabling him to show that *Shekhinah* was speaking to Jacob. See *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 10; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayetse* 21.

[^633]: **really telling him: ‘Until now Rachel was with you…’** *Shekhinah* was telling Jacob that now She would replace Rachel. On *Shekhinah* as Jacob’s wife, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-236b|236b]]; [[Zohar 9.09 - Yanuqa#^z3-187b|3:187b]].

    On the phrase “essence of the house,” see above, note 509.

[^634]: ***Rachel died* עלי (*alai*), *upon me*…** The verse continues: *in the land of Canaan*—as she was giving birth to Benjamin. See [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^35-16|Genesis 35:16–19]]. The word עלי (*alai*) means literally, “upon me, on me,” though it apparently connotes “alas, to my sorrow” (see [[Psalms 042#^42-6|Psalms 42:6–7]]). Here, drawing on midrashic tradition, Rabbi Yehudah reads it hyperliterally “upon me, on account of me.”

    See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 37:1; *Aggadat Bereshit* 52:1; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-32|Genesis 31:32]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 74:9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-175a|1:175a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223a|223a]].

[^635]: **another dweller came…** *Shekhinah*, the indwelling Presence. See above, pages 380–83.

[^636]: **That wicked one** Laban.

[^637]: **I see that Jacob rivets his eyes upon נוקבי (*nuqvei*),** females Because of his love for Rachel, Jacob had offered to serve Laban in exchange for her. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^29-18|Genesis 29:18]].

[^638]: **I did for the glory of the Holy King** To engender twelve tribes as a fitting site for *Shekhinah*.

[^639]: **Rather, *if you will do this for me*…** The verse continues: *I will go back and tend your flock, watching over them*. Jacob asks Laban instead to give him all of the dark-colored sheep and the white-spotted goats, both of which are relatively rare.

[^640]: ***That day he removed the streaked and spotted he-goats…*** The verse continues: *and all the speckled and spotted she-goats—every one that had white on it—and every dark-colored lamb, and handed them over to his sons*. Laban slyly removes all of the unusually colored animals that Jacob sought for his wages.

[^641]: ***He who walks blamelessly and does what** is righteous*… The verse continues: *and speaks the truth from his heart*.

[^642]: ***He who walks* תמים (*tamim*), *blamelessly*—Abraham… *tamim*** Abraham is enjoined to be *tamim* in [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-1|Genesis 17:1]], as God commands him to keep the covenant of circumcision: *When Abram was ninety-nine years old, YHVH appeared to Abram and said to him, “I am El Shaddai. Walk in My presence and be tamim, perfect”* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-1|Genesis 17:1]]). See *Midrash Tehillim* 15:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95a|1:95a]]–b.

[^643]: ***And does what is righteous*—Isaac** See *Midrash Tehillim*, ibid.: “*And does* צדק (*tsedeq*), *what is righteous*—Isaac, who acted righteously, offering himself to be bound on the altar.” In Kabbalah, Isaac symbolizes *Gevurah*, who stimulates and activates *Shekhinah*, known as *Tsedeq*, “Righteousness.”

[^644]: ***And speaks the truth*—Jacob** See ibid.: “*And speaks the truth*—Jacob, as is said: *You give truth to Jacob* ([[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]).” Truth symbolizes Jacob’s *sefirah, Tif’eret*.

[^645]: **why did he act this way with Laban?** Jacob used stratagems to increase the number of dark-colored sheep and white-spotted goats. For example, he peeled fresh shoots of wood, exposing the white tissue beneath, and then placed them in the water troughs, near where the animals mated. Jacob figured that if the mating animals were subjected to the sight of this white tissue, the color of their offspring would be affected. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-32|Genesis 30:32–43]].

    This interpretation rests on folkloristic beliefs and assumes the inheritability of acquired characteristics. See Jehuda Feliks, in *Encyclopaedia Judaica*, 4:1024–26; Speiser, *Genesis*, 234–39; Sarna, *Genesis*, 212.

    For justification of Jacob’s questionable behavior here and elsewhere, see *Sekhel Tov*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-10|Genesis 31:10]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-138a|138a]]–b, [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160a|160a]]; Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 46–49, 55–57, 64–67.

[^646]: **Jacob was examining the hour of his zodiacal constellation** If his attempt proved successful, this would signify astrological confirmation that the time was propitious for him to set out on his journey. “Zodiacal constellation” renders מזלא (*mazzala*), “constellation, planet, planetary influence, destiny, guardian angel.” See above, note 557.

    According to the *Zohar*, stars influence the world but are themselves messengers of God. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-171b|2:171b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-232a|232a]]; [[Zohar 3.216b|3:216b]] (*RM*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-251a|251a–b]] (*RM*); 281b (*RM*); Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:653–54.

[^647]: ***Let my honesty testify for me on a future day*** The verse continues: *when you come to check on my wages. Any goat in my possession that is not speckled or spotted, or any hmb that is not dark-colored, will be considered stolen*.

[^648]: **rather, in total honesty and perfect intention** In exchange for all his labor.

[^649]: **obtained Laban’s permission** Laban agreed that Jacob could keep any dark-colored sheep or white-spotted goats. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-34|Genesis 30:34]]: *Laban said, “Good! Let it be according to your word.”*

[^650]: **How many types of sorcery Laban employed…** On Laban’s sorcery, see *Targum Yerushalmi*, Rashi, Ibn Ezra, and *Sekhel Tov* on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-27|Genesis 30:27]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|1:133b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-139b|139b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164b|164b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166b|166b–167b]].

[^651]: **blessing from above is never less than a thousand…** Based on the sefirotic decade.

[^652]: **he found only ten of every species** Since Laban had appropriated all the rest.

[^653]: **by taking those rods and placing them facing the flock** See above, note 645.

[^654]: **whatever they bear of that pattern will be your wages** Only the dark-colored sheep and the white-spotted goats.

[^655]: ***ten* מונים (*monim*), *times*—ten מן (*min*), of, this…** Rabbi Abba’s play on words apparently also includes מין (*min*) and מינים (*minim*), “species.”

[^656]: **until the blessed Holy One… wresting forcefully what was his** On the divine role in this episode, see [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-10|Genesis 31:10–12]]; *Sekhel Tov*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-10|Genesis 31:10]]; and *Bereshit Rabbah* 73:10: “Rabbi Ḥunya said, ‘The ministering angels carried off some of Laban’s sheep and brought them over to Jacob’s flocks.’”

[^657]: **some depend on action… speech … aspiration of the heart** There are various ways to draw down the flow of blessing from the upper worlds: through holy action, verbal prayer, and genuine intention. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99b|1:99b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z1-220b|220b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-239b|2:239b]]; [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-184a|3:184a]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285a|285a]].

[^658]: **by prayer—with speech and aspiration** Words of prayer must be recited with pure intention.

[^659]: **if not by prayer, they depend on action** Certain supernal processes depend on action, as here, with Jacob.

[^660]: **Perfect Jacob** This title derives from a description of Jacob in *Targum Onqelos* on [[Torah 06 - Toldot#^25-27|Genesis 25:27]]. In the biblical verse Jacob—in contrast to Esau the hunter—is described as תם (*tam*), “simple, innocent, plain, mild, quiet, sound, wholesome.” The *Targum* employs the Aramaic word שלים (*shelim*), “complete, perfect.”

[^661]: ***He placed the rods …in the watering troughs*** The verse continues: *where the flock came to drink, facing the flock. Now, they were in heat when they came to drink*.

[^662]: **from the bubbling spring of all to all those supernal rungs…** From the upper sefirotic source to all six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, whose core is *Tif’eret*, Jacob’s share.

[^663]: ***rods*… Rungs, courts of justice** *Gevurah, Hod*, and *Shekhinah* (or forces beneath Her), through whom divine judgment is administered. These serve as rods of punishment.

[^664]: **stripping them of Judgment** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who removes the harshness of Judgment with the sweetness of *Ḥesed*.

[^665]: **ברהטים (*Ba-rehatim*)*… A king is bound* ברהטים (*ba-rehatim*)…** The *king* is *Binah*, source of blessing, who is *bound* or contained in the *rehatim, channels* (or “cavities”) of the divine brain. Rabbi El’azar understands *ba-rehatim* (in the verse from Genesis) as *with the channels*, since the preposition ב (*be*) can mean “with” as well as “in.” Jacob was able to strip the rods of their harshness *with* the flow of emanation from above.

    See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 31:4; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 7:6; *Tanḥuma, Tetsavveh* 6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-14a|1:14a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-162a|162a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-163a|163a]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-140a|3:140a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-269b|269b]]; *ZḤ* 111d (*Tiq*); *TZ* 6, 21b; *TZ* (2) 6, 144b.

    On מוחא‎ רהיטי (*rehitei moḥa*), “channels \[or: cavities\] of the brain,” see the last two citations (from *TZ*) and *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-136a|3:136a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-293a|293a–b]] (*IZ*). For various interpretations of the *rehatim* in this paragraph and the next, see *OY, KP*, Galante, *MM, Sullam, MmD*.

[^666]: **this *king is bound*… from the supernal king** *Tif’eret is bound* and contained in the *rehatim, channels*, of *Binah*, the supernal king.

[^667]: ***In the watering troughs*—whence all is watered** *With the watering troughs*, the *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*.

[^668]: **site where they gather** *Shekhinah*.

[^669]: ***Where the flock came to drink*** The full verse reads: *He placed the rods that he had peeled in the channels, in the watering troughs, where the flock came to drink, facing the flock. Now they were in heat when they came to drink*.

[^670]: ***watering all beasts of the field*…** The *beasts of the field* and the *flocks* symbolize camps of angels nourished by *Shekhinah*, who is pictured as a field. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-151b|1:151b]]–152a (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-152a|152a]].

[^671]: ***They were in heat*** The verse continues: *when they came to drink*.

[^672]: **When the north wind blows, waters congeal…** *Gevurah*, or *Din* (“Judgment”), symbolized by the north, freezes the flow of emanation. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-152a|152a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-30a|2:30a]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 341.

[^673]: **When the south wind arouses, waters warm…** The warmth of *Ḥesed*, symbolized by the south, thaws the frozen waters.

[^674]: **ויחמנה (*Va-yeḥamnah*)? But look at what is written: ויחמו (*Va-yeḥemu*)!** The two consecutive verbs are in different genders: first feminine, then masculine. How can this be explained?

[^675]: **However, they are all female** Since all the forces beneath *Shekhinah* receive from Her, they are depicted as feminine.

[^676]: ***Jacob took rods*…** The full verse reads: *Jacob took rods of fresh poplar, almond, and plane, and peeled white stripes in them, exposing the white of the rods*.

[^677]: ***For Jacob, chose for himself Yah, Israel as his own treasure*** The translation is intentionally vague; see below.

[^678]: **So far, we don’t know who chose whom…** The verse can be rendered: *For Yah has chosen Jacob for Himself, Israel as His own treasure*, or: *For Jacob has chosen Yah for himself, Israel* \[has chosen Yah\] *as his own treasure*. See *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 312.

[^679]: **Jacob selected his allotted portion as his share** Even though the verse from Deuteronomy apparently removes the ambiguity of the verse from Psalms and determines its meaning, Rabbi El’azar shows that the alternative sense is also true: *For Jacob has chosen Yah for himself*, choosing his divine share.

[^680]: **ascending above all rungs** The two letters of the divine name יה (*Yah*) signify *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*, issuing from *Keter* at the top of the sefirotic ladder.

[^681]: ***rods of fresh* לבנה (*livneh*), *poplar*** For the full verse, see above, note 676.

[^682]: **white rung of the right side** *Ḥesed*. On this and the following lines, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-162a|1:162a]] (*ST*); Moses de León, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 55–57, 64–67.

[^683]: **red rung of the left side** *Gevurah*.

[^684]: ***He peeled white stripes in them*, stripping it of Judgment…** Jacob removed the harshness of *Gevurah* with the sweetness of *Ḥesed*. See above, page 402.

[^685]: **entered between them… one in two colors** Jacob embodies *Tif’eret*, who blends the qualities of *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^686]: **so that white would be revealed over red** So that the quality of *Ḥesed* would predominate.

[^687]: **to his allotted rung from the bubbling spring of all…** To *Tif’eret* from the upper sefirotic source.

    The phrase “three as one,” referring here to the triadic nature of *Tif’eret*, may derive from a legal term referring to a triad of judges. See BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 22#22a|22a]]; *Tosafot*, ad loc., s.v. *be-motev telata ka-ḥada;* Asher ben Yeḥiel, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.022b|1:22b]] (*TZ*).

[^688]: **by this act of wisdom** By Jacob’s action with the rods.

[^689]: **while blessings settle upon him** Upon *Tif’eret*.

[^690]: ***In the morning… at evening*…** From Jacob’s deathbed blessing to Benjamin. *Morning* and *evening* symbolize *Ḥesed* and *Shekhinah. Tif’eret* draws blessing from *Ḥesed* and conveys it to *Shekhinah*, who then distributes it to the lower worlds. See [[Proverbs 31#^31-15|Proverbs 31:15]], often applied to *Shekhinah*, and *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247b|1:247b]]–248a.

    The word ולערב (*ve-la-erev*) is apparently understood as *to evening*, since the preposition ל (*le*) means “to” as well as “at.”

[^691]: **Rabbi Yeisa the Younger** On his youthfulness, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-79b|2:79b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-153a|153a]].

[^692]: ***Head of the righteous one* is Holy Corona…** *Shekhinah* is identified as עטרה (*Atarah*), “Crown, Corona” (*Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|74a]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-100b|2:100b]]). Here this name of *Shekhinah* alludes to the corona of the divine phallus, *Yesod* (Righteous One). In the ritual of circumcision, first the foreskin is removed, disclosing the mucous membrane, which is then torn down the center and pulled back, revealing the corona. The removal of the foreskin symbolizes *Yesod*, while uncovering the corona symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 12.12 - Passages (Mis)Labeled as Tosefta#^z1-32a|32a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96b|96b]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-98b|98b]] (*ST*); Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (67); idem, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 133. Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-40a|2:40a]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125b|125b]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-91b|3:91b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-95b|95b]]. On *Shekhinah* as corona of the phallus, see Wolfson, *Through a Speculum That Shines*, 274–75, n. 14; 357–68; idem, “Woman—The Feminine as Other in Theosophic Kabbalah”; idem, *Circle in the Square*, 85–92. On *Yesod* as Righteous One, see above, note 485. See also above, p. 242, n. 305.

[^693]: **Jacob, who receives blessings and channels them…** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who receives the flow of blessing from above and conveys it to *Yesod* (Righteous One), the divine phallus who disseminates blessed seed.

[^694]: **site of head of Covenant, from which bubbling springs gush** The head of the divine phallus.

[^695]: **Orifice of a flask…** דקיסטא‎ נוקבא (*Nuqba de-qista*). *Nuqba*, “Hole,” also means “female,” which appears in the next line. “Flask” renders *qista*, which derives from the Greek *xestes*, a measure about the size of a pint. The *Arukh* (s.v. *qst*) understands it as a flask or jug. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|1:33a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-121b|121b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]].

[^696]: **Similarly, *head of the righteous one*…** As wine pours from the opening of a measured vessel, so holy seed is ejaculated from the opening of the divine phallus into *Shekhinah*. See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 15#15a|15a]]: “Shemu’el said, ‘Any emission of semen that does not shoot forth like an arrow does not fructify.’”

[^697]: **guarding the sign of the holy covenant … Righteous…** By avoiding illicit sexual behavior, the male preserves the sanctity of his phallus—site of the covenant of circumcision—and thereby attains *Yesod*’s title: Righteous.

    Rabbi Shim’on’s idiom reflects the promiscuity prevalent in the Jewish community in thirteenth-century Castile. See Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 51–54 (63–67); Baer, in *Zion* 2 (1937): 31–33, 36–44; idem, *History*, 1:250–63; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1371–72; Assis, “Sexual Behavior in Mediaeval Hispano-Jewish Society.”

[^698]: ***I have been* נער (*na’ar*), *young*… uttered by the Prince of the World** By Metatron, the chief angel, also known as נער (*na’ar*), “Youth, Lad (heavenly servant).” See BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 16#16b|16b]]: “Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥmani said in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan, ‘This verse was uttered by the Prince of the World: *I have been young and now I am old*. Who said it? If you suggest it was the blessed Holy One, does old age pertain to Him? So David must have said it. But was he so old? Rather, you must conclude that the Prince of the World uttered it.’”

    See above, page 297; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-85c|85c]] (*MhN, Rut*). On Metatron, see *Tosafot, Yevamot* 16b, s.v. *pasuq zeh;* Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 377–81; Margaliot, *Mal’akhei Elyon*, 73–108.

[^699]: **imbued with more wisdom than people imagine** Some people, pointing to the righteous who suffer, doubt the truth of this verse.

[^700]: ***I have been na’ar, young, and now I am old*. So it is!** When the divine couple unites, Metatron is rejuvenated by their flow; when this union is interrupted, he wanes.

[^701]: **in praise of this union, in which day is never lacking night…** Metatron is extolling the union of divine male and female, symbolized by day and night.

[^702]: **Righteous One is linked above, linked below** *Yesod* constitutes the link between *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*.

[^703]: **does not woo the female…** *Yesod* does not need to seek out *Shekhinah*, who accompanies Him constantly. For *bread* as a female symbol, see [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^39-6|Genesis 39:6]], 9; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^39-6|Genesis 39:6]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 70:4; 86:6; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 11; *Shemot Rabbah* 1:32. Cf. [[Proverbs 30#^30-20|Proverbs 30:20]].

[^704]: **But during exile, not so!** When Israel languishes in exile and the Temple is destroyed, union above is interrupted.

[^705]: **It is written: *his seed*, not *him*…** The verse reads: *I have never seen… his seed begging bread*, not: *him begging bread*. The subject is not *Yesod* but His seed. When the people of Israel dwell in their own land, the divine couple is united intimately and seed issues, with no need of wooing the female. In time of exile, the couple is separated and no seed issues. In either case, *I have never seen … his seed begging bread*.

[^706]: ***I have never seen a righteous man forsaken*—what about during exile?** Here the subject is *Yesod* Himself, not *his seed*. Isn’t He *forsaken* when the union is ruptured by exile?

[^707]: **linked above, never *forsaken*!** Even if *Yesod* is no longer joined with *Shekhinah*, His connection is maintained above with *Tif’eret*.

[^708]: **At other times He is not *forsaken* by female…** When there is no exile, *Yesod* is linked above and below, to *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*.

[^709]: ***God placed them in the expanse of heaven*** The verse concludes: *to shine upon earth*. The context is the creation of the sun, moon, and stars.

[^710]: **Righteous One** *The expanse of heaven* is *Yesod*.

[^711]: **Although it is said: *in the expanse of heaven*, precisely…** One might assume that *expanse of heaven* designates *Tif’eret*, known as *heaven*, but a more precise reading points to *Yesod*, the extension of *Tif’eret*. While *Tif’eret* constitutes the torso of the male sefirotic body, *Yesod*, the divine phallus, is the body’s consummation.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20a|1:20a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34a|34a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-168b|168b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-14a|14a]] (*MhN*).

[^712]: **Eighth expanse… from below to above…** *Binah*, eighth *sefirah* counting up from *Shekhinah*. She conceives and births all the lower *sefirot*, pictured here as stars.

[^713]: **Similarly, eighth expanse from above to below…** *Yesod*, eighth *sefirah* counting down from *Ḥokhmah*. He absorbs all the sefirotic lights generated by *Binah*. See *Bahir* 105 (157).

[^714]: **forming a flowing river whose waters never cease** Together, *Binah* and *Yesod* constitute the river of emanation.

[^715]: ***To shine upon earth*** Upon *Shekhinah*, symbolized by *earth*.

[^716]: **Although already explained, all shares a single pattern** Although the verse has already been interpreted along similar lines, Rabbi Shim’on here emphasizes the shared pattern of *Binah* and *Yesod*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20a|1:20a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34a|34a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-14a|14a]] (*MhN*).

[^717]: **One sustains and nourishes the higher world, including itself…** *Binah* nourishes the higher world, including Herself, along with the *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*.

    The phrase “including itself” renders ביה‎ דאיהו (*de-ihu beih*), literally, “in which it is” or “which is in it.” The second alternative would refer to the configuration of *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*, originating within *Binah*.

[^718]: **lower world along with all those lower aspects** *Shekhinah* along with the realms and angelic powers beneath Her.

[^719]: **Now, you might ask ‘Who is the higher world? After all…** Since *Binah* Her self is the higher world, how can one say that She nourishes the higher world?

[^720]: **all those issuing from it are named after it** The *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod* share in the name “higher world.”

[^721]: **Who is *Lebanon?* This has already been established…** *Lebanon* symbolizes *Ḥokhmah*, while its cedars are the six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, which began as saplings in the higher sefirotic realm and were later transplanted into *Shekhinah*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 15:1: “*YHVH Elohim planted a garden in Eden* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-8|Genesis 2:8]])…. As is written: *The trees of YHVH are sated, the cedars of Lebanon that He planted*. Rabbi Ḥanina said, ‘They resembled antennae of grasshoppers, and the blessed Holy One uprooted them, transplanting them in the Garden of Eden.’”

    See Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-31a|1:31a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|35a]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-217b|3:217b]].

[^722]: **These are two birds we have discussed … issuing from Lebanon…** The two birds are *Binah* and *Shekhinah*, who emanate from *Ḥokhmah*, generating other divine or angelic powers and birdlike souls.

    On the angelic birds of *Shekhinah*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-172a|1:172a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-217b|217b]], [[Zohar 11.08 - Tosefta#^z1-234a|234a]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 5.06 - Sifra di-Tsni'uta#^z2-179a|2:179a]] (*SdTs*). On birdlike souls, see BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 91#91a|91a]]; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, add. *2*, 7:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-196b|3:196b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-217b|217b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-13c|13c]] (*MhN*).

[^723]: **Mystery of the word: *Now Laban had two daughters*…** Here לבן (*Lavan*), like לבנון (*Levanon*), symbolizes *Ḥokhmah*, while his two daughters symbolize *Binah* and *Shekhinah*.

[^724]: ***The stork*… as is written: *the name of the elder was Leah*** *The stork* symbolizes *Binah*, identified with Laban’s elder daughter.

[^725]: **those six supernal sons, six directions of the world…** The six *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, engendered by *Binah*. The term “six directions” (above, below, north, south, east, and west) derives from *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13.

[^726]: **Because this is the higher world—though called female** Although She is often depicted as Divine Mother, *Binah* is also “World of the Male,” encompassing the entire configuration of *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Yesod*, who constitute a masculine entity ready to join *Shekhinah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|1:96a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-147a|147a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149a|149a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246a|246a–247a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127b|2:127b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|72b]] (*ShS*). Cf. 1:17b, 46b; 2:4a.

[^727]: **for when it expands…*ḥasidah*, חסד (*Ḥesed*)…primordial light…** The masculine aspect of *Binah* emanates radiant goodness, exemplified by *Ḥesed*, first of the lower *sefirot*, which is here linked with *Binah’s* name: *ḥasidah, stork*. *Ḥesed* is also identified with the primordial light of Creation, spoken into being by *Binah* (*Elohim*). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-16b|1:16b]].

[^728]: **Do not read ברושים (*beroshim*)…but rather בראשים (*be-rashim*)…** *Binah’s* home is in the six supernal *sefirot* emanating from Her, who are known as *heads*.

[^729]: **other world *has her home* in lower realms… Court of Justice…** *Shekhinah* settles in the heavenly realms beneath Her. She derives from the *sefirah* of *Din* (“Judgment”) and pronounces the divine decree.

[^730]: **Sometimes She is called as above, by all those names** As *Shekhinah* conveys the qualities of the various *sefirot*, She adopts their names.

[^731]: **Of this site is written: *YHVH*** *was sorry*… The verse in Genesis reads: *YHVH was sorry that He had made humankind on earth, and His heart was saddened. Shekhinah* is pained by human sin and rages with holy wrath. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57a|1:57a]].

[^732]: **For everything above is entirely radiant, vitality in all directions** The realm above *Shekhinah* is characterized by the radiance of *Ḥesed*.

[^733]: **‘There is no sadness before the Omnipresent’—‘before,’ precisely!** See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 5#5b|5b]]: “*If you will not listen, My soul will weep in secret because of the arrogance* ([[Jeremiah 13#^13-17|Jeremiah 13:17]])…. But is there weeping before \[i.e., on the part of\] the blessed Holy One? Look, Rabbi Papa has said: ‘There is no sadness before the blessed Holy One, as is said: *Splendor and majesty are before Him, power and joy in His place.’* There is no contradiction: this refers to the inner chambers; that refers to the outer chambers.”

    The last sentence is usually taken to mean: “This \[the weeping\] refers to the inner chambers; that \[the joy\] refers to the outer chambers.” However, Ḥanan’el ben Ḥushi’el (ad loc.) adopts the reverse interpretation: “This \[the joy\] refers to the inner chambers; that \[the weeping\] refers to the outer chambers,” which accords with Rabbi Shim’on’s view here: There is no sadness in the inner, upper realms, “before,” preceding *Shekhinah*, Divine Presence, the Omnipresent.

    See *KP; Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57a|1:57a]], [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-17b|2:17b–18a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-15b|3:15b]]; Margaliot, *Sha’arei Zohar, Ḥagigah* 5b.

[^734]: ***with joy*… the higher world… *with singing*…the lower world** Pure *joy* befits only the higher world, where “there is no sadness,” not the lower world of *Shekhinah*.

[^735]: ***He placed the rods that he had peeled in the channels…*** The verse continues: *in the watering troughs, where the flock came to drink, facing the flock. Now they were in heat when they came to drink*. See above, pages 401–4.

[^736]: **Woe to the wicked…who neither know nor consider…Torah!…** This attack conveys a critique of thirteenth-century radical rationalists who read the Torah critically and challenged its divine origin and authority.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264b|2:264b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-149a|3:149a]]–b, [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-152a|152a]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 391–92; idem, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 55–56; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 203; Baer, *History*, 1:236–305; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1082.

[^737]: **all words of Torah are entirely precious** Even seemingly strange passages such as the one about Jacob and the rods.

    This theme recurs frequently in the *Zohar*. See 1:145b; 2:55b, 98b–99b 3:79b, 149a, 152a, 174b, 202a, 265a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-6d|6d]] (*MhN*). Cf. *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 336; *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-47|Deuteronomy 32:47]].

[^738]: ***She is more precious than rubies; nothing desired can compare…*** Rabbi El’azar blends two verses describing wisdom, here identified with Torah: *She is more precious than rubies; nothing you desire can compare with her* ([[Proverbs 03#^3-15|Proverbs 3:15]]); and: *For wisdom is better than rubies; nothing desired can compare with her* (ibid. 8:11).

[^739]: **humiliation of Torah** Neglecting and denigrating her. See M *Avot* 6:2: “Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Levi said, ‘Every single day an echo resounds from Mount Horeb \[Sinai\], proclaiming: “Woe to creatures for the humiliation of Torah!” For whoever does not engage in Torah is called “rebuked.”’”

[^740]: ***It is not an empty word* מכם (*mi-kem*), *for you*… because of you!** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:14, in the name of Rabbi Yish ma’el: “*It is not an empty word* מכם (*mi-kem*), *for you;* and if it is empty, it is *mi-kem*, from you—because of you, because you do not know how to interpret.” See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-47|Deuteronomy 32:47]]; JT *Pe’ah* 1:1, 15b.

[^741]: **this is his own benefit, since to Torah herself…** Any new discovery one finds in Torah was hidden there all along; nothing can be added to her.

    See [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^4-2|Deuteronomy 4:2]]; and *Vayiqra Rabbah* 22:1: “Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Levi said, ‘… Scripture, Mishnah, Talmud, *Tosefta*, homiletical expositions, and even what a devoted disciple will some day say in the presence of his teacher—all of these were uttered to Moses at Sinai.’”

[^742]: **When supernal letters all combine on this rung…** When the letters of the alphabet emanating from *Binah*, the higher world, combine on the rung of *Shekhinah*, who is known as את (*Et*), spanning א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*).

[^743]: **to water all those flocks… with judgment and compassion** *Shekhinah* dispenses the flow of emanation to all the angels beneath Her, so that they can transmit blessing and execute judgment throughout the world.

[^744]: **Jacob sought to inaugurate evening prayer…** According to rabbinic tradition, the patriarchs instituted daily prayer. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 26#26b|26b]]: “Rabbi Yose son of Rabbi Ḥanina said, ‘The patriarchs instituted the prayers.’…Abraham instituted the morning prayer;… Isaac, the afternoon prayer;… Jacob, the evening prayer.” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:9.

    Here “evening prayer” symbolizes *Shekhinah*, identified with the night and the moon. Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, the sun, who illumines *Shekhinah* and conveys to Her the multifaceted sefirotic blessing.

[^745]: ***rods*—severe judgments issuing from supernal *Gevurah*** Tools of punishment issuing from *Gevurah*, which is known as *Din* (“Judgment”). See above, pages 402–4.

[^746]: **to mend this rung** To purge *Shekhinah* of judgment.

[^747]: **those four *channels* found beneath this well…** Four camps of angels situated beneath the well of *Shekhinah*, which is filled by the sefirotic stream.

[^748]: **so they are called *channels*** The biblical word רהט (*rehat*), *channel*, is a cognate of the Aramaic רהט (*rht*), “to run, flow.”

[^749]: **There all come to drink…** All the lower angelic powers drink from the four camps, imbibing judgment and enacting it in the world.

[^750]: ***Where the flock came to drink, facing the flock*** The verse reads: *He placed the rods that he had peeled in the channels, in the watering troughs, where the flock came to drink, facing the flock*.

[^751]: ***Where the flock…facing the flock*, facing one another** In the verse, *facing the flock* refers to the rods facing the flock. Rabbi El’azar, responding to the repetition of the word *flock*, imagines various flocks of angels facing one another.

[^752]: ***Now they were in heat*** The verse concludes: *when they came to drink*.

[^753]: **When they are crowned with judgment…** When the angels are arrayed in judgment, poised to administer it throughout the world.

[^754]: ***watchers…holy ones*** Angels.

[^755]: ***My soul cleaves to You*—so that his rung would be crowned above** David’s rung is *Shekhinah*, also known as *soul*. He prayed that She be adorned above, uniting with *Tif’eret*.

[^756]: **right hand grasps it, raising it…** The divine right hand, *Ḥesed*, grasps *Shekhinah* and draws Her to *Tif’eret*.

[^757]: ***His left hand beneath my head, his right hand embracing me*** *Shekhinah* is held by the divine hands, *Gevurah* and *Ḥesed*, who are extensions of *Tif’eret*, the blessed Holy One.

[^758]: **all those channels…all those flocks…** The four main camps of angels and the numerous flocks of angels beneath them. See above, pages 411–12.

[^759]: **right side** The side of holiness.

[^760]: **other side…** The demonic realm and its powers, symbolized by Laban and his flock.

[^761]: ***For you are a people holy to YHVH your God*…** The verse continues: *It is you YHVH has chosen to be His treasured people out of all the peoples on the face of the earth*.

[^762]: **Jacob is Glory of the Patriarchs, totality of them all** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 76:1: “Rabbi Pinḥas said in the name of Rabbi Re’uven, ‘… The chosen of the patriarchs is Jacob, as is said: *For Yah has chosen Jacob for Himself* ([[Psalms 135#^135-4|Psalms 135:4]]).’” Cf. BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 56#56a|56a]].

    Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who harmonizes and completes the polar opposites, *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, symbolized by Abraham and Isaac. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|1:119b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144b|144b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149b|149b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-152a|152a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-171b|171b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-172b|172b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-180a|180a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-207a|207a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-23a|2:23a]].

[^763]: **to illumine the moon, to inaugurate evening prayer** See above, page 411.

[^764]: **arraying adornments for faith** For *Shekhinah*, culmination of the mystical realm of faith, arrayed by camps of angels.

[^765]: ***It is you YHVH has chosen to be His treasured people…*** The verse continues: *out of all the peoples on the face of the earth*.

[^766]: ***He has withdrawn His right…Deliver Your right*…** In these biblical verses, *right* refers to God’s right hand: the Temple was destroyed when God withdrew the protective power of *His right hand*, and the Psalmist pleads: *Deliver (with) Your right hand!* Rabbi Abba may be applying the word *right* also to Israel, who dwells on that side. God *has withdrawn* (exiled) *His right* (Israel) *in the face of the enemy* (who embody the demonic left). Consequently, the Psalmist pleads: *Deliver Your right!*

[^767]: **Nothing will remain… except the blessed Holy One… and Israel…** This extremely chauvinistic formulation should be understood in the context of the sole possession of theological truth claimed by medieval religions, and against the background of medieval Jewry’s suffering and degradation. Moses Cordovero maintains that other nations will be purified and integrated into Israel. See *OY*, citing [[Zephaniah 3#^3-9|Zephaniah 3:9]]. Cf. [[Isaiah 55#^55-5|Isaiah 55:5]]; [[Isaiah 56#^56-7|56:7–8]]; [[Isaiah 60#^60-3|60:3]].

[^768]: **Look, *Shekhinah* is with us! Let us engage in Her words!…** *Shekhinah* accompanies those who pray before setting out on the road. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 29#29b|29b]]–30a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]]–50a, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a–b]].

    “Her words” are words of Torah, which guarantee Her presence. See M *Avot* 3:6: “Rabbi Ḥalafta of the village of Ḥananya says, ‘If ten are sitting engaged in Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells among them…even five…even three…even two…even one.’”

    On the importance of engaging in Torah while on a journey, see [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-7|Deuteronomy 6:7]]; BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 54#54a|54a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|69b–70a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|115b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157a|157a]]; and the extreme formulation in BT *Ta’anit* 10b: “Rabbi Il’ai son of Berekhiah said, ‘Two disciples of the wise who are walking on the way and are not engaged in words of Torah deserve to be burned.’”

[^769]: **Righteous One above** *Yesod*, who is known as Righteous One, based on [[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]: עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*). The verse, which literally means *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, is understood as *The righteous one* is *the foundation of the world*.

    See BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 12#12b|12b]]; *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

[^770]: **righteous one below** The righteous human being.

[^771]: ***Benayahu son of Yehoyada, son of* חי‎ איש (*ish ḥai*), *a living man*** Benayahu was a loyal follower of King David who attains mythic status in the *Zohar*. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 18#18a|18a]]–b: “Rabbi Ḥiyya said…, ‘The righteous are called “living” even in their death, as is said: *Benayahu son of Yehoyada, son of a living man*…. Do you mean to say that all other people are sons of dead men? Rather, *son of a living man*, for even in his death he was called *living*.’”

    See Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Tsedeq*, 7a; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6a|1:6a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-132a|132a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-136a|136a]].

[^772]: **Because he is righteous, for Righteous One…** The righteous human emulates *Yesod* and attains His title, *Ḥai*.

    On various senses of the title העולמים‎ חי (*Ḥei ha-Olamim*), “Life of the Worlds,” see [[Daniel 12#^12-7|Daniel 12:7]]; *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 16; *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:5; Schäfer, *Synopse zur Hekhalot-Literatur*, §275; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-4b|1:4b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-132a|132a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-135b|135b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-167b|167b]].

[^773]: **All is one—*Ḥai* and *Blessed*—inseparable…** *Yesod*, known as *Ḥai*, joins *Shekhinah*, known as *Blessed*.

    The expression “well of living waters” appears in [[Torah 06 - Toldot#^26-19|Genesis 26:19]]; [[Song of Songs 4#^4-15|Song of Songs 4:15]]. In the *Zohar* it refers to *Shekhinah*, filled with the vivifying waters of *Yesod*.

[^774]: **supernal world, high and exalted…** *Binah*, source of the flow of emanation.

[^775]: **From there it is blessed, illumining all those below** From *Binah, Shekhinah* is blessed, and then She conveys blessing below.

[^776]: **When all is filled fittingly, *Exalted be the God of my salvation!*** *Binah* Herself is *exalted* by the process.

[^777]: ***His eye*** עינו (*Eino*). The Masoretic text reads עיניו (*einav*), *His eyes*, while the manuscripts and early editions of the *Zohar* are divided between the singular and the plural. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 71:5; Theodor, ad loc.; *Minḥat Shai* on [[Job 36#^36-7|Job 36:7]].

[^778]: ***The eyes of YHVH are upon the righteous*** The plural, צדיקים (*tsaddiqim*), *the righteous*, may refer to both the human righteous and *Yesod*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-60b|2:60b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-202a|202a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-225a|225a–b]].

[^779]: **reigning kings linked to the throne** The angels transporting the divine throne, which symbolizes *Shekhinah*. The angels “reign” in the sense that they conduct the affairs of the world.

[^780]: **To rule the world, so that the throne is established upon its supports** The reign of the angels exalts them and thereby firmly establishes the divine throne, which they support.

[^781]: **to unite with its site fittingly** So the divine throne, *Shekhinah*, can join with *Tif’eret*.

[^782]: **This man is certainly a Jew** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63b|1:63b]]: “As they were walking, they saw a Jew approaching. Rabbi Yose said, ‘This person is a Jew—he looks so!’”

[^783]: **to enable people to attain merit!** By giving them an opportunity to perform a *mitsvah*—here the *mitsvah* of charity, which benefits not only the recipient but the donor too. See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 34:1, 5, 8; and BT *Bava Batra* loa, which cites [[Proverbs 10#^10-2|Proverbs 10:2]].

[^784]: **pint** קיסטא (*Qista*), which derives from the Greek *xestes*, a measure about the size of a pint. The *Arukh* (s.v. *qst*) understands it as a flask or jug. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|1:33a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-121b|121b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-162a|162a]].

[^785]: **spiced brew for sipping on the way** דאורחא‎ בקירטוי‎ טירמא (*Tirma be-qirtoi de-orḥa*). *Tirma* derives from the Greek *trimma*, “a brew prepared of pounded groats and spices.” See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 38#38a|38a]], where four varieties of *trimma* are described: sesame, grape-kernel, date, and קורטמי (*qurtemei*), “safflower.” *Qurtemei* assonates with *qirtoi* here, but the latter apparently derives from the root קרט (*qrt*), “to take a bite, nibble,” as in the phrase באורחא‎ למקרטא (*le-miqreta beorḥa*), “to take a bite on the way.” See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 22:4; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 5:8; *Tanḥuma, Ḥuqqat* 1.

    On *tirma*, see *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 178; *KP*. For other interpretations of *qirtoi de-orḥa*, see *OY;* Galante; *KP; NZ;* Liebes, *Peraqim*, 392.

[^786]: **So people may attain merit through me** “I am traveling without any provisions so that people may attain merit by providing me with food.”

[^787]: **two sons who were taken captive when an officer came to town** In medieval Europe, Jews were at times taken captive due to a blood libel, Host desecration libel, or simply for extortion. Ransoming such captives was considered a supreme religious duty.

[^788]: **benefit through me** That is, “attain merit by providing me with funds for my sons’ release.”

[^789]: **They benefited along with him and gave him something to eat** By helping him, they benefited as well.

[^790]: **Daily offering of the blessed Holy One—to nourish the world…** The Jew emphasizes the cosmic function of the daily sacrifices in the Temple. This mystical view—typical of the *Zohar*—contrasts with that of Maimonides, who relativized the importance of the sacrifices, trying to explain them away as a concession to the primitive nature of ancient Israel.

    See Rashi on BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 10#10b|10b]], s.v. *mezin;* Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 3:32; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:878–90.

[^791]: **through arousal below, arousal above…** A general principle of Kabbalah. Here the Jew’s point is that the daily sacrifices in the Temple stimulate the flow of emanation above, nourishing all worlds.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-156b|156b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-235a|235a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-40b|3:40b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]].

[^792]: ***I have eaten my honeycomb with my honey*…** Alluding to the midrashic reading of this verse, according to which God is describing His consumption of sacrifices and libations. See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah*, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-3b|3:3b]]–4a, [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-241a|241a–242a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-248a|248a]].

[^793]: ***For My fire-offerings*, as is written: *Eat, O friends!*…** The verse continues in parallelism: *Drink and be drunk, O lovers!* God shares the offerings with the angels. The word אשי (*ishshai*), *My fire-offerings*, is interpreted as אישי (*ishai*), “My angelic personages.” (Actually, the literal meaning of אשה \[*ishsheh*\] is probably “gift”; see Milgrom, *Leviticus*, 1:161–62.)

    See *Sifrei Zuta*, [[Torah 41 - Pinchas#^28-2|Numbers 28:2]]; *Midrash Zuta*, [[Song of Songs 1#^1-15|Song of Songs 1:15]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-70a|1:70a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-81a|81a]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z2-43a|2:43a]]; 3:110a (*RM*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-258b|258b]]. On this class of angels, see Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Yesodei ha-Torah* 2:7.

    Cf. *Sifra, Vayiqra, dibbura dindavah* 6:9, 7c: “אשה (*Ishsheh*), *Fire-offering:* for the sake of אישים (*ishshim*), fire-offerings \[i.e., not for the sake of charring the flesh\].” See M *Zevaḥim* 4:6; BT *Zevaḥim* [[Zevachim 46#46b|46b]].

[^794]: **then if one offers nourishment to sustain a life…** Sustaining a poor person (such as this wandering Jew) has an effect similar to, or even greater than, the Temple sacrifices. See *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 4; BT *Sukkah* [[Sukkah 49#49b|49b]].

[^795]: **one should never disparage anyone else in the world** See M *Avot* 4:3: “Ben Azzai said, ‘Do not despise any human being.’”

[^796]: **In two ways we have benefited from this person!** By helping him and by learning from him this secret of Torah.

[^797]: **He opened** The Jew opened.

[^798]: **mystery of Assembly of Israel…את (*et*)—precisely, *Et!*** *Shekhinah* is known as Assembly of Israel. See above, note 15.

    Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when et appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 22#22b|22b]]; *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 12#12a|12a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    In the *Zohar* the word את (*et*) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who comprises the entire alphabet of divine speech, from א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*). See the Christian parallel in [[Revelation 01#^1-8|Revelation 1:8]]: “I am *alpha* and *omega.”*

[^799]: ***Qorbani, My offering*—קרבנא (*qorbena*), bringing near…** The word קרבן (*qorbari*), *offering*, means literally: “bringing near.” Its kabbalistic purpose is to bring *Shekhinah* near, to unite Her with *Tif’eret*. See *Bahir* 77–78 (108–9).

[^800]: ***My food*—nourishment coming from above through arousal below** The sustaining flow of emanation issuing from *Binah*, stimulated by the sacrificial offering below.

[^801]: ***For My fire-offerings*—including other forces who must be nourished…** Angels. See above, note 793.

[^802]: **aspiration and connection…** The pure intention of those participating in the ritual (priests, Levites, and Israelites), which serves to unify all realms below and above. See *Targum Onqelos*, [[Torah 41 - Pinchas#^28-2|Numbers 28:2]].

[^803]: **When Abraham roused himself to perform His will…** To offer up Isaac as God commanded. These two patriarchs symbolize *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, associated with morning and evening, the *appointed* times for the two daily offerings. See [[Torah 41 - Pinchas#^28-4|Numbers 28:4]]. Cf. BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 26#26b|26b]].

[^804]: **And Rabbi Ḥiyya said** The Jew reports how Rabbi Ḥiyya responded to Rabbi El’azar.

[^805]: **the phrase… should read: *at their appointed times*!** Referring to the two *times* of the patriarchs and their corresponding daily offerings: morning and evening.

[^806]: **He replied** The Jew relates Rabbi El’azar’s reply to Rabbi Ḥiyya’s challenge.

[^807]: **fire merged with water, water with fire…** Abraham’s act of binding Isaac on the altar merged their corresponding *sefirot: Ḥesed* (symbolized by water) and *Gevurah* (symbolized by fire). Similarly, the daily offering harmonizes these opposite tendencies. This unified harmony is indicated by the singular construction: *at its appointed time*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-103b|1:103b]]–104a, [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118b|118b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|133b]], and especially 119b.

[^808]: **mystery of שמור (*Shamor*), *Observe*** *Shekhinah,* the feminine aspect, is signified by the opening word of the fourth of the Ten Commandments: שמור (*Shamor*), *Observe, the Sabbath day to keep it holy* ([[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^5-12|Deuteronomy 5:12]]). This word signifies the feminine because the other version of the Ten Commandments ([[Torah 17 - Yitro#^20-8|Exodus 20:8]]) reads: זכור (*Zakhor*), *Remember, the Sabbath day*…, and זכור (*zakhor*) suggests זכר (*zakhar*), “male.”

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 20#20b|20b]]; *Bahir* 124 (182); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 496–97; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 17 - Yitro#^20-8|Exodus 20:8]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47b|47b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-48b|48b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 118.

[^809]: **who must be drawn above… by right and left…** The purpose of the daily offering is for *Shekhinah* to be embraced and drawn above by *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, the two divine arms, symbolized by the two patriarchs and by their corresponding *appointed* times: morning and evening.

[^810]: **If I came here just to hear these words, I am satisfied!** Similar exclamations appear in rabbinic literature and often in the *Zohar*.
