See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 1:3; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 3:11; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 6:2; *Qohelet Zuta* 5:17; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 16#16a|16a]], 24b; *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 41#41a|41a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-2a|1:2a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-148b|148b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-240a|240a]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-99a|2:99a]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-121b|121b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-122a|122a]], [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-193b|193b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-26a|3:26a]]; *KP*, 1:20d.

[^811]: **Happy are Israel… *Your people are all righteous!*** Even wandering beggars. The verse, appearing frequently in the *Zohar*, reads in full: *Your people, all of them righteous, will inherit the land forever—sprout of My planting, work of My hands, that I may be glorified*. In M *Sanhedrin* 10:1, it is cited to demonstrate that “all of Israel have a share in the world that is coming.”

[^812]: **Out of disgrace… ‘place of תורף (*toref*), pudenda’** According to this view, the name תרפים (*terafim*) is a contemptuous term, based on תורף (*toref*), “decay, filth, obscenity.” The phrase “place of תורף (*toref*)” appears in BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 24#24a|24a]].

    See M *Avodah Zarah* 2:3; *Niddah* 8:1; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 12; *Arukh*, s.v. *trf*. Cf. BT *Megillah* [[Megillah 25#25b|25b]].

[^813]: **Laban was steeped in all sorceries of the world** On Laban’s sorcery, see *Targum Yerushalmi*, Rashi, Ibn Ezra, and *Sekhel Tov* on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-27|Genesis 30:27]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133b|1:133b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-139b|139b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158b|158b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-161a|161a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-166b|166b–167b]].

[^814]: **They were made by magic… By witchcraft** The two terms are קסם (*qesem*) and נחש (*naḥash*), linked in [[Torah 40 - Balak#^23-23|Numbers 23:23]], where they refer to “divination” and “augury.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-167a|1:167a]]; [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-112b|3:112b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-207a|207a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-211a|211a]], [[Zohar 3.299b|299b]].

    On the process of fashioning the *terafim*, see *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-19|Genesis 31:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* (ed. Friedlander), 36; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 12; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-34|Genesis 31:34]].

[^815]: **They were made only at precise times** At propitious moments, to attract astral powers. See Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-19|Genesis 31:19]]; Rashi on [[II Kings 23#^23-24|2 Kings 23:24]].

[^816]: **expert in moments and hours** An astrologer who knows when the time is propitious.

[^817]: **They speak constantly… Rachel feared they would offer advice…** According to a related interpretation, she feared they would inform Laban that Jacob had fled. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 36; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 12; Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-19|Genesis 31:19]]. On the oracular power of the *terafim*, see [[Ezekiel 21#^21-26|Ezekiel 21:26]]; [[Zechariah 10#^10-2|Zechariah 10:2]].

[^818]: **now what is written? *She sat on them*** The verse reads: *Rachel had taken the terafim and put them in the camel’s saddle and sat on them*. According to Rabbi Yehudah, her contemptuous act prevented them from speaking. See BT *Megillah* [[Megillah 25#25b|25b]].

[^819]: **many rituals were performed for them before they would speak** See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* (ed. Friedlander), 36; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 12.

[^820]: **unaware that Jacob had fled…** Since Rachel had stolen the *terafim*, which could have informed him (see above, note 817). Now he was told by his servant.

[^821]: ***An Aramean was about to destroy my father!*** The verse is usually understood to mean: *My father was a wandering* \[or: *fugitive, straying, perishing*\] *Aramean*. The creative reading here appears in the Passover Haggadah, and in Septuagint, *Targum Onqelos, Targum Yerushalmi*, ad loc.

[^822]: ***It is in the power of my hand*… The witchcraft in his hand** Perhaps Rabbi Yehudah is playing on אל (*el*), *the power of*, and אל (*el*), “god,” false god, referring to Laban’s witchcraft.

[^823]: **In a single day Laban covered the distance…** Jacob was already three days’ distance from Laban before he fled ([[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^30-36|Genesis 30:36]]), so it took a messenger three days to reach Laban and inform him, meaning that now they were six days’ distance apart. Laban presumably set out on the following day, and in that one day he covered a distance that would have taken Jacob seven days. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 74:6; Rashi on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-23|Genesis 31:23]].

[^824]: **Rachel, even though she acted to uproot her father…** According to this view, Rachel stole the *terafim* with pure intentions, seeking to eradicate her father’s idolatry. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 74:5: “Her purpose was solely for the sake of heaven. She said, ‘What, should we go and leave this old man in his corruption?’”

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 36; *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 12; Rashi on [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-19|Genesis 31:19]].

[^825]: **punished; for she never raised Benjamin…** Despite her pure motives, Rachel was severely punished for the suffering her father experienced as a result of the theft: she died giving birth to Benjamin ([[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^35-16|Genesis 35:16–20]]). The extreme punishment demonstrates the ultimate value of honoring one’s parents.

    See Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Mamrim* 6:11; *Shulḥan Arukh, Yoreh De’ah* 240:18; *Siftei Kohen*, ad loc.; Ḥayyim Joseph David Azulai, *Devash le-Fi*, 1:39.

    On Jacob’s punishment for terrifying his father Isaac, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 84:19; 91:11; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-144b|1:144b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-185b|185b]]. On Esau’s reward for honoring Isaac, see *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 336; *Devarim Rabbah* 1:15; *Pesiqta Rabbati* 23; *Targum Yerushalmi* and *Ba’al ha-Turim*, [[Torah 44 - Devarim#^2-5|Deuteronomy 2:5]]; above, page 318.

    For another linkage between Rachel’s act of theft and her death, see [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-32|Genesis 31:32]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 74:9; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-175a|1:175a]].

[^826]: **All this rebuke that Jacob delivered to Laban** See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^31-36|Genesis 31:36–42]].

[^827]: **That wicked one reverted to his stumbling…*god of Nahor*** Immediately after acknowledging the one God, he reverted to his belief in the idolatrous god of his grandfather Nahor.

[^828]: ***the Fear of his father Isaac*** This striking divine name appears only twice in the Bible, both times in this chapter (see verse 42). According to *Sekhel Tov*, [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^15-16|Exodus 15:16]], it expresses the terror felt by Isaac as he was bound upon the altar.

[^829]: **not *by the God of Abraham*** As Laban had sworn.

[^830]: **he didn’t want to trouble the Right for the sake of Laban** Jacob didn’t want to call upon the divine right arm, *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham, apparently because Laban was too wicked to deserve the abounding love of that *sefirah*. Rather, he invoked *Gevurah*, also known as *Paḥad* (“Fear”), symbolized by his father, Isaac. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-60a|3:60a]].

[^831]: **one should not swear, even in truth, by the highest realm of all** *Ḥesed* is the highest of the seven *sefirot* issuing from *Binah*, while *Gevurah* is next. Further, *God of Abraham* is a more explicit divine name than *Fear of…Isaac*.

[^832]: **leaving out my father** Isaac, who symbolizes *Gevurah*.

[^833]: **I will complete all!…** By invoking *the Fear of Isaac* (*Gevurah*), Jacob balanced *the God of Abraham* (*Ḥesed*), thereby expressing the harmony of his own *sefirah, Tif’eret*.

[^834]: **to be encompassed by Judgment, confronting Laban** Through swearing by *the Fear of Isaac*, Jacob invoked the power of *Gevurah*, or *Din* (“Judgment”), to secure protection against Laban.

[^835]: ***He blessed them and named them* אדם (*Adam*), *Human*…** The verse continues: *on the day they were created*.

[^836]: ***Come, eat of my bread!… Whoever is simple, turn in here!*…** Rabbi Abba inverts the order of these two verses.

[^837]: **within and without** Esoteric and exoteric.

[^838]: **Implying that sun and moon share a single bond…** Esoterically, the phrase *male and female* implies the divine couple, *Ti’feret* and *Shekhinah*, who are symbolized by sun and moon. For Rabbi Abba, the single word בראם (*bera’am*), *He created them*, implies the union of the two, since Scripture could have employed two words: אותם‎ ברא (*bara otam*), emphasizing two separate creations. Similarly with Habakkuk, who deletes the conjunction ו (*ve*), “and,” and employs the singular forms עמד (*amad*), *stood*, and *in her lofty abode*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-55b|3:55b]].

[^839]: **Implying that Adam and Eve were created as one…** Esoterically, the phrase *male and female* implies that Adam and Eve were originally a single, androgynous creature. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 8:1: “Rabbi Yirmeyah son of El’azar said, ‘When the blessed Holy One created Adam, He created him androgynous, as is said: *Male and female He created them* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-27|Genesis 1:27]]).’ Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥman said, ‘When the blessed Holy One created Adam, He created him with two faces. Then He sawed him and gave him two backs, one on this side and one on that.’”

    See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 61#61a|61a]]; *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 18#18a|18a]]; Plato, *Symposium* 189d–191d; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-2b|1:2b]], [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13b|13b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47a|47a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-55a|2:55a]].

[^840]: **blessing abides only where male and female are found** See BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 62#62b|62b]]: “Rabbi Tanḥum said in the name of Rabbi Ḥanilai, ‘Any man without a wife is without joy, without blessing, without goodness.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|1:55b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182a|182a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-74b|3:74b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-296a|296a]] (*IZ*).

[^841]: **ויפגע (*Va-yifga*), *He entreated, the place*** The verse is usually understood to mean: *He encountered a certain place*. However the root פגע (*pg’*) means not only “to encounter” but also “to entreat,” a sense applied to the verse already in the Midrash. See *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ* 2; *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:9; BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 26#26b|26b]]; *Zohar* [[#^z1-148b|1:148b]].

    In rabbinic literature, המקום (*ha-maqom*), “the place,” is a name of God, emphasizing divine immanence and omnipresence. Here it designates *Shekhinah*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:9; Urbach, *The Sages*, 1:66–79.

[^842]: **answered only in a dream** Jacob’s unmarried status left him incomplete and unfit for direct encounter with the divine. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-12|Genesis 28:12–17]]; above, page 331.

    Cf. BT *Yevamot* [[Yevamot 63#63a|63a]]: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Any man without a wife is not a man.’” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 17:2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|1:55b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-5a|3:5a]], [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-7b|7b]], [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-34a|34a]].

[^843]: **Now that he was married…supernal…angels entreated him…** Now that Jacob was married, he was complete. He didn’t need to entreat the divine; angels *entreated him*. See above, pages 331–38.

    This verse is usually understood to mean *They encountered him*. See above, note 841.

[^844]: **Because by virtue of Jacob and those tribes, they are watered…** Now that Jacob is married and father of so many tribes, he attains the rung of *Tif’eret*, poised to unite with *Shekhinah* and convey to Her the flow of emanation, while the tribes below adorn Her. The angels depend for their sustenance upon the flow proceeding from the vast sea of *Shekhinah*, so they come to entreat Jacob.

[^845]: **eyes wide open…as is written: *When he saw them*…** The expression *he saw them* implies that Jacob was open-eyed.

[^846]: **the same ones he had seen in the dream** Ascending and descending the ladder to heaven. See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^28-12|Genesis 28:12]].

[^847]: **So he called them מחנים (*Maḥanayim*), *Double Camps*…** See [[Torah 07 - Vayetzei#^32-3|Genesis 32:3]]: *When he saw them, Jacob said, “This is a camp of God!” So he named that place* מחנים (*Maḥanayim*), *Double Camps*. According to Rabbi Abba, the name refers to the camps of angels he saw above in his dream and the camps he sees now with open eyes on earth.

[^848]: **Because *Shekhinah* approached him to possess the house…** Once Benjamin is born, completing the full complement of twelve tribes, *Shekhinah* arrives, escorted by angels, to replace Rachel as Jacob’s spouse and “essence of the house.” She and Jacob will then reside in peace fittingly in the Holy Land. See above, pages 379–83, 397–98.
